# 1 Chronicles

# ANNOTATIONS ON THE FIRST BOOK OF CHRONICLES

## THE GENERAL ARGUMENT OF THE TWO BOOKS OF CHRONICLES

The two books of Chronicles, of old compiled in one, contain an history of three thousand four hundred fifty and seven years at least, even from Adam, 1 Chr. 1:1, to the return of the Israelites from the Babylonish captivity, 2 Chr. 36:23. Yea, the genealogy of David’s posterity is extended far further, 1 Chr. 2:19, etc. The penman of these books penned also that of Ezra, as appears by the two last verses of the second book of Chronicles, and the three first of Ezra, which are almost the same word for word. By which also it appears that they were penned after the captivity. And it is probable that the genealogies here set down, and a great part of the histories were taken out of records kept by some of the priests or Levites all the time of the captivity.

## THE SPECIAL ARGUMENT OF THE FIRST BOOK OF CHRONICLES

An history of two thousand nine hundred fourscore and seven years is registered in the first book: of which two thousand nine hundred forty and seven are passed over in reciting genealogies, a few brief historical passages being here and there inserted, and the death of Saul added thereto, ch. 10. The genealogies are an evidence of God’s providence in preserving his church generation after generation, and memorials thereof being for the most part of such as appertained to the church. Forty years more are added about the acts of David in the time of his reign, with whose death the first book is concluded, ch. 29:27, etc.

## THE TITLE

*{The first Book}* This distinction is expressed by reason of that division of the Chronicles which the church has used for many generations. See the general argument.

*{Of the Chronicles}* A chronicle is a register of acts done time after time. The Hebrew title, *Words of days*, or, *Deeds of days*, imports the same that the title Chronicles does. The Greek title, *Παραλειπόμενα*, *Remainders*, has relation to the books of the Kings, as if the Chronicles had gathered up what the books of the Kings had left out. True it is, that in the books of the Kings there are frequent references to the book of the Chronicles; but these books of sacred Chronicles cannot be there meant, because not then penned. See the general argument. Besides, many things there referred to the books of the Chronicles are not found in these books.

## CHAPTER 1

**Verse 1.** *{Adam}* This was the proper name of the first man, given to him by reason of the matter out of which his body was framed, the earth, Gen. 2:7. It sometimes comprises under it both man and woman, Gen. 1:27; 2:23; 3:22, 24, yea, and all mankind, Gen. 9:5, 6; Ps. 82:7.

*{Sheth}* In this first genealogy the names only of men are set down for brevity’s sake, but in such order as the son succeeds his father. Some, to make up the full sense, would add this phrase, *is born*, as it had been said, *Of Adam Sheth is born*. Some thus, *Adam begat Sheth*. Others thus, *Adam, Sheth his son*, and so in the rest. Sheth was not Adam’s first son, but Cain, Gen. 4:21. But Cain and his posterity were an impious generation, and belonged not to the church. Abel was Adam’s next son: but he, before he had any children, was slain by Cain, Gen. 4:8. After Abel’s death Sheth was born, and called Sheth, because put or appointed instead of Abel, Gen. 4:25.

*{Enosh}* The eldest son of Sheth called Enosh in memorial of the miserable and mortal condition whereunto man was brought. For Enosh signifies *miserable or mortal*. The posterity of Cain having filled a great part of the world, with an impious and profane brood, Enosh gathered such as came from him into a church, and brought them to worship God publicly, he himself being as a priest unto them in the room of Abel. In this respect it is said of Enosh his time, *Then began men to call upon the name of the Lord*, Gen. 4:26. These three first names, Adam, Sheth, Enosh, according to their signification import thus much, *Man is made mortal*.

**V. 2.** *{Kenan, Mahalaleel, Jered}* Of these three there is nothing registered, but their names, their long life, and that they begat sons and daughters. See Gen. 5:12, etc.

**V. 3.** *{Henoch}* This man is expressly said to be *the seventh from Adam*; and this genealogy declares it so to be. He prophesied in his time of Christ’s coming unto judgment, Jude 14, 15. There is an excellent commendation given of him, that *he walked with God*, Gen. 5:24. The Hebrew word there used implies a continual walking with God. To walk with God is all one as to *walk before God*, whereof see 1 Kings 2:4. In that respect Henoch is said to *have pleased God*, Heb. 11:5. And as an evidence of God’s gracious acceptance of him it is said that *he was not: for God took him*, Gen. 5:24. He was not on earth after the three hundred and sixty and five years of his life: for *God had translated him that he should not see death*. That translation was both from one place to another, namely, from earth to heaven; and also from one condition to another, namely, from mortality to immortality. Thus was he translated as Elijah was afterward, 2 Kings 2:11, and as the saints which shall be living on earth at Christ’s last coming, 1 Cor. 15:51, 52, 53, and in this respect he never saw death, Heb. 11:5. Though he were one of the best patriarchs before the flood, yet he lived the shortest time of them all: so as the best men may be soonest taken out of this world. Enoch’s continual walking with the Lord well answers the notation of his name, which is, *dedicated*. By his parentage and pious carriage he is distinguished from Cain’s son, who gave his own name to a city that he built, Gen. 4:17, and from Abraham’s grandchild by Keturah, Gen. 25:4, and from Reuben’s son, Gen. 46:9. Though there be in our English translation a little difference in these names, yet in Hebrew they are the same.

*{Methuselah}* This man lived the longest of all the patriarchs before the flood, Gen. 5:27, yea, we may well say, the longest of all that ever were on earth. Some imagine that Adam may be accounted elder than he, because he was of man-age when first created. But Methushelah lived thirty-nine years longer than Adam; and it is to be supposed Adam was created in the stature of a young man. Now grant him to be of such a seeming age when he ate of the forbidden tree as Christ was when he died upon the tree, and add so many years (which were betwixt XXXIII and XXXIV), yet they will not make up the full years of Methushelah. Methushelah died about the time of the coming of the flood.

*{Lamech}* This Lamech the son of Methushelah is distinguished from that impious and cruel Lamech which was of Cain’s posterity, Gen. 4:18, 19. He had the gift of prophecy as appears by the name Noah which he gave to his son, and the reason rendered thereof, Gen. 5:29. He died about five years before the flood.

**V. 4.** *{Noah}* This was the tenth and last great patriarch before the flood. For his son Shem is reckoned among the patriarchs after the flood. His name signifies rest; and he brought comfort, rest and refreshing to the world. He was a lively type of Christ, who is the comfort, rest and peace of man. In the ark which he built was the seminary of the world and the church preserved; a matter of great comfort and rest. In the sacrifice he offered up when he came out of the ark God smelled *a savour of rest*, Gen. 8:2. To him God renewed a covenant of peace and rest, no more to drown the world, Gen. 9:9. The history of this man is set down Gen. 6, and 7, and 8, and 9. From the beginning of the world to the flood, which fell out in Noah’s days, were a thousand six hundred fifty and six years.

*{Shem}* Though this man be here and in sundry other places set before Japheth, yet is Japheth said to be the elder, Gen. 10:21. And thus it appears to be so: Noah is said to beget Shem, Ham, and Japheth when he was five hundred years old, Gen. 5:32. And again it is said, *Shem was an hundred years old two years after the flood*, Gen. 11:10. Now the flood was in the sixth hundredth year of Noah, and continued about one year. Compare Gen. 7:11 with Gen. 8:13, 14. If then Shem had been born in the five hundredth year of Noah, he would have been a hundred years old about the time of Noah’s entering into the ark. But it being two years after the flood before he was a hundred years old, he must by this computation be born when Noah was five hundred and three years old. Whereas it is said that *Noah was five hundred years old, and begat Shem, Ham and Japheth*, we are not to think that he begot all these three sons in that year, but, according to the meaning of the Hebrew phrase, he then began to beget them. In that year he begot Japheth, two or three years after he begot Shem, and after these he begot Ham; for *Ham was his younger son*, Gen. 9:24. Shem therefore is put in the first place for dignity’s sake, as, Isaac and Ishmael, Gen. 25:9; Jacob and Esau, Gen. 28:5; Ephraim and Manasseh, Gen. 48:20. Shem was the father and head of all those patriarchs that were born after the flood, and were in their order progenitors of Christ. He lived about thirty years after Abraham’s decease. And by reason of the many years he had lived before Abraham’s time, his father, his mother, his descent, and beginning of his days were not known to Abraham and those that then lived. In this respect he is reputed both by many of the Jewish rabbins and also by ancient and later divines of the Christian church to be that Melchisedec who met Abraham, Gen. 14:18; Heb. 7:1, etc.

*{Ham}* This was the youngest of Noah’s three sons; one of those that were in the ark, but after he came out of the ark, he was cursed for scoffing at his father’s infirmity, Gen. 9:22, 24, 25. The land wherein his posterity was to dwell was adjudged to the posterity of Shem, Gen. 12:7, which may be one reason why he is set next to Shem, before his brother Japheth.

*{and Japheth}* He was the father of such Gentiles as should in process of time be converted to the Christian faith: in which respect his father thus prophesies of him, *He shall dwell in the tents of Shem*, Gen. 9:27. He is here set in the last place, because his posterity was to be reckoned up in the next verses. These three, Shem, Ham and Japheth, though they did not succeed one another in a right line, are set down together, because by them the nations were divided in the earth after the flood, Gen. 10:32. The sons of Shem possessed the eastern parts, the sons of Cham the southern, the sons of Japheth the northern.

**V. 5.** *{The sons of Japheth}* Gen. 10:2. He begins with the last that was named, and ends with the first, that he might the more fitly continue the pedigree of Shem, from whom the Jews, who were the peculiar people of God, descended.

*{Gomer, etc.}* This and other names following were given not only to men, but also to the countries where those men lived. Of Gomer, Magog, Madai, Javan, Tubal, Meshech, and Tiras, see on Gen. 10:2.

**V. 6.** *{And the sons of Gomer, Ashchenaz, etc.}* These three sons of Gomer were also heads of distinct nations. See on Gen. 10:3.

*{and Riphath}* Or, *Diphath*, as it is in some copies. If we diligently compare divers parts of Scripture, we shall find difference in sundry names. Sometimes like letters are put one for another, as here, *ר* *R* for *ד* *D*. Compare Gen. 21:3 with Amos 7:9. Sometimes a letter or syllable is left out, as Jer. 22:28. See 2 Kings 24:6. Sometimes letters or syllables are transposed, as *Salathiel*, Matt. 1:12, for *Shealtiel*, Ezra 3:2. Sometimes a letter or syllable is added, as v. 17, *Meshec* for *Mash*, Gen. 10:23. Sometimes two or three names be given to one and the same person, as 2 Kings 23:30. Sometimes the same name is given to the male and female sex, as v. 36. Sometimes a name may be taken either as proper or appellative. See 2 Kings 25:19. Sometimes the same name is put for a man and for a country. See v. 5.

*{and Togarmah}* Ezek. 27:14; 38:6.

**V. 7.** *{And the sons of Javan}* This was the fourth son of Japheth, v. 5. His sons were four which follow in order.

*{Elishah, and Tarshish, Kittim}* See on Gen. 10:4. Some suppose the Macedonians descended from Kittim.

*{and Dodanim}* Or, *Rodanim*, according to some copies. There is a city in Europe called Dodona, the inhabitants whereof are supposed to come from Dodanim. These names Kittim and Dodanim, being of the plural number, comprise both the particular persons here said to be the sons of Javan, and also their posterity. The like may be said of other names of the plural number in these genealogies; yea, and of such names as consist of collective words, as Tarshish.

**V. 8.** *{The sons of Ham}* Of Ham see v. 4.

*{Cush, and Mizraim, Put and Canaan}* Of these see Gen. 10:6.

**V. 9.** *{And the sons of Cush, Seba}* See Gen. 10:7.

*{and Havilah}* There were questionless as two men at least, so also two countries of this name: for one of the posterity of Shem was called Havilah, Gen. 10:29. The country where the posterity of Ishmael dwelt is called Havilah, Gen. 25:10, as also that where the Amalekites dwelt, 1 Sam. 15:7. See Gen. 2:11.

*{and Sabta}* The people called Sabateans are supposed to descend from this Sabta.

*{and Raamah, and Sabtecha}* Sundry people of Arabia are thought to come from these.

*{and the sons of Raamah, Sheba, and Dedan}* These also were two countries in Arabia. And two sons of Keturah were of these names, Gen. 25:3. These people and places long continued. Mention is made of them about the time of the captivity, Ezek. 38:13.

**V. 10.** *{And Cush begat Nimrod}* Gen. 10:8.

*{he began to be mighty upon the earth}* See on Gen. 10:8.

**V. 11.** *{And Mizraim}* Of such words in the plural number see v. 7. See on Gen. 10:6.

*{begat Ludim}* See Gen. 10:13.

*{and Anamim, and Lehabim, and Naphtuhim}* These names are nowhere read but in Gen. 10:13 and in this place.

**V. 12.** *{And Pathrusim, and Casluhim, (of whom came the Philistines)}* See on Gen. 10:14.

*{and Caphthorim}* They who are comprised under this name are said to come forth out of Caphtor, Deut. 2:23. These were also of the Philistims.

**V. 13.** *{And Canaan begat Zidon his firstborn, and Heth}* See on Gen. 10:15.

**V. 14.** *{The Jebusite also}* These were those Canaanites which dwelt in that part of Canaan where Jerusalem was afterwards built, Josh. 18:28.

*{and the Amorite}* This people dwelt in the land beyond Jordan, which was given to Reuben, Gad, and half the tribe of Manasseh, Deut. 2:32, 33.

*{and the Girgashite}* These inhabited that place which in the New Testament is called the country of the Gergesenes, Matt. 8:28.

**V. 15.** *{And the Hivite}* These inhabited that land where afterwards the Gibeonites were, Josh. 9:17.

*{and the Arkite}* The people here intended were in Phoenicia, not far from Libanus. The chief city in the nation is called Arca.

*{and the Sinite}* These people are taken to be in Idumea, not far from the Jebusites.

**V. 16.** *{And the Arvadite}* The posterity of these are said to be mariners, and called *men of Arvad*, Ezek. 27:8, 11.

*{and the Zemarite}* These dwelt in that part of Canaan which fell to the lot of Benjamin, and are called Zemaraim, Josh. 18:22. In their region was that mount whereon Abijah stood when he began the battle against Israel, called mount Zemaraim, 2 Chr. 13:4.

*{and the Hamathite}* The region where these inhabited was called Chamatha, and extended itself from Libanus to Arabia.

**V. 17.** *{The sons of Shem}* Gen. 10:23. The two former genealogies of Japheth and Cham are inserted, because by them a great part of the world is replenished with people. Now he returns to the church, in which respect this verse depends upon the fourth. Of Shem see v. 4.

*{Elam}* From him came the Elamites, Ezr. 4:9. Acts 2:9, Jer. 25:22, Dan. 3:2. within the confines of Persia.

*{and Asshur}* This man was the head of the Assyrians, from whom they received their name. See on Gen. 10:11.

*{and Arphaxad}* Though he had two elder brothers, Elam and Ashur, yet the blessing of being a head of the church fell upon him, as afterwards upon Abraham, Gen. 12:1, 2, and Isaac, Gen. 21:12, and Jacob, Gen. 27:29.

*{and Lud}* Of him came those who are called Lydians, Jer. 46:9.

*{and Aram, and Uz}* See Gen. 10:22.

*{and Hul}* His progeny inhabited certain deserts reaching to Euphrates. There is a certain famous city called Cho.

*{and Gether}* His posterity inhabited a part of Syria where was a city called Gindarus.

*{and Meshech}* Or, *Mash*, Gen. 10:23. His posterity inhabited another part of Syria, where was a place called Masius, betwixt Cilicia and Mesopotamia.

**V. 18.** *{And Arphaxad begat Shelah}* This Shelah was one of the direct line from whom Abraham, and after him Christ, came. The Septuagint do here put in Cainan, thus, *Arphaxad begat Cainan, and Cainan begat Shelah*. But though this genealogy be twice set down by Moses, namely, Gen. 10:24 and 11:22, yet is it in neither place expressed. Indeed in the genealogy of Christ set down by Luke, Cainan is also inserted, Luke 3:36. For the Greek translation of the Septuagint being in use among the Gentiles then converted, the evangelist would not alter it, it being but a mere addition of a name.

*{and Shelah begat Eber}* From him came the name of Hebrews. See Gen. 14:13. This Eber lived the longest of any registered to be born after the flood, Gen. 11:16, 17. He lived many years in Abraham’s days. He retained the original Hebrew language, and likewise the true worship of God; and in this respect might Abraham retain his name, rather than any others after him.

**V. 19.** *{And unto Eber were born two sons}* These two sons had a different posterity: from one of them descended the holy seed; from the other impious generations.

*{the name of the one was Peleg}* i.e. division.

*{because in his days the earth was divided}* See on Gen. 10:25.

*{and his brother’s name was Joktan}* See on Gen. 10:25.

**V. 20.** *{And Joktan begat Almodad, etc.}* There are thirteen sons of this Joktan set down in order, of whom no mention is made but here and Gen. 10:26, etc. so that it is supposed that their posterity was not very great, and that many of them had no children, and that they which had are all comprehended under the family of their father, as if he had had but one son, Of as many of them as in their generations remained on earth it is said Gen. 10:30. that *their dwelling was from Mesha*, (whereof see v. 17.) *as thou goest unto Sephar*, which is a city in Mesopotamia near Euphrates.

**V. 23.** *{And Ophir}* See 1 King. 9:28.

*{and Havilah}* See v. 9.

**V. 24.** *{Shem, etc.}* Luk. 3:36. There are, with Abraham, ten patriarchs here set down that in a direct line descended one from another, and in their order and time were heads of the church, and progenitors of Jesus Christ. Their names only are set down, as the ten patriarchs before the flood were. Of the manner of setting them down see v. 1. In relation to the church this genealogy may be added to v. 4. thus, *Noah, Shem, Arphaxad*, etc., Of Shem see v. 4.

*{Arphaxad}* See v. 17.

*{Shelah}* See v. 18.

**V. 25.** *{Eber}* See v. 18.

*{Peleg}* See v. 19.

*{Reu}* Of Rehu and Serug v. 26, there is no other mention made but here and Gen. 11:18, 20; Luke 3:35, in all which places the progenitors of Christ are set down. And this was honor enough, to be of his progenitors, and in sacred Scripture so to be recorded.

**V. 26.** *{Serug}* See on v. 25.

*{Nahor}* See Gen. 11:25. He was Isaac’s grandfather, Gen. 24:47.

*{Terah}* See on Gen. 11:27, 31, 32.

**V. 27.** *{Abram, the same is Abraham}* See on Gen. 17:5. This Abraham of all that were born after the flood was most famous; being the head and father of the church from his own days to Christ’s coming in the flesh, and also of all that believe to the end of the world, by reason of the promises made to him, and extended to his seed, not only after the flesh, but also after the faith, Rom. 4:12, Gal. 3:7, 29. His history is very largely set down from Gen. 12:1 to Gen. 25:11.

**V. 28.** *{The sons of Abraham, Isaac}* See v. 34. Isaac was younger than his brother after named: but he was the son of Abraham by his first wife, the other by a bond-woman; and he was in his order an head of the church, and a progenitor of Christ; and therefore for dignity’s sake he is first mentioned. See v. 4. Josh. 24:4.

*{and Ishmael}* Gen. 16:11. Because he was the son of Abraham, he was blessed with a numerous and mighty posterity, and is put into the public and sacred record, Gen. 17:20.

**V. 29.** *{These are their generations}* This relative their has reference to Isaac and Ishmael mentioned v. 28. Ishmael’s generation is first set down, because but few are to be named of them; but Isaac’s generation is set down to many multitudes.

*{The firstborn of Ishmael, Nebaioth}* Gen. 25:13. From him descended the people called Nabatheans in Arabia Petrea; and Napateans in Arabia the Happy.

*{then Kedar}* From him came the people called Kedarens, bordering near the Nabatheans.

*{and Adbeel}* The Adabeni or Agabeni came from this man.

*{and Mibsam}* The people called Mnasemanenses, at the hill Thamathum in Arabia, descended from Mibsam.

**V. 30.** *{Mishma}* People called Raabeni near the border of Persia sprung from Mishma.

*{and Dumah}* From him descended the people called Dumei, who possessed the city Dumetha.

*{Massa}* His posterity were called Masani, near to the Raabeni before mentioned.

*{Hadad}* Or, *Hadar*. Gen. 25:14. The Athrite near to the Napateans before mentioned, came from Hadad.

*{and Tema}* From him came the people called Themaneans, who possessed a city called Them, by the hills of Arabia the Happy.

**V. 31.** *{Jetur}* From him came the Itureans, inhabiting the borders of the desert of Arabia.

*{Naphish}* From him descended the Nabeans, whose borders reached to Mount Libanus. They inhabited a city called Niceta.

*{and Kedemah}* From him descended the people called Cadmoneans, bordering upon the Babylonians.

*{These are the sons of Ishmael}* Twelve in all. Where these are first reckoned up, it is said that *these are their names by their towns and by their castles*, Gen. 25:16, so as these towns or cities and forts had their names from their first builders. It is further added that they were *twelve princes according to their nations*: whereby is expressly verified the promise made to Abraham concerning his son Ishmael’s posterity, Gen. 17:20.

**V. 32.** *{Now the sons of Keturah Abraham’s concubine}* After the death of Sarah Abraham took Keturah to be his wife. Of the difference betwixt wives and concubines see 1 King. 11:3; Gen. 25:1.

*{she bare}* It is probable that she was of such an age as according to the course of nature she might bring forth many children; but Abraham many years before this was so old as Sarah thought it impossible he should beget children, Gen. 18:12. And the apostle says of Abraham’s body at that time, that it was dead, Rom. 4:19. But God bestowed upon him an especial blessing beyond the course of nature, whereby he did not only beget Isaac, Gen. 21:2, but also six other sons of Keturah here mentioned, Gen. 25:1, 2.

*{Zimran, etc.}* These six sons of Keturah were all inhabitants of Arabia, bordering on Syria. The posterity of Zimran inhabited the region called Zaaram or Zamram.

*{and Jokshan}* His posterity were called Catanians, bordering on Syria, who inhabited the city called Casaman.

*{and Medan}* His posterity inhabited Madiania or Madania, in the borders of Arabia the Happy.

*{and Midian}* From him descended the Midianites in the borders of Arabia Petraea.

*{and Ishbak}* A place called Scabiota near Laodicea in Syria was inhabited by the posterity of Ishbak.

*{and Shuah}* From him came people called Sacceans, which inhabited the eastern parts of Syria.

*{And the sons of Jokshan}* This was the second son of Keturah. It may be Zimran and Medan and Ishbak had no posterity; for they are not here set down.

*{Sheba and Dedan}* See v. 9. In Gen. 25:3. is added, *And the sons of Dedan were Ashurim, and Letushim, and Leummim*. Of names of the plural number see v. 7.

**V. 33.** *{And the sons of Midian}* This was Keturah’s fourth son.

*{Ephah}* We read of a region of this name, Isa. 60:6.

*{and Epher, and Henoch, and Abida, and Eldaah}* The five sons of Midian were the fathers or founders of five kingdoms; for we read of five kings of Midian, Numb. 31:8.

*{All these were the sons of Keturah}* By sons he means posterity, sons, and sons’ sons.

**V. 34.** *{And Abraham begat Isaac}* Of his first wife, after she had been many years barren and grown to old age, Gen. 18:11; 21:1, 2; Rom. 4:19.

*{The sons of Isaac, Esau, and Israel}* These two were twins, but Esau came first out of his mother’s womb, and so was by nature the elder, Gen. 25:24, 25. Of this name Israel see Gen. 32:28.

**V. 35.** *{The sons of Esau}* Gen. 36:9, 10. Though Esau were the elder, yet God loved Jacob, Mal. 1:2, and Jacob obtained both the birth-right, Gen. 25:33, and the blessing, Gen. 27:29.

*{Eliphaz}* This was the son of Esau by Adah his first wife, Gen. 36:10.

*{Reuel}* This was Esau’s son by Bashemath, another wife, Gen. 36:10.

*{and Jeush}* This was Esau’s son by Aholibamah, a third wife, Gen. 36:14.

*{and Jaalam and Korah}* These were brothers to Jeush by father and mother, Gen. 36:14.

**V. 36.** *{The sons of Eliphaz, Teman, and Omar, Zephi}* Or, *Zephon*. Gen. 36:11, these among others are said to be dukes; i.e., heads of great families. Dukes had a sole and supreme authority, as kings in their own dominions: as the dukes of Venice and of Savoy are supreme in their places: but in royal dignity and sundry prerogatives they were inferior to kings.

*{and Gatam, Kenaz}* Kenaz is put before Gatham, Gen. 36:15, 16.

*{and Timna}* For Timna Korah is named. It may be these were two names of one and the same person.

*{and Amalek}* Amalek was the son of Eliphaz by his concubine Timnah, Gen. 36:12. Timna was the name of a woman and of a man: for Amalek’s mother was of this name; so was one of Eliphaz his sons, here set down immediately before Amalek. There was also another man of this name, v. 51. This Amalek was the father of those Amalekites who were mortal enemies of the Israelites, and devoted by God himself to destruction, Exod. 17:14; Deut. 25:19; 1 Sam. 13:2, 3. See on Ezek. 3:1.

**V. 37.** *{The sons of Reuel}* This was Esau’s second son.

*{Nahath, Zerah, Shammah, and Mizzah}* The three former of these names are also given to sundry of the children of Israel at several times: the fourth we read not of but here, and Gen. 36:13. So these four, as coming from the stock of Esau, being not elsewhere mentioned cannot be set forth by their posterity.

**V. 38.** *{And the sons of Seir}* Seir possessed the land wherein Esau and his posterity afterwards dwelt, Deut. 2:12, 22, by reason whereof both the land and a famous mountain therein is called Seir, Gen. 32:3; 36:8, 9. Esau and Eliphaz made affinity with the posterity of Seir, and therefore the genealogy of Seir is here mixed with that of Esau. The affinity was this: Esau married Aholibamah daughter of Seir’s grandchild, Gen. 36:2, 20, 24, 25, and Eliphaz Esau’s son had Timna for his concubine, who was sister to Lotan Seir’s son, Gen. 35:12, 20, 22.

*{Lotan, and Shobal, and Zibeon}* The genealogy of these is set down in the verses following. Zibeon had also a son of his name, v. 40.

*{and Anah, and Dishon}* Anah had a son called Dishon, v. 41.

*{and Ezer, and Dishan}* There is but little difference betwixt the names of the fifth and seventh sons of Seir, Dishon and Dishan. Sometimes the very same name is given to two brothers.

**V. 39.** *{And the sons of Lotan, Hori, and Homam}* Or, *Heman*, Gen. 36:22.

*{and Timna was Lotan’s sister}* She was also Eliphaz his concubine, Gen. 36:12.

**V. 40.** *{The sons of Shobal, Alian}* Or, *Alvan*, Gen. 36:23.

*{and Manahath, and Ebal, Shephi}* Or, *Shepho*, Gen. 36:23.

*{and Onam}* There was one of the posterity of Judah who also had this name, ch. 2:26.

*{And the sons of Zibeon, Aiah, and Anah}* See Gen. 36:24.

**V. 41.** *{The sons of Anah, Dishon}* See v. 38.

*{And the sons of Dishon, Amram}* Or, *Hemdan*, Gen. 36:26.

*{and Eshban, and Ithran, and Cheran}* Nothing elsewhere is spoken of these men.

**V. 42.** *{The sons of Ezer, Bilhan, and Zavan, and Jakan}* Or, *Achan*, Gen. 36:27.

*{The sons of Dishan, Uz, and Aran}* The land where Job dwelt was called Uz, Job 1:1.

**V. 43.** *{Now these are the kings}* Gen. 36:40. Kings were such as had a sole and supreme authority. The relative particle these has reference, not to the sons of Seir mentioned in the former verses, but to those that follow.

*{that reigned in the land of Edom}* Namely, after the posterity of Esau were possessors thereof.

*{before any king reigned over the children of Israel}* The children of Israel were in Canaan and Egypt about four hundred years, Exod. 12:40, and it was four hundred after that before they had a king, 1 Kings 6:1. Now Moses sets down such things of Edom as had been before his time, which was above four hundred years before there was a king in Israel. The Edomites were the sooner raised up to the height of their dignity, because God intended to pull them down again from the same. See 2 Sam. 8:19.

*{Bela, the son of Beor}* The city Zoar, which was saved for Lot’s sake, Gen. 19:21, 22, was called Bela, Gen. 14:2.

*{and the name of his city was Dinhabah}* This was the place which Bela chose to set his royal palace in.

**V. 44.** *{And when Bela was dead, Jobab, etc.}* See Gen. 36:33.

**V. 45.** *{And when Jobab was dead, Husham of the land of the Temanites, etc.}* See Gen. 36:34; Jer. 49:7, 20; Amos 1:12.

**V. 46.** *{And when Husham was dead, Hadad, etc.}* This name Hadad was afterwards given to those who were of the king’s seed in Edom, 1 Kings 11:14.

*{which smote Midian}* The Midianites bordered upon the Edomites, which was an occasion of quarrels betwixt them.

*{in the field of Moab}* The Midianites and Moabites bordered one upon another, and were confederates, Num. 22:4.

*{reigned in his stead: and the name of his city was Avith}* The several cities where the several kings of Edom had their distinct courts imply that they did not succeed one another by a natural descent, but rather by election, or by force.

**V. 47.** *{And when Hadad was dead, Samlah of Masrekah, etc.}* This might be the name of the place where Shamlah was born, or where he kept his court; as the names of the cities before mentioned.

**V. 48.** *{And when Samlah was dead, Shaul of Rehoboth}* See Gen. 36:37.

*{by the river, etc.}* Some take this river to be Tigris, some Euphrates, both which come from the same head or spring, Gen. 2:10. The river called Hiddekel, Gen. 2:14, some take to be Tigris, and Euphrates is there joined to it.

**V. 49.** *{And when Shaul was dead, Baal-Hanan the son of Achbor, etc.}* The place where this king kept his court is not named.

**V. 50.** *{And when Baal-Hanan was dead, Hadad, etc.}* Or, *Hadar*, Gen. 36:39. The fourth king of Edom was also of this name, v. 46.

*{and the name of his city was Pai}* Or, *Pau*, Gen. 36:39.

*{and his wife’s name was Mehetabel the daughter of Matred, the daughter of Mezahab}* Matred was the name of a man: therefore the word daughter in the second place is put for grandchild. And grandchildren are oft styled sons or daughters, Exod. 2:20.

**V. 51.** *{Hadad died also}* This was the last of the eight kings of Edom here mentioned. It may be they ruled so tyrannically as the people would no longer endure that kind of monarchical regiment.

*{And the dukes of Edom were}* Of dukes see v. 36.

*{Duke Timnah}* This was also the name of a woman, Gen. 36:12.

*{Duke Aliah}* Or, *Alvah*, Gen. 36:40.

*{Duke Jetheth}* Gen. 36:40.

**V. 52.** *{Duke Aholibamah}* This also was a woman’s name, Gen. 36:2.

*{Duke Elah, etc.}* There were eleven of them in all.

*{These are the dukes of Edom}* Which succeeded the kings before mentioned. Many of these, if not all, lived after Moses his time. But yet he who set down sundry things before his time by a prophetical spirit might by the same spirit make known what was also to fall out after his time.

## CHAPTER 2

**Verse 1.** *{These are the sons of Israel}* Jacob was his first name: of which see Gen. 25:26. The name Israel was given him as an evidence of his fervency and faith in prayer, Gen. 32:24, etc.; Hos. 12:4. See Gen. 32:28. Not only Jacob himself was styled Israel, but also the church both under the law, Exod. 4:22; Deut. 4:1, and under the gospel, Gal. 6:16. All the genealogies following from this man are of such as were of the church. For though Jacob had twelve sons, yet were they all true believers and heads of the church, and therefore styled *the twelve patriarchs*, Acts 7:8, 9. Abraham and Isaac had each of them but one such son.

*{Reuben}* He committed a very foul sin, Gen. 35:22, for which he lost his birthright, ch. 5:1; Gen. 49:3, 4.

*{Simeon}* He executed a cruel act, Gen. 34:25, for which he received a curse, Gen. 49:5, 6, 7.

*{Levi}* He joined with his brother Simeon in the foresaid cruel act. But the curse denounced against him, which was to be divided in Jacob, and scattered in Israel, Gen. 49:7, proved a great blessing. For the Levites having no entire inheritance by themselves, had their cities and land in the choicest places of the other tribes, and so became the greater means of instructing their brethren.

*{and Judah}* He also committed a great sin; but temptation and ignorance of the person much extenuated it, Gen. 38:13, etc. The royal dignity is conferred on him, ch. 5:2; Gen. 49:8, etc. These four first were born of one mother immediately one after another, Gen. 29:32, etc.

*{Issachar and Zebulun}* These two were of the same mother that the other four were, but after their mother had left bearing for a time, Gen. 29:35; 30:17, etc.

**V. 2.** *{Dan}* He was the son of Bilhah Jacob’s maid, Gen. 30:5, and concubine, Gen. 35:22.

*{Joseph}* He was an especial preserver of his father’s house, Gen. 45:7.

*{and Benjamin}* Joseph and Benjamin were the children of Rachel, whom Jacob most loved, Gen. 29:18. She died in bringing forth Benjamin, Gen. 35:16, 17, 18.

*{Naphtali}* He was also the son of Bilhah, Gen. 30:7, 8.

*{Gad and Asher}* These two were sons of Zilpah Jacob’s other maid, Gen. 30:10, etc. She also was his concubine. All these had their faults, and some more heinous than others, yet none of them clean fell from the faith, but remained heads of the church. Of the reasons and notations of each and every of these patriarchs’ names see Gen. 29:32, etc.; 30:6, etc.; 35:18.

**V. 3.** *{The sons of Judah}* Judah was Jacob’s fourth son, v. 1, yet his genealogy is first set down by reason of the royal dignity settled on him, Gen. 49:10, and the descent of the Messiah from him, Luke 3:33.

*{Er, and Onan, and Shelah}* Gen. 38:3, 4, 5; 46:12.

*{which three were born unto him of the daughter of Shua the Canaanitess}* This epithet has relation to the word daughter. For Shuah was a man. See Gen. 38:2.

*{And Er the firstborn of Judah}* His dignity is here mentioned to aggravate the evil he did. Being the firstborn, he might have been progenitor of the royal stock, and of the Messiah king of kings.

*{was evil in the sight of the LORD}* See 1 Kings 14:2 and Gen. 38:7.

*{and he slew him}* By a sudden and extraordinary death. The like is noted of Onan, Gen. 38:9, 10.

**V. 4.** *{And Tamar}* Gen. 38:29, 30. Tamar is put into the catalogue of Christ’s ancestors, as Rahab and Ruth, Matt. 1:3, 5, to show that Christ came both of Jews and Gentiles (for these three women were Gentiles) and that notorious sins repented of hinder not salvation; for Christ came to save all sorts of sinners, Matt. 21:31.

*{his daughter in law}* See Gen. 38:12, etc.

*{bare him Pharez and Zerah}* These two were born of incest, yet through the mercy of God that was no prejudice to their prerogative. Of their names see Gen. 38:29, 30. Pharez first came out of the womb, and so was counted the firstborn, but it was by a kind of struggling with his brother.

*{All the sons of Judah were five}* The two eldest of the five died without children, so as no genealogy could be made; and Shelah’s genealogy is set down ch. 4:20. That God who of his free grace chose Jacob before Esau, Mal. 1:2, chose Pharez before Shelah and Zerah.

**V. 5.** *{The sons of Pharez}* God so blessed Pharez as among the posterity of Judah it was said, in craving a blessing on a family, *Let thy house be like the house of Pharez, whom Tamar bare unto Judah*, Ruth 4:12.

*{Hezron}* Gen. 46:12. This Hezron was reckoned in the right line among the progenitors of Christ, Matt. 1:3; Zach. 3:33.

*{and Hamul}* Of him came the Hamulites, Num. 26:21.

**V. 6.** *{And the sons of Zerah}* This was Judah’s youngest son, v. 4.

*{Zimri}* Or, *Zabdi*, Josh. 7:1. Zimri was the only son, properly so called, of Zerah, and grandfather to Achar, v. 7.

*{and Ethan, and Heman, and Calcol, and Dara}* Or, *Darda*. See 1 Kings 4:31.

*{five of them in all}* The four latter are counted to be of the posterity of Zerah. But they were not immediately born of him; for they were the sons of Mahol, 1 Kings 4:31.

**V. 7.** *{And the sons of Carmi, Achar}* Or, *Achan*. See on Josh. 7:1.

*{the troubler of Israel}* This is added to show the reason of putting Achar instead of Achan. For Achar signifies *a troubler*.

*{who transgressed in the thing accursed}* See Josh. 6:19 and 7:1, 25.

**V. 8.** *{And the sons of Ethan, Azariah}* It is supposed from the word sons that Ethan had more sons than Azariah, and that he only as the chiefest is named. A like instance is given v. 31. But the plural number may here synecdochically be put for the singular, as Gen. 46:23.

**V. 9.** *{The sons also of Hezron that were born unto him}* This is added, because many of the persons following, though of his posterity, came not immediately from his loins.

*{Jerahmeel}* Though this were the eldest son, yet Ram was preferred before him, and was one of the progenitors of the royal stock. See v. 4.

*{and Ram}* Or, *Aram*, Matt. 1:3; Luke 3:33.

*{and Chelubai}* Or, *Caleb*. His pedigree is set down v. 18.

**V. 10.** *{And Ram begat Amminadab}* Ruth 4:19; Matt. 1:4; Luke 3:33.

*{and Amminadab begat Nahshon prince of the children of Judah}* See Num. 1:7 and 2:3. This phrase of dignity has relation to the state of the Israelites in the wilderness.

**V. 11.** *{And Nahshon begat Salma}* Or, *Salmon*, Ruth 4:20; Matt. 1:4; Luke 3:32. There was a mountain called Salmon, Ps. 68:14; Judg. 9:48. This Salma married Rahab, Matt. 1:5. See v. 4.

*{and Salma begat Boaz}* Read his history in the book of Ruth.

**V. 12.** *{And Boaz begat Obed}* Of Ruth the Moabitess, Matt. 1:5.

*{and Obed begat Jesse}* Jesse’s name was famous in Israel, because he was the father of David, who is oft styled *the son of Jesse*; sometimes indeed by way of disgrace by Saul and his flatterers, as 1 Sam. 22:7, 8, 9. If we consider the years that passed betwixt Salma and David, we shall find that Salma, Boaz, Obed and Jesse must all be very old when they begot children, seeing more than four hundred years passed over in their days. It is said 1 Kings 6:1 that the fourth year of Solomon was four hundred and fourscore years from the children of Israel’s coming out of the land of Egypt. Take from them the forty years of David’s reign, and the thirty before he began to reign, and there remain to the time of his birth four hundred and ten. Now Rahab, wife to Salma, was a housekeeper when the children of Israel came into Canaan, Josh. 2:1. Not long after that Salma married her, whom we cannot in any probability account at that time under fifteen years old. Exempt then the forty years wherein the Israelites were in the wilderness from the fore-named four hundred and ten, reserving the fifteen years of Salma’s age, there will remain three hundred fourscore and five. By this account these four patriarchs must be about a hundred years apiece when they begot their children. It may be some of them might be over this number, and others under it. Some think there might be others betwixt these that are not recorded. But this genealogy being so express, not only in this place, but also Ruth 4:21, 22; Matt. 1:5, 6 and Luke 3:32, I dare not admit of any such deficiency in the sacred records.

**V. 13.** *{And Jesse begat his firstborn Eliab}* This man was of stature so high, and of countenance so comely, as when God appointed Samuel to anoint one of Jesse’s sons for king, he thought that Eliab was surely the Lord’s anointed, 1 Sam. 16:6.

*{and Abinadab the second, and Shimma the third}* Shimma is styled Shamma, 1 Sam. 16:9. These two, together with their eldest brother, were men of war, and went with King Saul to the battle, 1 Sam. 17:13.

**V. 14.** *{Nethaneel the fourth, Raddai the fifth}* Of these two, and of Ozem, v. 15, we have no mention elsewhere. There were others named Nathanael, as ch. 15:24 and 24:6 and 26:4. We read 1 Sam. 17:12 that Jesse had eight sons: and so much also is implied 1 Sam. 16:10, 11, but here is mention only of seven. It may be one of them died without issue, and so is not put into this genealogy. Or one of the eight there mentioned might be Jesse’s grandchild, and in that respect reputed among his sons.

**V. 15.** *{Ozem the sixth, David the seventh}* David was one of the most famous kings that ever was in the world. He was famous for integrity of heart and life (in which respect he is styled *a man after God’s own heart*, 1 Sam. 13:14; Acts 13:22), for wisdom, for valor, for zeal, for skill in music, and for many other excellent endowments. He was set as a pattern before all his successors, 1 Kings 3:14 and 11:38. More is registered of his praises in sacred Scripture than of any other man’s.

**V. 16.** *{Whose sisters were}* They are styled David’s sisters rather than the sisters of Eliab, or any other of the brothers, yea, rather than the daughters of Jesse, for honor’s sake. For it was a greater honor to be David’s sister than Jesse’s daughter.

*{Zeruiah}* A woman of great note; therefore her sons, valiant men all, are ordinarily styled by her name, 2 Sam. 2:13, 18 and 16:9, 10.

*{and Abigail}* She is called *the daughter of Nahash*, 2 Sam. 17:25. David married a wife of this name, 1 Sam. 25:42.

*{And the sons of Zeruiah, Abishai}* This was one of David’s worthies, 2 Sam. 23:18, and ever cleaved close to him, even before he was king, 1 Sam. 26:6. Much is spoken of his valiant deeds, but nothing of his death.

*{and Joab}* Though younger than Abishai, he got the preeminence above him, and was made general of David’s host, ch. 11:6.

*{and Asahel, three}* Asahel also was one of David’s worthies, 2 Sam. 23:24, but slain in the very beginning of his reign, 2 Sam. 2:23.

**V. 17.** *{And Abigail bare Amasa}* A valiant man; but he took part with Absalom, and was general of his army, 2 Sam. 17:25. He was treacherously slain by his cousin Joab, 2 Sam. 20:10.

*{and the father of Amasa was Jether the Ishmeelite}* An Ishmeelite by birth, but by profession an Israelite, 2 Sam. 17:25. Or he might be called an Ishmeelite, because he had long lived among the Ishmeelites. He is called Ithra, 2 Sam. 17:25.

**V. 18.** *{And Caleb}* Or, *Chelubai*, v. 9. Not that Caleb who was sent to search the land, Num. 23:7, and after entered into Canaan, Num. 14:30; Josh. 14:6, whose genealogy is set down ch. 4:15. This man’s generation was very great, and is therefore here set down to give an instance of God’s blessing of Judah by multiplying his posterity. The genealogy of David is here broken off, because, after the rest of the fathers of the tribe of Judah, it is to be set down further than any in this book, ch. 3:1, etc.

*{the son of Hezron}* Mentioned, v. 5. Hereby this Caleb is distinguished from the son of Jephunneh, ch. 4:15.

*{begat children of Azubah his wife}* This was his first wife, v. 19. Of this name was Jehoshaphat’s mother, 1 Kings 22:42. The posterity of Caleb by Azubah are set down v. 42, etc.

*{and of Jerioth}* This was Caleb’s second wife.

*{her sons are these}* This relative her has reference to Jerioth.

*{Jesher, and Shobab, and Ardon}* The posterity of these are nowhere mentioned. David had a son named Shobab, ch. 3:5.

**V. 19.** *{And when Azubah was dead, Caleb took unto him Ephrath}* This was his third wife. She gave the name to the city Ephratah, Ruth 4:11; Mic. 5:2.

*{which bare him Hur}* There was anon after the time that this Hur lived, if not in his time, another of his name, that was a great prince and governor over the children of Israel in the wilderness, Exod. 24:14 and 17:10.

**V. 20.** *{And Hur begat Uri, and Uri begat Bezaleel}* This was that Bezaleel, Exod. 31:2, 3, 4.

**V. 21.** *{And afterward}* After the foresaid sons of Hezron were born.

*{Hezron went in}* A modest expression of the matrimonial duty, such a one as that Gen. 4:1.

*{to the daughter}* This was Hezron’s third wife.

*{of Machir}* Manasseh’s eldest son, Gen. 50:23; Num. 32:39.

*{the father}* The word father is put for head or chief ruler.

*{of Gilead}* Gilead was the name of a mount, Gen. 31:21, 23, 25, of a land, Num. 32:1, in which were sundry cities, and Josh. 13:15. Some take it here for the proper name of a man: if so, then father is here to be taken properly. Moses seems to put Gilead here spoken of for the proper name of a man, Num. 26:29, 30.

*{whom he married}* Heb. *took*. Namely, to be his wife.

*{when he was threescore years old}* Heb. *a son of threescore years*. This age implies that the daughter of Machir was his last wife.

*{and she bare him Segub}* This was also the name of the youngest son of Hiel, 1 Kings 16:34.

**V. 22.** *{And Segub begat Jair}* Jair is said to be the son of Manasseh, Num. 32:41, but son is there to be taken for grandchild. He was accounted the son of Manasseh by reason of his grandmother the daughter of Machir, v. 21, for by the father’s side Hezron was his grandfather. Either Jair or Segub may be supposed to be adopted into the family of Machir: for Jair had his inheritance in the tribe of Manasseh, as the words following show.

*{who had three and twenty cities in the land of Gilead}* This relative who has reference to Jair and his posterity. The land of Gilead fell to Machir, whose daughter was grandmother to Jair, v. 21. The many cities here mentioned show that the land of Gilead was of a large compass.

**V. 23.** *{And he took Geshur, and Aram, with the towns of Jair, from them}* Jair by force of arms took all those places from those that inhabited them before, being of the cursed nations which God had devoted to destruction, Gen. 15:18, etc. Upon Jair’s taking of them they were called *Havoth-Jair*, i.e., *cities of Jair*. See Num. 32:42; Deut. 3:14; Josh. 13:30.

*{with Kenath}* It is said Num. 32:42, *Nobah took Kenath, and called it Nobah, after his own name*. This Nobah might be one of the posterity of Jair, and so that be said to be done by Jair, which was done by his sons, or sons’ sons. Or Nobah might be a chief commander under Jair: and that which is done in war uses to be attributed to the general.

*{and the towns thereof}* Heb. *daughters*. See ch. 7:28. Which appertained thereunto; or, whereof Kenath had a command.

*{even threescore cities}* It seems Kenath was a city of great consequence, in that it had command over so many other cities.

*{All these belonged to the sons of Machir}* No verb in this sentence being expressed in the Hebrew, translators and expositors differ thereabouts, some inserting one verb, some another; as, *All these the sons of Machir took*; or, *All these the sons of Machir held*; or, as our translators, *All these belonged to the sons of Machir*. All tend to one and the same end. By the sons of Machir Segub, Jair, yea and Nobah too, are meant. For though Hezron were their proper father, yet they being born of the daughter of Machir, Machir adopted them to be his sons, took them into his family, and gave them inheritances.

*{the father of Gilead}* See v. 21.

**V. 24.** *{And after that Hezron was dead in Caleb-ephratah}* This place might be so called in memorial of Caleb and his wife, both buried there. See v. 19.

*{then Abiah Hezron’s wife}* This might be another wife of Hezron; or else Abiah might be the name of that daughter of Machir, v. 21.

*{bare him Ashur}* This son was *Posthumus*; born after his father’s death.

*{the father of Tekoa}* I.e., the progenitor, or prince and chief governor of the inhabitants of Tekoa. Mention is made of this city 2 Sam. 14:2. It was near to Jerusalem.

**V. 25.** *{And the sons of Jerahmeel the firstborn of Hezron}* See v. 9.

*{were Ram the firstborn}* This also was the name of his father’s brother, v. 9.

*{and Bunah, and Oren, and Ozem, and Ahijah}* Ahijah is by most expositors taken for the wife of Jerahmeel, and not for one of his sons. Being taken for his wife, it is thus to be translated, *Of Ahijah*.

**V. 26.** *{Jerahmeel had also another wife, whose name was Atarah}* This gives evidence that Ahijah before mentioned was Jerahmeel’s first wife; and in reference thereunto Atarah is styled another wife.

*{she was the mother of Onam}* It seems Ahijah was the mother of those sons of Jerahmeel, mentioned v. 25.

**V. 27.** *{And the sons of Ram the firstborn of Jerahmeel}* See v. 25.

*{were Maaz, and Jamin, and Eker}* We read no more of these, nor of any of their posterity. One of Simeon’s sons was called Jamin, Gen. 46:10.

**V. 28.** *{And the sons of Onam were Shammai and Jada}* Of Juda’s posterity see v. 32.

*{And the sons of Shammai, Nadab}* Aaron’s eldest son was also of this name, Exod. 6:23.

*{and Abishur}* See v. 27.

**V. 29.** *{And the name of the wife of Abishur was Abihail}* Wives and mothers are oft named for honor’s sake. Rehoboam’s wife also was called Abihail, 2 Chr. 11:18, and Esther’s mother, Esth. 2:15.

*{and she bare him Ahban and Molid}* There was no more mention of these.

**V. 30.** *{And the sons of Nadab, Seled, and Appaim: but Seled died without children}* The best means to preserve his memory.

**V. 31.** *{The sons of Appaim, Ishi}* See ch. 5:24. Of sons see v. 8.

*{And the sons of Ishi, Sheshan. And the children of Sheshan, Ahlai}* By children daughters are here meant. See v. 34. Withal here is a synecdoche in the word children, as in the word sons before mentioned: for we read but of one daughter he had, Ahlai; *q.d.*, *All the daughters that he had, was Ahlai*. See the like Gen. 21:7.

**V. 32.** *{And the sons of Jada the brother of Shammai}* See v. 28.

*{Jether, and Jonathan: and Jether died without children}* There were others called Jether, as Gideon’s son, Judg. 8:20; Amasa’s father, v. 17; Ezra’s son, ch. 4:17, and others.

**V. 33.** *{And the sons of Jonathan, Peleth, and Zaza}* No further mention of these.

*{These were the sons of Jerahmeel}* By sons are here meant posterity.

**V. 34.** *{Now Sheshan had no sons, but daughters}* If the word translated *children*, v. 31, be taken for *sons*, then it is to be supposed that all his sons at this time were dead.

*{and Sheshan had a servant an Egyptian, whose name was Jarha}* Mention is here made of that servant because of the honor done him, v. 35. He was certainly a proselyte, otherwise Sheshan might not have given his daughter to him being an Egyptian.

**V. 35.** *{And Sheshan gave his daughter to Jarha his servant to wife}* Questionless this servant was very faithful, and one whom Sheshan highly accounted of.

*{and she bare him Attai}* If him have reference to Sheshan, it shows that the posterity following were so accounted Sheshan’s as they kept up his name and memory; if to Jarha, it shows that wives bear children to their husbands to continue their names.

**V. 36.** *{And Attai begat Nathan}* The numerous posterity here in order set down shows that the stock of Jarha, though an Egyptian, long continued in Israel.

*{and Nathan begat Zabad}* This Zabad was one of David’s worthies, ch. 11:41, and is there said to be *the son of Ahlai*; but in relation to the third generation.

**V. 37.** *{And Zabad begat Ephlal}* Not mentioned elsewhere.

*{and Ephlal begat Obed}* Another Obed than he mentioned v. 12.

**V. 38.** *{And Obed begat Jehu, etc.}* Here are thirteen generations, from v. 35 to 42, that came from Sheshan by his daughter, not mentioned elsewhere.

**V. 42.** *{Now the sons of Caleb the brother of Jerahmeel}* See v. 9, 18.

*{were Mesha his firstborn}* By Azubah his first wife, v. 18.

*{which was the father of Ziph}* Ziph was a city of Judah, Josh. 15:55. It was also the name of a wilderness, 1 Sam. 23:14, 15, 24. The word father here may be taken for a planter or first establisher of a city, so v. 45, 49, or for the first head of a stock, or for the chief or prince, and so Ziph here put for the inhabitants of Ziph. See ch. 4:16.

*{and the sons of Mareshah}* I.e., the sons of Maresha descended from Caleb.

*{the father of Hebron}* Father has reference to Maresha. Hebron is the name of a place frequently mentioned in Scripture, as Gen. 13:18; Num. 13:23; Josh. 10:36 and 14:13; 2 Sam. 2:1, 3, 11.

**V. 43.** *{And the sons of Hebron, Korah}* There were divers of this name, as Gen. 36:5; Exod. 6:21. But of this Korah we read only here.

*{and Tappuah}* This also was the name of a place, Josh. 12:17 and 17:8.

*{and Rekem}* This also was the name of a king of Midian, Num. 31:8, and of a city of Benjamin, Josh. 18:27.

*{and Shema}* One of Reuben’s posterity, ch. 5:8, and one of Benjamin’s also, ch. 8:13, were of this name. It was also the name of a city of Judah, Josh. 15:26.

**V. 44.** *{And Shema begat Raham, the father of Jorkoam}* Raham and Jorkoam are mentioned nowhere else.

*{and Rekem begat Shammai}* Rekem was the third son of Hebron, v. 43. There was another Shammai mentioned v. 28.

**V. 45.** *{And the son of Shammai was Maon}* There was also a city of this name, Josh. 15:55, and a wilderness, 1 Sam. 23:24, 25 and 25:2.

*{and Maon was the father of Beth-zur}* Beth-zur was a city of Judah, Josh. 15:58, so as father is here to be taken as v. 42.

**V. 46.** *{And Ephah Caleb’s concubine}* Of concubines see 1 Kings 11:3. Besides the three wives, v. 18, 19, Caleb took also concubines, the more to increase his posterity.

*{bare Haran, and Moza, and Gazez}* Haran’s and Moza’s offspring follow; but no posterity of Gazez is recorded.

*{and Haran begat Gazez}* Haran’s brother and son had both one and the same name.

**V. 47.** *{And the sons of Jahdai}* Moza is supposed to be here understood out of v. 46, *q.d.*, *And the sons of Moza were Jahdai*.

*{Regem, and Jotham, etc.}* These six were all sons of Jahdai.

**V. 48.** *{Maachah Caleb’s concubine}* See v. 46.

*{bare Sheber, and Tirhanah}* Nothing is elsewhere said of these.

**V. 49.** *{She bare also Shaaph the father of Madmannah}* Some understand under this relative she this word wife, *q.d.*, *The wife of Shaaph the father of Madmannah bare Shevah*.

*{Sheva the father of Machbenah, and the father of Gibea}* In all the three places father is taken alike, for a planter, or founder, or head, or prince, as v. 42, 45. Madmannah, Machbenah, and Gibeah are cities of Judah, Josh. 15:31, 57.

*{and the daughter of Caleb was Achsah}* Josh. 15:17. This is taken to be the eldest daughter of the first Caleb, whose genealogy has in this chapter been set down. True it is, the other Caleb had also a daughter of this name; but it is not unusual for divers persons to have the same name.

**V. 50.** *{These were the sons}* This has relation to Shobal, etc., v. 51.

*{of Caleb the son of Hur}* Hur was the son of that Caleb, v. 19, so as this Caleb was the other Caleb’s grandchild. There are three Calebs famous in Israel: I. The son of Hezron, v. 18. II. The son of Hur. III. The son of Jephunneh, ch. 4:15.

*{the firstborn of Ephratah}* Called Ephrath, v. 19. Hur is said to be her firstborn, because Caleb had another firstborn by another wife, v. 18.

*{Shobal}* See another of this name ch. 1:40; Gen. 36:20.

*{the father of Kirjath-jearim}* Father is here to be taken as v. 43. Kirjath-jearim was one of the cities of the Gibeonites, Josh. 9:17, and afterwards fell to the lot of Judah, Josh. 18:14.

**V. 51.** *{Salma the father of Bethlehem}* Father is here taken as before. Bethlehem was also a city of Judah, and therefore called Bethlehem-Judah, Judg. 17:7 and 19:1, famous by being Christ’s birthplace, Mic. 5:2; Matt. 2:6. Salma had a right to Bethlehem by his grandfather Hur, who is also called *the father of Bethlehem*, ch. 4:4.

*{Hareph the father of Beth-Gader}* Father is here to be taken as v. 49.

**V. 52.** *{And Shobal the father of Kirjath-jearim}* See v. 50.

*{had sons, Haroeh}* Or, *Reaiah*, ch. 4:2. Haroe may be taken appellatively.

*{and half of the Manahethites}* Or, *half of the Manuchites*; or, *Hatzi-Hammenucoth*. Or, *of those that inhabit the country of Menucoth*. This is taken to be a region in the confines of Judah, Benjamin and Dan. If the word Haroe be taken appellatively, it may be thus translated, *He was overseer (or governor) of half of the Manahethites*.

**V. 53.** *{And the families of Kirjath-jearim, the Ithrites, etc.}* These descended from Shobal, v. 52. We read of Ira an Ithrite, and Gareb an Ithrite, two of David’s worthies, 2 Sam. 23:38; 1 Chr. 11:40. But we read not elsewhere of Puhites, Shumathites, Mishraites, Zareathites, and Eshtaulites.

*{of them came the Zareathites, and the Eshtaulites}* I.e., the inhabitants of Eshtaol and Zoreah in the tribe of Judah, Josh. 15:33.

**V. 54.** *{The sons of Salma, Bethlehem}* See v. 51. By sons are here meant posterity; and by Bethlehem, such as inhabited it. Some translate, *Bethlehemites*.

*{and the Netophathites}* Such as came of the city or family of Netophathi in the tribe of Judah, Neh. 12:28.

*{Ataroth, the house of Joab, etc.}* Or, *Atarites*; or, *crowns of the house of Joab*. Ataroth is put for the name of a place; and it was a plain in the borders of Ephraim, Benjamin and Judah, which was distinguished with divers names according to its different situation or bounds. See Josh. 16:2, 5, 7. Here the house of Joab is added thereunto, to show that the family of Joab possessed part of Ataroth. Ataroth is also taken appellatively, and signifies crowns. In which sense the Netophathites are called the *crowns of the house of Joab*, because by their valor and good success under his conduct they were as crowns to him; they were his glory. See Phil. 4:1.

**V. 55.** *{And the families of the scribes that dwelt at Jabez}* This verse is here inserted because the families mentioned therein, though they belonged not to the tribe of Judah, yet dwelt in the desert of Judah, where Jabez was, Judg. 1:16. By scribes are meant public notaries, such as by writing maintained themselves and their families, though they had no inheritance among the Jews. Some take scribes for such as were well exercised in the law of God, as Ezra 7:6, and instructed the people therein.

*{the Tirathites, the Shimeathites, and Suchathites}* These are nowhere else mentioned. Their titles may be taken from some of their predecessors. The Suchathites may be so called from dwelling in tabernacles.

*{These are the Kenites}* The Kenites were of the posterity of Jethro Moses his father-in-law, Exod. 3:1; Judg. 1:16, so called from the country where they dwelt.

*{that came of Hemath}* Hemath was an ancient progenitor of the Kenites; as also Heber was, Judg. 4:11, who also came from Moses his father-in-law. They that descended from Heber seated themselves among the Naphtalites, Judg. 4:11, but these here mentioned, among the children of Judah, Judg. 1:16.

*{the father of the house of Rechab}* This is added for distinction’s sake, to show what Hemath is spoken of; and also for honor’s sake, Rechab being a man of great note and name. See 2 Kings 10:15; Jer. 35:2.

## CHAPTER 3

**Verse 1.** *{Now these were the sons of David}* This chapter has reference to ch. 2:15, where mention is made of David the youngest son of Jesse. David’s genealogy reaches further than any other in this book, even far beyond the captivity. David was the head of that royal progeny from whence Christ came.

*{which were born unto him in Hebron}* Here David was first settled in his kingdom, and here he reigned over Judah in his seven first years, 2 Sam. 5:5. We may hence infer that while Saul persecuted him from place to place he had no children.

*{the firstborn Amnon}* He deflowered his sister Tamar, and was slain by her brother Absalom, 2 Sam. 13:1, 14, 28, 29.

*{of Ahinoam the Jezreelitess}* Jezreel, whence this woman had her name, was a city of Judah, Josh. 15:56.

*{the second Daniel}* Or, *Chileab*, 2 Sam. 3:3.

*{of Abigail the Carmelitess}* First Nabal’s wife, and after his death married to David, 1 Sam. 25:3, 39, etc. She is called Carmelitess from Carmel, where Nabal dwelt, 1 Sam. 25:2, 40.

**V. 2.** *{The third, Absalom}* He sought to take the crown from his father, and was slain in his treason, 2 Sam. 15:1, etc., and 18:13, etc.

*{the son of Maachah the daughter of Talmai king of Geshur}* David, while he was in the land of the Philistims, invaded Geshur, and took all with him, 1 Sam. 27:1, 8, 9. And it may be that then he took Maachah. Or the king of Geshur, retaining his kingdom, and observing what good success David had against all his enemies, sought peace with him, and gave him his daughter to wife. We read how Absalom, after he had slain his brother Amnon, fled to his grandfather Talmai, 2 Sam. 13:37. But howsoever David took this wife, it is to be supposed that she became a proselyte.

*{the fourth, Adonijah}* He would have usurped the kingdom before his father was dead, 1 Kings 1:5, etc. He was slain by his brother Solomon, 1 Kings 2:25.

*{the son of Haggith}* See 1 Kings 1:5.

**V. 3.** *{The fifth, Shephatiah of Abital}* This name Shephatiah was given to sundry other persons, ch. 9:8; 12:5; 27:16. But of this Shephatiah and Abital we read no more than that the one was the son of David, and the other his wife.

*{the sixth, Ithream by Eglah his wife}* The five before mentioned were also David’s wives, so as to every of them may his wife be referred. See 2 Sam. 3:5.

**V. 4.** *{These six were born unto him in Hebron}* See v. 1.

*{and there he reigned seven years and six months}* After Saul’s death the men of Judah came to Hebron, and there anointed David king, 2 Sam. 2:3, 4. But many of the tribes of Israel took part with Abner, who set up Ishbosheth Saul’s son to be king, 2 Sam. 2:8, 9. Betwixt these two houses there was long war; but at length David prevailed, 2 Sam. 3:1, etc., and Ishbosheth was slain, 2 Sam. 4:7. After which all Israel took part with David, and made him king, 2 Sam. 5:3. This is the reason of the distinction of the years of his reign.

*{and in Jerusalem he reigned thirty and three years}* It is said 2 Sam. 5:4 that *he reigned forty years*; so that six months must either be added to those forty years, or taken from these thirty and three.

**V. 5.** *{And these were born unto him in Jerusalem}* After he was settled in his kingdom, and taken by all Israel to be their king. See ch. 14:3, etc.

*{Shimea}* Or, *Shamma*, 2 Sam. 5:14.

*{and Shobab, and Nathan}* Some think these were the sons of Uriah, whose wife David deflowered, 2 Sam. 11, and that David having adopted them his sons, they are said to be born unto him, i.e., every way accounted his, as if they had proceeded from his loins. But this will not well stand with that which is noted of Nathan, who was one of these three, Luke 3:31. If he had been Uriah’s son, then had not Christ properly descended from David. Whereas they say that Solomon, who is here set after all, was the next to him that was born in adultery, 2 Sam. 12:25, etc., I answer, The order of birth is not here observed, but rather these four are set down in a clean contrary order; the youngest first, the eldest last. That Solomon was the eldest is evident, 2 Sam. 12:24, and that Nathan was the second appears, in that Solomon’s stock failing, Nathan’s came to have the royal dignity, Luke 3:31. It being the purpose of him that wrote this genealogy to prosecute Solomon’s progeny, he sets him in the last place. Of Shimea and Shobab we read no more but that they were sons of David.

*{and Solomon, four}* This particle four has relation to the number of the men, not to their order. For Solomon was not the fourth, i.e., the last of the four, but one of the four, namely, the first.

*{of Bath-shua}* Or, *Bath-sheba*, 2 Sam. 11:3. This latter name is most used in Scripture. The former signifies *a daughter of salvation*; the latter, *a daughter of oath*.

*{the daughter of Ammiel}* Or, *Eliam*, 2 Sam. 11:3. Her father’s name is set down for honor’s sake.

**V. 6.** *{Ibhar also}* We read no other mention of this man.

*{and Elishama}* Or, *Elishua*, 2 Sam. 5:15.

*{and Eliphelet}* These two names Elishama and Eliphelet were given to two other sons of David, v. 8, these two perhaps being dead before the other two were born.

**V. 7.** *{And Nogah}* This Noga and his elder brother Eliphelet are left out in the account, 2 Sam. 5:15, because, it may be, they died young without children.

*{and Nepheg, and Japhia}* No other mention is made of these two. There was of old a king of Lachish named Japhia, Josh. 10:3, and a place also of that name, Josh. 19:12.

**V. 8.** *{And Elishama}* See v. 6.

*{and Eliada}* Or, *Beliada*, ch. 14:7.

*{and Eliphelet}* See v. 6.

*{nine}* This number has reference to those mentioned, v. 6, 7, 8. These were born to David after those four born unto him in Jerusalem of Bathshuah, v. 5. Those added to these nine make up thirteen. Of these nine only seven were living together, 2 Sam. 5:15, 16. Whether all the nine were born of Bath-sheba, or of several wives, is not expressed: but Bath-sheba lived long enough with David to have had so many children: for she outlived David, 1 Kings 2:13.

**V. 9.** *{These were all the sons of David}* Nineteen in all; six born in Hebron, v. 4, four in Jerusalem, v. 5, and nine after them, v. 8.

*{besides the sons of the concubines}* See 1 Kings 11:3. The best of the saints under the law had both many wives and also concubines: for great was their desire of a numerous progeny.

*{and Tamar their sister}* By the father she was sister to all the nineteen before mentioned; but by the mother only to Absalom, 2 Sam. 13:4.

**V. 10.** *{And Solomon’s son}* This has reference to v. 5.

*{was Rehoboam}* 1 Kings 11:43. With this man begin Solomon’s royal race. And it is in order set down, till it utterly failed in Jeconiah, Jer. 22:30. He was a foolish king, though the son of a wise father, and by his folly lost the greater part of his kingdom.

*{Abia his son}* Or, *Abiam*. A wicked king, 1 Kings 15:1, 3.

*{Asa his son}* He long continued a good king, 2 Chr. 14:2 and 16:13.

*{Jehoshaphat his son}* A very good king, yet not without his faults, 2 Chr. 17:4; 18:1; 19:2.

**V. 11.** *{Joram his son}* Or, *Jehoram*. A very wicked king, 2 Chr. 21:1, etc.

*{Ahaziah his son}* Or, *Azariah*, 2 Chr. 22:6. He was an evil king, and reigned but a while, 2 Chr. 22:2, etc.

*{Joash his son}* A notorious hypocrite, 2 Chr. 24:17, etc.

**V. 12.** *{Amaziah his son}* An evil king, 2 Chr. 25:14, etc.

*{Azariah his son}* Or, *Uzziah*, 2 Kings 15:30. This king apostatized and fell from a glorious profession, 2 Chr. 26:16.

*{Jotham his son}* A good king, 2 Chr. 27:2.

**V. 13.** *{Ahaz his son}* A notorious idolater, 2 Chr. 28:1, etc.

*{Hezekiah his son}* A very good king. But after many mercies received his heart was too much puffed up. Notwithstanding he humbled himself, 2 Chr. 32:25, 26.

*{Manasseh his son}* This man from his childhood was very lewd, cruel, and a great idolater; but afterwards repented, 2 Chr. 32:2, etc.

**V. 14.** *{Amon his son}* A wicked king, 2 Chr. 33:22.

*{Josiah his son}* He was the last good king: yet he fell into an error in opposing the king of Egypt, which was his death, 2 Chr. 35:10, etc.

**V. 15.** *{And the sons of Josiah were}* Three of his sons sat upon the throne one after another, 2 Kings 23:30, 34 and 24:17.

*{the firstborn, Johanan}* Or, *Joachaz*. See 2 Kings 23:30; Jer. 22:11.

*{the second Jehoiakim}* Or, *Joakim*. Or, *Eliakim*. See 2 Kings 23:34. This man had three names.

*{the third Zedekiah}* Or, *Mattaniah*. See 2 Kings 24:17.

*{the fourth Shallum}* This name was given to Johanan, Jer. 22:1. This word of number, the fourth, implies that Shallum was another from the former three; but because no mention is made elsewhere of him, it is to be supposed that he died before his father Josiah.

**V. 16.** *{And the sons of Jehoiakim, Jeconiah his son}* Or, *Jehoiachin*, 2 Kings 24:6. This man succeeded his father in the kingdom for three months: then yielding to Nebuchadnezzar, his life was spared, and he carried captive into Babylon.

*{Zedekiah his son}* This relative his seems to have reference to Jeconiah before mentioned. But this is the same Zedekiah that is mentioned v. 15, the son of Josiah, and uncle to Jeconiah, 2 Kings 24:17. But he is here said to be Jeconiah’s son legally, because he succeeded him on the throne. He is not reckoned among the sons of Jeconiah in the verses following.

**V. 17.** *{And the sons of Jeconiah}* Or, *Coniah*, Jer. 22:24. See on Jer. 22:30 in what respect Jeconiah is said to be childless.

*{Assir}* This word may be taken appellatively, and translated *bound*, or *imprisoned*, and so thus read, *The sons of Jeconiah bound, Salathiel*. Nor do we read in any other place of a son of Jeconiah called Assir. One of Corah’s sons had that name, Exod. 6:24, and two others of his posterity, ch. 6:23, 37. See on Jer. 22:30.

*{Salathiel his son}* Heb. *Shealtiel*. Salathiel is said to be the son of Neri, Luke 3:27, from whom Christ by natural descent came: and he is here said to be Jeconiah’s son, in that he succeeded him in a right to the royal dignity, Matt. 1:12. Or else this Salathiel was a distinct person from him whom Luke mentions.

**V. 18.** *{Malchiram also, and Pedaiah, etc.}* These six were sons of Salathiel. The expression is very concise: and something must be added, either in the beginning, thus, *The sons of Salathiel were these, Malchiram, etc.*, or in the end, thus, *Were the sons of Salathiel*.

**V. 19.** *{And the sons of Pedaiah were Zerubbabel and Shimei}* Zerubbabel is said to be the son of Shealtiel, Ezra 3:2 and 5:2; Neh. 12:1; Hag. 1:1; Matt. 1:12; Luke 3:27. Therefore it is supposed that Pedajah died while his sons were young, and Salathiel their grandfather brought them up, and in this respect Zerubbabel is called the son of Salathiel, or Shealtiel. Besides, it is usual to call grandchildren sons.

*{and the sons of Zerubbabel, Meshullam and Hananiah}* Neither of these are mentioned Matt. 1:13; Luke 3:27, whereupon some suppose this was another Zerubbabel than he that is mentioned in those places.

*{and Shelomith their sister}* She was sister to those two before mentioned both by father and mother, and therefore is added next to them, before the others, v. 20.

**V. 20.** *{And Hashubah, etc.}* These five were also sons of Zerubbabel, though by another wife than Meshullam and Hananiah, v. 19. Some take these to be sons of Meshullam.

**V. 21.** *{And the sons of Hananiah, Pelatiah, and Jesaiah, etc.}* Here is set down a catalogue of such families as belonged to the royal stock of David, and remained after the captivity.

**V. 22.** *{And the sons of Shechaniah, Shemaiah}* Either the plural sons is synecdochically put for the singular, or the grandchildren following are comprised under the title sons.

*{and the sons of Shemaiah, Hattush, and Igeal, and Bariah, and Neariah, and Shaphat, six}* There are but five sons here reckoned up, therefore the father must be added to make up the number, as ch. 7:3. Or this number may have relation to another son that was dead before the rest. Some refer this to Shecaniah, and so bring in Shemaiah to be comprised under the number of six.

**V. 23.** *{And the sons of Neariah}* Neariah is the last save one of the former number, yet his posterity only is set down. It may be none of the rest had issue, or the royal dignity that remained was settled on him.

*{Elioenai, and Hezekiah, etc.}* Heb. *Hizkijahu*. The names, here and in other places, that end with *Jah* have *Vav* added to them; which wants not emphasis: for Jah signifies *Lord*; and *Jahu*, *his Lord*.

**V. 24.** *{And the sons of Elioenai, were Hodaiah, etc.}* Here are many generations set down of persons one descending from another after the captivity, which questionless extended beyond the days of Ezra: yet he might by a prophetical spirit set them down beforehand; or else some other prophet, after his death, add them: as the death and burial of Moses is added to his last book, Deut. 34:5, etc. The Jews place this book last, because the latest genealogies and stories are recorded in it.

## CHAPTER 4

**Verse 1.** *{The sons of Judah}* By sons, posterity is here meant; for the particulars following were of several and distinct generations.

*{Pharez}* This was the only proper son of Judah here mentioned, Gen. 38:29 and 46:12. See ch. 2:4.

*{Hezron}* He was the son of Pharez, ch. 2:5.

*{and Carmi}* Or, *Chelubai*, ch. 2:9. Or, *Caleb*, ch. 2:18. All these three names set out one and the same person, which was the son of Hezron.

*{and Hur, and Shobal}* This Hur was the son of the first Caleb, called Chelubai, ch. 2:9, 19, and had a son called also Caleb, who was the immediate father of Shobal, ch. 2:50, but is here left out.

**V. 2.** *{And Reaiah}* Or, *Haroe*, ch. 3:52.

*{the son of Shobal}* By this relation we see how Reajah came by lineal descent from Judah. The former genealogy from Judah by Caleb here ended, and the royal stock by David was inserted, ch. 3. Now that former genealogy is prosecuted, and the posterity of Reajah distinctly set down.

*{begat Jahath}* This has relation to Reajah.

*{and Jahath begat Ahumai, and Lahad}* No further mention is made of these two.

*{These are the families of the Zorathites}* From those before mentioned descended the inhabitants of Zoreah, Josh. 15:33.

**V. 3.** *{And these were of the father of Etam, Jezreel, and Ishma, and Idbash}* Etam was a city belonging to Judah, 2 Chr. 11:6. By father is meant the founder or governor thereof. This father of Etam must needs be one of those ancestors from whom Jahath and his children, and those also here mentioned, descended; probably Reajah, v. 2, or one of the former progenitors, v. 1.

*{and the name of their sister was Hazelelponi}* Women of note and name use to be mentioned in genealogies, ch. 3:9, 19.

**V. 4.** *{And Penuel the father of Gedor}* Or, *of the father of Gedor*, as v. 3. For Jered is also styled the father of Gedor, v. 18.

*{and Ezer the father of Hushah}* Or, *of the father*, as v. 3. Gedor and Hushah are names of places, v. 30; Josh. 15:18; 2 Sam. 21:18 and 23:27. Hushah by transposition of letters Shuah, v. 11.

*{These are the sons of Hur}* This has relation to Shobal and to all under him before mentioned.

*{the firstborn of Ephratah}* See ch. 2:50.

*{the father of Bethlehem}* Father has reference to Hur, v. 1. See ch. 2:51.

**V. 5.** *{And Ashur the father of Tekoa}* See ch. 2:24.

*{had two wives, Helah, and Naarah}* Their several children are set down, v. 6, 7.

**V. 6.** *{And Naarah bare him Ahuzam, and Hepher}* Frequent mention is made of this name Hepher. It was the name of Zelophehad’s father, Num. 36:2, of one of David’s worthies, ch. 11:36, of a region, Josh. 12:17; 1 Kings 4:10.

*{and Temeni, and Haahashtari}* These were names of families.

*{These were the sons of Naarah}* This phrase is to be taken exclusively. She had none but these.

**V. 7.** *{And the sons of Helah}* The first wife of Ashur, v. 5.

*{were Zereth, Jezoar, and Ethnan}* To these must Coz be added.

**V. 8.** *{And Coz begat Anub, and Zobebah}* Though Coz were not before mentioned, yet the bringing in of his genealogy in this place shows that he was one of Ashur’s sons by Helah. See the like v. 14 and ch. 2:47.

*{and the families of Aharhel, the son of Harum}* This relative son has reference to Aharhel.

**V. 9.** *{And Jabez was more honourable than his brethren}* Jabez was some prime man, yet nowhere else mentioned. He is taken to be head of one of the families of Aharhel, v. 8, and to be the chiefest among the posterity of Ashur. The city Jabez, ch. 2:55, might be called from him. In that city dwelt scribes, and in that respect he is counted to have been a learned scribe.

*{and his mother called his name Jabez}* I.e., *sorrowful*. *Jazeb* properly signifies sorrowful; so as there is a transposition of letters in the word Jabez.

*{saying, Because I bare him with sorrow}* It seems she had a very hard travail, and (as Rachel, Gen. 35:18) gave a name to her son in memorial thereof. But it is said that his father changed his name (as Jacob did) and called him Othniel: of whom see Judg. 1:13, etc., and 3:9.

**V. 10.** *{And Jabez called on the God of Israel, saying}* This shows he was a man of piety and faith.

*{O that thou wouldest bless me indeed}* Heb. *If thou blessing wilt bless*. Of doubling words see 1 Kings 8:13. This phrase is in form of a vow and is concise and emphatical; that which he vowed to return to God being not expressed, but understood. It implies that he would render praise unto God, and cleave close unto him as a faithful servant.

*{and enlarge my coast}* It seems his portion fell out too narrow for him and his children; and therefore he seeks to enlarge it by force against those cursed enemies that remained not cast out.

*{and that thy hand might be with me}* He knew and acknowledged that without God’s help he could do nothing.

*{and that thou wouldest keep me from evil (Heb. do me, or, that thou dost with the evil) that it may not grieve me}* Some here add deliverance, thus, *That thou work deliverance from the evil, that it grieve me not*. His meaning is, that God would protect and prosper him, and give him such success that he might not be foiled nor oppressed by the enemy. Probably he was in some extreme danger when he made this prayer, as Jehoshaphat, 1 Kings 22:32, or made it while he was in the fight.

*{And God granted him that which he requested}* God gave him victory, so as the enemy was destroyed, he possessed their land, and enlarged his own habitation. In this was he more honorable than his brethren.

**V. 11.** *{And Chelub the brother of Shuah begat Mehir}* See v. 4.

*{which was the father of Eshton}* Which has reference to Mehir.

**V. 12.** *{And Eshton begat Beth-rapha}* Eshton is taken to be the name both of a person and a place. So is Beth-rapha.

*{and Paseah, and Tehinnah the father of Ir-nahash}* Or, *the city of Nahash*. The word Ir-nahash being taken for a city, father must be taken for founder or governor; and the city put for the inhabitants as well as for the place.

*{These are the men of Rechah}* Rechah is here taken for a city.

**V. 13.** *{And the sons of Kenaz}* Some include the last clause of v. 11 and the whole 12th verse in a parenthesis, *q.d.*, *Chelub begat Mehir, and the sons of Kenaz*. This is the Kenaz mentioned Josh. 15:17. These were the grandchildren of Kenaz, but educated by him as by a father.

*{Othniel, and Seraiah}* Othniel was the first judge after Joshua, Judg. 3:9, 10.

*{and the sons of Othniel, Hathath}* Or, *Hathath, and Meonothai*, who begat, etc.
