**V. 13.** *{And Meonothai begat Ophrah}* See v. 8.

*{and Seraiah begat Joab, the father of the valley}* Or, *inhabitants of the valley*. This valley was in the limits of Judah and Benjamin, Neh. 11:35.

*{of Charashim}* I.e., craftsmen.

*{for they were craftsmen}* This is the reason why the valley had that name. Craftsmen of all sorts dwelt therein.

**V. 15.** *{And the sons of Caleb}* See ch. 2:50. This was that faithful valiant champion that entered into the land of promise, Num. 14:24 and 32:12; Josh. 14:6, etc.

*{the son of Jephunneh}* Num. 13:6. Some make Jephunneh to be the son of Kenaz, in that Caleb his son is called a Kenezite, Num. 32:12; Josh. 14:6.

*{Iru, Elah, and Naam}* No more mention is made of Iru and Naam.

*{and the sons of Elah, even Kenaz}* The particle translated *even* is the copulative commonly translated *and*. Some in relation to Kenaz, v. 13, translate it *second*, thus, *The second, Kenaz*. Others make it part of the name and translate it *Uknaz*, or *Ukenaz*.

**V. 16.** *{And the sons of Jehaleleel}* The manner of bringing in Jehaleleel implies he was the son of Kenaz last mentioned.

*{Ziph}* See ch. 2:42.

*{and Ziphah, Tiria, and Asareel}* There is no more of these.

**V. 17.** *{And the sons of Ezra}* The last letter of this man’s name differs from the learned scribe’s, Ezra 7:1. Besides they differed in their tribes, this being of Judah, the other of Levi; and in the age of the world wherein they lived.

*{were Jether}* See ch. 2:17, 32.

*{and Mered}* Again mentioned v. 18.

*{and Epher}* There was another of this name, ch. 5:24.

*{and Jalon}* There is no more mention of him.

*{and she bare}* Some translate it in the masculine gender, *he begat*, and refer it to Jalon: but the Hebrew is feminine, and in that respect well translated, *she bare*. But because the woman is not expressly set down, some refer it to the wife of Ezra, intimating that Ezra had two wives, and that the latter of them is here meant. Others more fitly apply this to Bithiah, v. 18.

*{Miriam}* This is the name of a woman, Exod. 15:20. But here of a man.

*{and Shammai}* There were two others of this name, ch. 2:28, 44.

*{and Ishbah the father of Eshtemoa}* I.e., the progenitor or governor of the inhabitants of Eshtemoa, Josh. 21:14.

**V. 18.** *{And his wife Jehudijah}* Jehudijah either was her proper name, or may be taken appellatively, to signify that she was a *Jewess*. His other wife Bithiah was an Egyptian.

*{bare Jered the father of Gedor}* I.e., of the inhabitants of Gedor, Josh. 15:58.

*{and Heber the father of Socho, and Jekuthiel the father of Zanoah}* Socho and Zanoah were cities of Judah, Josh. 15:34, 35.

*{And these are the sons of Bithiah}* This relative these, or those, has reference to Miriam, Shammai and Ishbah, v. 17. Mered had them by Bithiah; but he had Jered, Heber and Jekuthiel by his other wife.

*{the daughter of Pharaoh, which Mered took}* It seems Bithiah was an Egyptian, and of the royal stock: and Mered was some great man, seeing he married so great a king’s daughter.

**V. 19.** *{And the sons of his wife Hodiah}* Or, *Jehudijah*, v. 18. She may also be taken for Mered’s third wife.

*{the sister of Naham}* Though no other mention be made of Naham, yet it appears he was a man of note, in that Mered’s wife is set out by her relation to him.

*{the father of Keilah}* Josh. 15:44. This was that city which David saved from the Philistims, 1 Sam. 23:1, 5.

*{the Garmite}* The name of a family, having reference to Naham.

*{and Eshtemoa}* This word is frequently used for a city, Josh. 21:14; 1 Sam. 30:28; ch. 6:57. See v. 17. But it may be also taken for the man of whom that city was named.

*{the Maachathite}* The name of a family, 2 Sam. 23:34; 2 Kings 25:23; Jer. 40:8. Some of those also whom the Israelites expelled beyond Jordan bore this name, Josh. 12:5 and 13:11, 13.

**V. 20.** *{And the sons of Shimon were}* This is not Simeon the second son of Jacob, Gen. 29:33. For he is mentioned v. 24. Neither do the Hebrew letters in this and that name agree.

*{Amnon}* This was also David’s eldest son’s name, ch. 3:1. We read of no other besides these two of this name; and of this man no more.

*{and Rinnah, Ben-hanan, and Tilon}* These names also are only this once mentioned. *Ben* is a Hebrew word signifying *son*; so as *Ben-hanan* may be translated *the son of Hanan*.

*{And the sons of Ishi were}* Ishi being not before mentioned, some take it to be another name given to one of the forementioned sons of Shimon. Others take him to be the son of Tilon: but no such relation is here expressed.

*{Zoheth, and Ben-zoheth}* Or, *the son of Zoheth*.

**V. 21.** *{The sons of Shelah the son of Judah, were}* Of Shelah Judah’s third son, Gen. 38:5, came the family of the Shelanites, Num. 26:20.

*{Er the father of Lecah}* Er had an uncle of this name, Gen. 38:3. One of Christ’s progenitors was also called Er, Luke 3:28.

*{and Laadah the father of Mareshah}* One of Caleb’s posterity was of this name, ch. 2:42. So was also a city of Judah, Josh. 15:44; 2 Chr. 11:8 and 14:9 and 20:37.

*{and the families of the house of them that wrought fine linen}* This fine linen was made of flax brought out of Egypt, which is said to be as fine as silk: and in that respect these are styled silk-weavers.

*{of the house of Ashbea}* This is added to express in particular from whom those families descended.

**V. 22.** *{And Jokim, and the men of Chozeba}* Called Chezib, Gen. 38:5, and Achzib, Josh. 15:44.

*{and Joash, and Saraph, who had the dominion in Moab}* When as the Moabites were tributary to the Israelites, these were made commanders in Moab, there to rule and govern.

*{and Jashubi-lehem. And these are ancient things}* Or, *things waxed old and out of memory*.

**V. 23.** *{These were the potters}* Exercised in making earthen vessels.

*{and those that dwelt among plants and hedges}* Exercised in all manner of husbandry, as planting gardens, tilling fields, fencing them with hedges, and such like inferior works. This is added as a reason to show how the things concerning them come to be ancient and worn out, because the posterity of Shelah having little respect to the dignity and inheritance of their predecessors, contented themselves with mean callings.

*{There they dwelt with the king for his work}* Some refer there to Moab, v. 22, as if they had been contented to abide in Moab after the Moabites had recovered the supreme government to themselves, and to be serviceable to the king of Moab about his works. Others apply it to the land of Chaldea, as if this had been written of the time of Judah’s return from captivity: and so the words intend thus much, That they were so basely content with the servile works that they did to the kings of Babylon, as there they would abide and continue in their callings still doing service to the king under whom they lived; and cared not to return to Judea with the people of God. Others apply it to the land of Judah, where the people then were, and by king mean the king of Judah, to whom the forementioned persons did good service about such works as he appointed them.

**V. 24.** *{The sons of Simeon were}* A curse being laid upon Simeon, that he with his brother Levi should be divided in Jacob, and scattered in Israel, Gen. 49:7, he had no distinct inheritance of his own, as most of the other tribes had, but his lot fell out to be in the tribe of Judah, Josh. 19:2. Therefore his genealogy is reckoned with Judah. Besides, he being the second son of Jacob, ch. 2:1, and Judah put into Reuben’s place, Simeon’s genealogy is here set down next after Judah’s.

*{Nemuel}* Or, *Jemuel*.

*{and Jamin}* These two might be by one mother, and therefore joined together by the copulative and.

*{Jarib}* This might also be called Ohad, or Jachin, Gen. 46:10.

*{Zerah}* Or, *Zohar*, Exod. 6:15.

*{and Shaul}* This man is said to be *the son of a Canaanitish woman*, Gen. 46:10, yet might she be a proselyte, and profess the true faith. The variety of names is manifested by comparing Gen. 46:10 and Exod. 6:15 with this verse. In the two former places mention is made of six sons of Simeon, but here of five only. The reason of which difference may be that Moses sets down all the sons that Simeon had, but Ezra only such as became heads of families. Probably Ohad, or Jachin, died childless, and in that respect came not into this genealogy. See on Num. 26:12.

**V. 25.** *{Shallum his son}* There were many of this name in other tribes.

*{Mibsam his son}* Ishmael also had a son of this name, ch. 1:29.

*{Mishma his son}* This man’s posterity follows, v. 26.

**V. 26.** *{And the sons of Mishma, Hamuel his son, Zacchur his son}* No genealogies of these are mentioned.

*{Shimei his son}* This man’s pedigree follows.

**V. 27.** *{And Shimei had sixteen sons and six daughters}* This man had the most children of any mentioned in these genealogies. In other histories we read of many that had more. *Ibzan had thirty sons and thirty daughters*. *Abdon had forty sons and thirty sons’ sons*, Judg. 12:9, 14. Gideon had *threescore and ten sons of his body begotten*, Judg. 8:30.

*{but his brethren had not many children}* This restraint amplifies God’s blessing on him.

*{neither did all their family multiply}* This clause has reference to the former, and implies that some of those that had children did not much increase the tribe, because they did not much multiply, either in regard of their own children, or in regard of such generations as might have followed them.

*{like to the children of Judah}* Heb. *unto the children*. This implies a mighty increase of the posterity of Judah, whereunto that of Simeon was not comparable. In this respect a part of Judah’s inheritance was sufficient for Simeon, Josh. 19:1, 9.

**V. 28.** *{And they dwelt at Beer-sheba}* *Sheba* signifies *an oath*; and *Beer*, *a well*. At this place Abimelech and Abraham bound themselves by oath to keep covenant each with other: there also was *a well*. In these respects the place was called sometimes Sheba, sometimes Beer-sheba, Gen. 21:23, 25, 31 and 26:33; Josh. 9:12.

*{and Moladah, and Hazar-shual}* Josh. 19:2, 3.

**V. 29.** *{And at Bilhah}* Or, *Balah*, Josh. 19:3.

*{and at Ezem, and at Tolad}* Or, *Azem, and Eltolad*, Josh. 19:4.

**V. 30.** *{And at Bethuel}* Or, *Bethel*, Josh. 19:4.

*{and at Hormah}* See Num. 14:45 and 21:3.

*{and at Ziklag}* This city was within the inheritance of Judah, Josh. 15:35. But after the Philistims had held it a long while, Achish their king gave it to David, 1 Sam. 27:6, and so it pertained to the kings of Judah; yet allotted to Simeon.

**V. 31.** *{And at Beth-marcaboth, and Hazar-Susim}* Or, *Hazar-Susah*, Josh. 10:5.

*{and at Beth-birei}* Or, *Beth-lebaoth*, Josh. 19:6.

*{and at Shaaraim}* Or, *Sharuhen*, Josh. 19:6. Thirteen cities in all.

*{These were their cities unto the reign of David}* This phrase properly taken signifies that from Joshua’s time even till the kingdom was settled in David the forementioned cities belonged to the Simeonites. But tropically the reign of David may be applied to the continuance of the kingdom in the stock of David even to the Babylonian captivity.

**V. 32.** *{And their villages were Etam}* Or, *Ether*, Josh. 19:7. This was in the tribe of Simeon.

*{and Ain, Rimmon, and Tochen}* Tochen is not mentioned Josh. 19.

*{and Ashan, five cities}* The places called before villages are here called cities: they are villages appertaining to cities. Some make this difference betwixt cities and villages: the cities were walled, the villages unwalled. The cities also were larger than villages; and the principal officers were in the cities, under whose government the villages were.

**V. 33.** *{And all their villages were round about the same cities}* One city might have many villages appertaining to it.

*{unto Baal}* Or, *Baalath-Beer*, Josh. 19:8.

*{These were their habitations}* To wit, the cities and villages mentioned from v. 28 to this place.

*{and their genealogy}* Or, *as they divided themselves by nations among them*. The former reading, their genealogy, has reference to the persons reckoned up v. 24, 25, 26, 27; the latter reading has reference to the places before mentioned. A genealogy is a catalogue or rehearsal of sundry persons that by lineal descent have proceeded one after another from the same stock or father.

**V. 34.** *{And Meshobab, etc.}* The persons reckoned up from hence to v. 38 were all of them of the posterity of Simeon, and men of renown in their days.

**V. 38.** *{These mentioned}* Heb. *coming*.

*{by their names}* They are said to come by names whose names are registered in public records; by which means they do oft as it were come forth into the world.

*{were princes in their families}* Heads of their families, and famous therein.

*{and the house of their fathers increased greatly}* They increased in a numerous posterity by that blessing and increase which God gave to them. Yea, the fame and honor of their father’s house greatly increased by their honorable enterprises and valorous acts.

**V. 39.** *{And they went}* By reason of their multitude they dispersed themselves.

*{to the entrance of Gedor}* Some by Gedor understand those places which are called Gederah and Gederothaim, Josh. 15:36.

*{even unto the east side of the valley}* The valley here meant is that where David slew Goliath. It reached from Ephesdammim to the gates of Ekron, 1 Sam. 17:1, 52.

*{to seek pasture for their flocks}* It seems they had store of cattle, but want of good pasture.

**V. 40.** *{And they found fat pasture and good}* This is one occasion why they went thither.

*{and the land was wide}* It had room enough for all their cattle.

*{and quiet and peaceable}* Secure and careless, having no enemy about them to disquiet them, or to make them think of any preparations for war. They were like to Laish, Judg. 18:7, 27, 28.

*{for they of Ham had dwelt there of old}* This is a third reason, and the chiefest of all, that moved the Simeonites to enter upon those lands; they had been invaded and possessed by the posterity of Cham, who was accursed, Gen. 9:25.

**V. 41.** *{And these written by name}* Those mentioned v. 34, etc.

*{came in the days of Hezekiah king of Judah}* The tribe of Simeon being within the inheritance of Judah, might continue in their estate after Israel was carried away captive by the Assyrians; and so in the days of Hezekiah seek to enlarge their habitation. See 1 Kings 11:13.

*{and smote their tents, and the habitations that were found there}* These two words tents and habitations are here used to show what kind of dwellings they had; namely tents, such as the Arabians use. And tents are the fittest habitations for such as spend their time in feeding cattle, being easy to remove when there is occasion to seek out fresh pasture.

*{and destroyed them utterly}* This they did the rather because they were of that stock which was by God himself devoted to destruction.

*{unto this day}* See 1 Kings 9:13 and 12:19.

*{and dwelt in their rooms}* As the rest of the children of Israel had before this dwelt in the room of other Canaanites.

*{because there was pasture there for their flocks}* See v. 39.

**V. 42.** *{And some of them, even of the sons of Simeon}* Here are set down other valiant exploits done by the men of that small tribe.

*{five hundred men}* Though these singly and simply considered were a great multitude, yet for so great an adventure as they undertook they were but few.

*{went to mount Seir}* The country of the Amalekites bordering on mount Seir, these Simeonites went thither against them. See Exod. 17:8, etc.; Deut. 25:17, 18.

*{having for their captains, Pelatiah, and Neariah, and Rephaiah, and Uzziel, the sons of Ishi}* Ishi being a famous man in his tribe (as others of his name, v. 20; ch. 2:31, were in theirs) for honor’s sake is here named. And it was a great honor to have such sons.

**V. 43.** *{And they smote the rest of the Amalekites that were escaped}* Joshua overthrew an army of them, Exod. 17:13. Saul destroyed others, 1 Sam. 15:7, and David more, 1 Sam. 30:17; 2 Sam. 8:12. The posterity of them which escaped those destructions were now destroyed by these Simeonites. Thus was the judgment threatened Exod. 17:14 accomplished. See Esth. 3:1.

*{and dwelt there}* Where these Amalekites dwelt before.

*{unto this day}* See 1 Kings 9:13 and 12:19.

## CHAPTER 5

**Verse 1.** *{Now the sons of Reuben the firstborn of Israel}* Here the genealogies of those that had their inheritance beyond Jordan is set down; and Reuben’s first, because he was the eldest.

*{for he was the firstborn}* Gen. 29:32.

*{but forasmuch as he defiled his father’s bed}* Gen. 35:22 and 49:4. This was one of the highest degrees of incest, like that 1 Cor. 5:1, etc. See Lev. 18:8.

*{his birthright was given}* There being among other two especial privileges of birthright, dignity, Gen. 40:7, and inheritance, Deut. 21:17, both these were given away from Reuben.

*{unto the sons of Joseph the son of Israel}* Joseph was the youngest son save one that Jacob had, but the first son of that wife whom he first agreed to have, Gen. 29:18 and 30:23, 24. Therefore upon Reuben’s forfeiture of his birthright Jacob takes occasion to confer a principal part thereof, which was the double portion, upon Joseph. It is said to be given unto his sons, because they made two tribes, Ephraim and Manasseh, Gen. 48:5 and 49:22; Num. 1:10, whereas the other sons of Jacob made but one tribe apiece. Besides, the posterity of Ephraim was the most numerous of all except Judah. This title, son of Israel, is as it were appropriated unto Joseph, because he was most beloved of him, Gen. 37:3.

*{and the genealogy is not to be reckoned after the birthright}* Of genealogy see ch. 4:33. This is here set down either as a reason why Reuben’s genealogy is set before Joseph’s, who had the birthright; namely, Because the genealogy is not to be reckoned after the birthright: or as a reason why Judah’s is set before Reuben’s and Joseph’s too, even because he had a greater dignity; and the genealogy is not to be reckoned according to that right of a double portion which belongs to the firstborn.

**V. 2.** *{For Judah prevailed above his brethren}* He prevailed in a prime dignity, which by divine instinct was conferred upon him in his father’s last will, Gen. 49:8, 9, 10. He had also a preeminence in honor, even in the wilderness, Num. 2:3, and ever after. He had also the royal dignity from David’s time to the captivity. After that, some of the chief governors of the Jews, and their great senate, were of that tribe, till Christ the everlasting king, who sprang out of it, came.

*{and of him came the chief ruler}* Or, *prince*. Though this may have reference to David and the royal stock that descended from him, yet principally it points at Christ, Mic. 5:2; Matt. 2:6.

*{but the birthright was Joseph’s}* See v. 1.

**V. 3.** *{The sons, I say, of Reuben, the firstborn of Israel}* This repetition of the first clause of the first verse shows that all from that clause to this is to be included in a parenthesis.

*{Hanoch, and Pallu, Hezron, and Carmi}* See Gen. 46:9; Exod. 6:14; Num. 26:5, 6, 7.

**V. 4.** *{The sons of Joel}* He is supposed to be the son of Hanoch.

*{Shemaiah his son, etc.}* These seven that follow were princes in the tribe of Reuben, one after another, till the captivity.

**V. 6.** *{whom Tilgath-Pilneser}* Or, *Tiglath-Pileser*, 2 Kings 15:29 and 16:7.

*{king of Assyria}* At that time the king of Assyria was a mighty monarch, and had subdued many nations.

*{carried away captive}* They on that other side Jordan were carried away captive before their brethren on this side; they by Tiglath-Pileser, 2 Kings 15:29, these by Shalmaneser his son, 2 Kings 17:3, 6. See v. 26.

*{he was prince of the Reubenites}* Because Beerah is here styled prince of the Reubenites, it is inferred that Joel, v. 4, his prime progenitor was the son of Hanoch, who was a prince, being the eldest son of Reuben, v. 3.

**V. 7.** *{And his brethren by their families}* The brethren here meant are Pallu, Hezron, and Carmi, sons of Reuben, and brethren to Hanoch.

*{when the genealogy of their generation was reckoned}* Of genealogy see ch. 4:33. There is mention made of reckoning genealogies in the days of Jotham, v. 17, but many refer this reckoning to the time of the captivity.

*{were the chief}* This has reference to the first clause of this verse, which if it be thus read, *of his brethren*, then the sense is, *Of or among the chief of them were they who are hereafter named*.

*{Jeiel, and Zechariah}* Jeiel was the chief, and Zechariah the next.

**V. 8.** *{And Bela the son of Azaz}* He might be the third in order.

*{the son of Shema}* Or, *Shemaiah*, v. 4.

*{the son of Joel}* See v. 4.

*{who dwelt in Aroer}* Aroer is said to be built by the children of Gad, Num. 32:34. It was at the first the inheritance of the Amorites, Josh. 12:2, and lay betwixt that part which fell to Reuben and that which fell to Gad, Josh. 13:15, 16, 24, 25. It is here implied that Bela and his posterity, or in general the posterity of Reuben, dwelt there.

*{even unto Nebo, and Baal-meon}* Two cities in the inheritance of Reuben, and built by him, Num. 32:38.

**V. 9.** *{And eastward he inhabited}* This is meant of Reuben’s posterity in the days of Saul, v. 10.

*{unto the entering in of the wilderness from the river Euphrates}* This land lay betwixt Euphrates and Jordan, and it was very fertile, very good pastureland.

*{because their cattle were multiplied in the land of Gilead}* This is rendered as a reason why they chose that place, though it were on the other side of Jordan. It is requisite that they who have much cattle should have store of good pasture for them. Of Gilead see ch. 2:21.

**V. 10.** *{And in the days of Saul}* At that time they were much increased in number.

*{they made war}* This which is here in particular said of the Reubenites is attributed to the Gadites and Manassites together with them, v. 18, 19.

*{with the Hagarites}* These were of the posterity of Ishmael, called Hagarites from Hagar the mother of Ishmael, Gen. 16:2, 15.

*{who fell by their hand: and they dwelt in their tents}* The Hagarites keeping much cattle, had such habitations as they might remove from place to place, according to the pasture which they chose here and there for their cattle.

*{throughout all the east land}* Heb. *upon all the face of the east*.

*{of Gilead}* I.e., which did border upon the east of Gilead.

**V. 11.** *{And the children of Gad dwelt over against them}* Or, *bordered next unto them*. See Num. 32:1, etc.

*{in the land of Bashan}* Bashan appertained to Og, Num. 21:33. It is sometimes taken for a country containing sundry cities and store of land, Josh. 13:30, 31. Sometimes for a fair city, Josh. 17:1. The tribe of Gad possessed but part of Bashan, the other part thereof being given to Manasseh, Josh. 13:29, 30; 1 Kings 4:13.

*{unto Salchah}* A city in the kingdom of Og, Deut. 3:10.

**V. 12.** *{Joel the chief, and Shapham the next}* These were found to be chief princes of the tribe of Gad, when their genealogies were reckoned up.

*{and Jaanai and Shaphat in Bashan}* These are said to be in Bashan, because they were there left to defend their inheritance while their brethren went forth to war. See ch. 27:29.

**V. 13.** *{And their brethren of the house of their fathers}* By these brethren are meant such as were next in dignity to those before mentioned.

*{of the house of their fathers}* Heads of their fathers’ family.

*{Michael, etc.}* These seven were those brethren before mentioned.

**V. 14.** *{These are the children of Abihail}* I.e., those seven, v. 13.

*{the son of Huri, etc.}* The eight were heads of families in the tribe of Gad.

**V. 15.** *{Ahi the son of Abdiel, the son of Guni, chief of the house of their fathers}* Some include the 14th verse in a parenthesis, and refer this verse to the thirteenth, making Ahi the chief over all those mentioned v. 13 and general of the army, v. 18.

**V. 16.** *{And they dwelt in Gilead in Bashan, and in her towns}* Heb. *daughters*. See ch. 7:28. This has reference to Bashan.

*{and in all the suburbs of Sharon}* See ch. 27:29.

*{upon their borders}* Heb. *their goings forth*.

**V. 17.** *{All these were reckoned by genealogies}* The Jews were careful in reserving catalogues of their ancestors, whereby they made genealogies of many generations.

*{in the days of Jotham king of Judah}* To wit, when he governed the kingdom for his father, 2 Kings 15:5. For he began not to reign as sole king till the second year of Pekah, 2 Kings 15:32.

*{and in the days of Jeroboam king of Israel}* To wit, Jeroboam the second. And it must needs be in the latter end of his reign, 2 Kings 15:1.

**V. 18.** *{The sons of Reuben, and the Gadites, and half the tribe of Manasseh}* Num. 32:33.

*{of valiant men}* Heb. *sons of valor*. This Hebraism has an emphasis in it, and implies that they were courageous even from their youth, and trained up to warlike exploits.

*{men able to bear buckler and sword}* I.e., to use warlike weapons against their enemies.

*{and to shoot with bow}* Bows in those days were of great use against enemies. See Gen. 48:22; Deut. 32:23, 42; Josh. 24:12; 1 Sam. 31:3; 2 Sam. 1:18, 22; 1 Kings 22:34; 2 Kings 9:24; 1 Chr. 12:2; 2 Chr. 35:23; Ps. 44:6 and 46:9 and 78:9; Isa. 22:6 and 66:19; Jer. 49:35; Zech. 10:4.

*{and skilful in war}* They had been trained up in warlike exercises, and oft went out to war, and thereby gathered great skill.

*{were four and forty thousand seven hundred and threescore}* These made a great host: but see a far greater out of two tribes only, 2 Chr. 13:3.

*{that went out to the war}* That were ready on all occasions to set upon the enemy.

**V. 19.** *{And they made war with the Hagarites}* See v. 10.

*{with Jetur, and Nephish, and Nodab}* Jetur and Nephish were sons of Ishmael, Gen. 25:15, and Nodab also of his posterity. We read, v. 10, that the Reubenites subdued such of the Hagarites as bordered upon their tribe: here are meant such as bordered on Gad and Manasseh.

**V. 20.** *{And they were helped against them}* By God.

*{and the Hagarites were delivered into their hand}* So as they either destroyed them or took them captives.

*{and all that were with them}* It seems the Hagarites got other nations to assist them: but they prevailed nothing thereby; for all were vanquished.

*{for they cried unto God in the battle}* I.e., the Israelites. This is not to be restrained to the very time when they were in fight (in which case Jehoshaphat cried, 1 Kings 22:32), but also to be extended to their preparation unto the battle. In which case Asa cried, 2 Chr. 14:11.

*{and he was intreated of them}* God is ready to hear prayer, Ps. 65:2. See ch. 4:10.

*{because they put their trust in him}* Faith is to prayer as fire to powder.

**V. 21.** *{And they took away}* Heb. *led captive*.

*{their cattle}* Having vanquished the enemy they took their cattle. The words following show how many.

*{of their camels fifty thousand}* See 1 Kings 10:2.

*{and of sheep two hundred and fifty thousand}* Sheep are very profitable, by their wool, flesh, and breed: therefore there was far the greatest number of them.

*{and of asses two thousand}* Asses of old were of great use for carrying both persons and burdens, Gen. 49:14; Exod. 4:20; Judg. 5:10 and 10:4 and 12:14.

*{and of men}* Heb. *souls of men*, as Num. 31:35. See Gen. 46:26.

*{an hundred thousand}* More than double the number of them that took them, v. 18.

**V. 22.** *{For there fell down many slain}* This much amplifies the success, that besides the number of captives so many were slain.

*{because the war was of God}* *If God be with us, who can be against us?* Rom. 8:31.

*{And they dwelt in their steads}* They dwelt with their enemies, as their ancestors in Joshua’s time dwelt with theirs, Josh. 23:12, 13.

*{until the captivity}* I.e., the first captivity, 2 Kings 15:29 and 17:6.

**V. 23.** *{And the children of the half-tribe of Manasseh dwelt in the land}* This is added as a reason why this half tribe joined with Reuben and Gad against the Hagarites, v. 18, 19.

*{they increased from Bashan}* See v. 11. The greatest part of Bashan was given to the half-tribe of Manasseh, Josh. 17:1.

*{unto Baal-hermon, and Senir, and unto mount Hermon}* These three places were their uttermost bounds, all near to Lebanon. Of Baal-hermon see Judg. 3:3. Of Senir, Ezek. 27:5. Of mount Hermon, Deut. 4:48.

**V. 24.** *{And these were the heads of the house of their fathers}* They were such as had distinct stocks proceeding from them, and were princes or chief men of their families.

*{even Epher, etc.}* Seven in all. There was the like number of princes in the tribe of Gad, v. 13.

*{mighty men of valour}* Such as had courage, strength, and skill.

*{famous men}* Heb. *men of names*. They had given such evidences of their valor as they were highly esteemed by all.

*{and heads of the house of their fathers}* Because this was a matter of great honor, it is again repeated.

**V. 25.** *{And they transgressed}* Their honor and valor was so far from making them thankful, as it puffed them up and made them bold in sinning.

*{against the God of their fathers}* By fathers Abraham, Isaac and Jacob are especially intended, to whom God made many promises, and extended those promises to their seed, Gen. 15:5 and 17:7 and 26:4 and 35:11, 12.

*{and went a whoring after the gods}* See 2 Kings 9:22.

*{of the people of the land}* I.e., the heathen round about them.

*{whom God destroyed before them}* By this it appears that the people before mentioned were especially the remainders of those cursed Canaanites whom Joshua destroyed.

**V. 26.** *{And the God of Israel stirred up the spirit}* See on 2 Sam. 24:1. Ambition and other evil causes stirred up their rage, but God ordered it to the correction of his people.

*{of Pul king of Assyria}* See 2 Kings 15:19.

*{and the spirit of Tilgath-Pilneser king of Assyria}* See 2 Kings 15:22. Pul first troubled the land, and after his son Tilgath-Pilneser subdued it.

*{and he carried them away, even the Reubenites, and the Gadites, and the half-tribe of Manasseh}* He subdued and carried away captive all the ten tribes, 2 Kings 18:11, but he first began with these on the other side Jordan, 2 Kings 15:29. See on Num. 32:22.

*{and brought them unto Halah, and Habor}* 2 Kings 17:6.

*{and Hara}* This was near the other places, in the land of Media.

*{and to the river Gozan}* See 2 Kings 17:6.

*{unto this day}* See 1 Kings 9:13.

## CHAPTER 6

**Verse 1.** *{The sons of Levi}* Jacob’s third son, ch. 2:1. The firstborn were of old governors, priests and prophets in their several families. But when the church was become a nation, God chose the tribe of Levi to supply the priest’s office all the time the polity of the Jews remained, Num. 1:50, etc. And that there might be enough both to attend the services of the Lord’s house, and also to instruct the people throughout all the tribes, God did wonderfully increase this tribe. Concerning these Levites three things are set down in this chapter: I. Their genealogy, to v. 31. II. Their particular services, to v. 54. III. Their habitations, to the end.

*{Gershon}* Or, *Gershom*, v. 16. This was also the name of Moses his son, and signifies *a stranger*, Exod. 2:22.

*{Kohath}* This name signifies *a congregation*. And the Levites were to congregate the people together.

*{and Merari}* There is bitterness in this name and provocation. It may be that by reason of that cruel fact which Levi committed, Gen. 34:25, this name was given to his son. These three were heads of the families of the Levites, Num. 3:21, 27, 33.

**V. 2.** *{And the sons of Kohath}* From Kohath the second son descended the high priests, therefore his genealogy is set down first.

*{Amram, Izhar}* Izahar is called Aminadab, v. 22.

*{and Hebron, and Uzziel}* The four sons of Kohath were born in Egypt: for they did not go with Jacob thither, Gen. 46:11.

**V. 3.** *{And the children of Amram, Aaron}* The priesthood was settled on Aaron and his sons, Exod. 29:44.

*{and Moses}* Though Moses were the younger brother, yet God gave him a dignity above Aaron, Exod. 4:16 and 7:1. He was also a prince and governor over all the Israelites, Acts 7:35, and by him did God give all his laws to his people.

*{and Miriam}* She was sister to Aaron and Moses, and elder than they. She is supposed to be that maid that brought Moses his mother to be his nurse, Exod. 2:7, 8. She was a prophetess, Exod. 15:20; Mic. 6:4.

*{The sons also of Aaron, Nadab, and Abihu}* These two were Aaron’s eldest sons, but they died childless, Lev. 10:1, 2.

*{Eleazar}* His two elder brothers being dead, the privilege of the firstborn descended upon him, and he succeeded his father in the chief priesthood, Num. 20:28. He entered upon this office in the wilderness, and held it all the while that Joshua governed Israel, Josh. 24:33.

*{and Ithamar}* Eli, who descended from Ithamar, was high priest; but by what right is not expressed. That Eli descended from Ithamar is evident, in that Ahimelech his grandchild is said to be of the sons of Ithamar, ch. 24:3. Though Eli and his posterity, namely Ahitub, Ahiah (who also was called Ahimelech), and Abiathar, exercised the priesthood, 1 Sam. 14:3 and 22:29, yet because they descended not from Eleazar, they are not put into this catalogue of high priests.

**V. 4.** *{Eleazar begat Phinehas}* Phinehas executed the office of high priest in the beginning of the judges’ time, Judg. 20:28, which may by many arguments be proved to be about that time when the Israelites first turned to idolatry, Judg. 2:11. As the right of priesthood belonged to Phinehas and his posterity, because he was the son of Eleazar, so it was further confirmed unto him, upon his zeal in executing just vengeance upon Zimri and Cozbi, Num. 25:11, etc.

*{Phinehas begat Abishua, etc.}* Abishua and the six other that follow unto Zadok, v. 8, namely, Bukki, Uzzi, Zerahiah (who is called Zeraiah, Ezra 7:4), Meraioth, Amariah, and Ahitub, had right to the priesthood, and are set down as succeeding one another, here, and v. 50, 51, 52, and Ezra 7:2, etc. Yet nothing in particular is in Scripture set down concerning anything that any of them did, or concerning the time wherein they lived.

**V. 8.** *{And Ahitub begat Zadok, etc.}* See another Ahitub, 1 Sam. 14:3.

**V. 9.** *{And Ahimaaz begat Azariah}* This Ahimaaz was very faithful and serviceable to David, even while his father Zadok lived, 2 Sam. 17:17 and 18:19, etc.

**V. 10.** *{And Johanan begat Azariah}* This man’s grandfather was of the same name, v. 9.

*{he it is that executed the priest’s office}* This relative he may have reference either to Johanan or Azariah. If to the former, it points to that worthy act which Jehoiada did in putting down Athaliah, and setting up King Joash. This was done in the temple, 2 Chr. 23:10, etc. But if to the latter, it has reference to that courage which Azariah showed in thrusting King Uzziah out of the temple, 2 Chr. 26:16, etc. For Azariah is there expressly named.

*{in the temple}* Heb. *in the house*. I.e., the house of the Lord.

*{that Solomon built in Jerusalem}* 1 Kings 6:1, etc.; 2 Chr. 3:1, etc. This is added because when this book was written another temple was built by Zerubbabel, Zech. 4:9.

**V. 11.** *{And Azariah begat Amariah, etc.}* Amariah and the rest that follow to Hilkiah, namely, Ahitub, Zadok, and Shallum (or Meshullam, ch. 9:11), succeeded one another in the high priest’s office; but nothing is registered of anything they did. Ahitub and Zadok carried some of their predecessors’ names, v. 8.

**V. 13.** *{And Shallum begat Hilkiah}* That good priest, 2 Kings 22:8.

*{and Hilkiah begat Azariah}* Two of his predecessors had this name Azariah, v. 6, 10.

**V. 14.** *{And Azariah begat Seraiah, etc.}* Seraiah was priest when Nebuchadnezzar carried the Jews away captive, and among others was slain, 2 Kings 25:18, etc. Though he himself were a wicked man, yet he had a very good son, Ezra the scribe. Which shows either that Ezra was but young at the beginning of the captivity, or else very old when he returned to Jerusalem, Ezra 7:1. Or it may be that Ezra was Seraiah’s son’s son. See Ezra 7:1.

**V. 15.** *{And Jehozadak went into captivity}* Seraiah being slain, his son was carried away captive.

*{when the LORD carried away}* Because the Lord made Nebuchadnezzar his instrument to punish his people, what he did, the Lord is said to do.

*{Judah and Jerusalem}* The inhabitants of both country and city.

*{by the hand of Nebuchadnezzar}* This man was at that time the great monarch of the world, Jer. 27:6.

**V. 16.** *{The sons of Levi, etc.}* See v. 1. Having been long in setting forth the posterity of Aaron, he repeats this general again, the rather because he proceeds to set down the genealogy of others besides the high priests.

**V. 17.** *{And these be the names of the sons of Gershom}* Gershom was Levi’s eldest son: therefore the stock of the high priest being set down, he next adds the posterity of Gershom.

*{Libni}* See his genealogy, v. 20, etc.

*{and Shimei}* See on v. 20.

**V. 18.** *{And the sons of Kohath were Amram, etc.}* These four were mentioned before v. 2, but because he sets down other genealogies of Kohath, he repeats these heads of families again.

**V. 19.** *{The sons of Merari, Mahli, and Mushi}* Num. 3:33.

*{And these are the families of the Levites according to their fathers}* This has relation to the three sons of Levi, Gershom, Kohath, Merari: for all of them were heads of families: and families were distinguished by their names, as Libnites, Shimites, Amramites, Mahlites, etc., Num. 3:21, 27, 33.

**V. 20.** *{Of Gershom, Libni his son}* Or, *Concerning Gershom, Libni was his son*.

*{Jahath his son}* This man with the five following, Zimmah, Joah, Iddo, Zerah, Jeaterai, were all heads of families among the posterity of Gershom.

*{Zimmah his son}* Zimmah was Jahath’s grandchild: for he was the son of Shimei the son of Jahath, v. 42, 43.

**V. 21.** *{Joah}* Or, *Ethan*, v. 42.

*{Iddo}* Or, *Adaiah*, v. 41.

*{Jeaterai}* Or, *Ethani*, v. 41.

**V. 22.** *{The sons of Kohath}* See v. 1, 2.

*{Amminadab his son}* Or, *Izhar*, v. 2, 18. Here is reckoned up another posterity of Kohath, not of the line of the high priests, which are set down, v. 3, etc.

*{Korah his son}* This was that Korah mentioned Num. 16.

*{Assir his son}* Assir here is a proper name. See ch. 3:17.

**V. 23.** *{Elkanah his son}* In this genealogy there are two other Elkanahs, v. 25, 26.

*{and Ebiasaph his son}* These three, Assir, Elkanah, and Ebiasaph, are said to be *the families of the Korhites*, Exod. 5:24. See ch. 9:19.

*{and Assir his son}* Here again Assir is taken for a proper name, as v. 22. For children and children’s children used oft to bear the names of their predecessors. See Luke 1:61.

**V. 24.** *{Tahath his son, Uriel his son}* This Uriel is called Zephaniah, v. 36.

*{Uzziah his son}* Or, *Azariah*, v. 31. These two names Uzziah and Azariah were also given to a king, 2 Kings 15:1, 13.

*{and Shaul his son}* Called Joel, v. 36.

**V. 25.** *{And the sons of Elkanah}* Some take this Elkanah to be the son of Shaul; as if it had been thus more largely expressed, *Shaul his son, and Elkanah his son. And the sons of Elkanah*.

*{Amasai}* See v. 35, 36.

*{and Ahimoth}* We read no more of this name.

**V. 26.** *{As for Elkanah}* Some make this Elkanah to be the son of Amasai, v. 25, and the same that is called Mahath, v. 35.

*{the sons of Elkanah}* This Elkanah is taken to be another from him that is mentioned in the beginning of the verse; the former to be the father, called also Mahath; the latter, the son; and both father and son called by this one name Elkanah, *q.d.*, *The son of Amasai, Elkanah: his son, Elkanah*. This is collected from v. 35, 36.

*{Zophai his son}* Or, *Zuph*, 1 Sam. 1:1.

*{and Nahath his son}* Or, *Toah*, v. 34.

**V. 27.** *{Eliab his son}* Or, *Eliel*, v. 34.

*{Jeroham his son}* See 1 Sam. 1:1.

*{Elkanah his son}* This being the father of Samuel, 1 Sam. 1:1, 20, some translators add to the end of this verse, *Samuel his son*.

**V. 28.** *{And the sons of Samuel}* This is that great prophet whose history is set down in the first book of Samuel.

*{the firstborn, Vashni}* Or, *Joel*, v. 33 and 1 Sam. 8:2.

*{and Abiah}* These two being appointed by their father in his old age to be judges over Israel, were so corrupt as they made the people weary of that government, and desirous of a king, 1 Sam. 8:4, 5.

**V. 29.** *{The sons of Merari, Mahli}* Mahli was Merari’s grandchild, v. 47; the rest were his children’s children. Therefore after Mahli is to be added this clause, *his son*.

*{Libni his son, etc.}* These seven here said to be sons of Merari, namely Mahli, Libni, Shimei, Uzza, Shimea, Haggiah, Asaiah, were the heads of such Levites as were set apart to holy functions in God’s house. More of the posterity of Merari are reckoned v. 44.

**V. 31.** *{And these are they}* This relative these has reference to such as follow from v. 33 to v. 49. In the former verses the genealogies of the Levites are set forth: here begin to be declared the offices of both Levites and priests; of the Levites first.

*{whom David set over the service of song}* Heb. *the hands of music*. Hands is oft put for ministry. See 1 Kings 8:53. Some expound it of the place, others of the persons, others of the function. In David’s time the number of Levites being exceedingly increased, he distributed them into distinct orders, and being a man zealous of God’s glory, he did not only use to quicken up his own spirit by vocal and instrumental music, but also settled sundry orders of Levites in their places and courses, both by voice and by sounding and stringed instruments to quicken up the spirits of God’s people to praise him the more cheerfully. See ch. 25:1, etc. In these respects he is styled *the sweet psalmist of Israel*, 2 Sam. 23:1. Though before David’s time there was great use of all manner of music about the service of God, yet in his time and after there is much more registered of music in God’s house than in the days before him.

*{in the house of the LORD}* Till Solomon had built the temple, divine services were performed in the tabernacle; and therefore it was called *the house of the Lord*, 1 Sam. 1:7, 24.

*{after that the ark had rest}* The ark was wont to be carried upon men’s shoulders from place to place; yea, the tabernacle also itself was oft removed. See 1 Kings 8:4. But David fixed the tabernacle in a close place, and pitched a tent for the ark, out of which it was not removed till it was carried into the temple, ch. 16. After the ark was settled in that tent, it was said to have rest.

**V. 32.** *{And they ministered before the dwelling-place of the tabernacle}* The tabernacle is called a dwelling-place, because the Lord manifested his abode there, by sundry evidences and types of his presence, not for a while, but so long as the tabernacle and temple continued. In this respect it is also styled *the house of God*, v. 48. See 1 Kings 3:1. It was also called a dwelling-place, because David had now fixed it, not to be removed as formerly it had been. See 1 Kings 8:4. It was made like a tent of cloth, and therefore called a tabernacle. See Exod. 26:1, etc.

*{of the congregation}* See 1 Kings 8:4.

*{with singing}* Both with voice and instruments. See v. 31.

*{until Solomon had built the house of the LORD}* I.e., the temple. When it was built, the tabernacle was laid up in it, 1 Kings 8:4. And all the services that had been before performed in the tabernacle were performed in the temple.

*{in Jerusalem}* The city which God chose, 1 Kings 11:36.

*{and then they waited on their office}* They attended diligently on that particular and distinct function whereunto they were appointed.

*{according to their order}* Order has here respect to the places where they were set, see v. 39, 44; to the times or turns when they should serve, for they were to serve by course; and to the particular services which they should perform; as some to sing with the voice, some to play on instruments, some to blow trumpets. See 2 Kings 11:5. They continued the same order in the temple that David had appointed them in the tabernacle.

**V. 33.** *{And these are they that waited}* Heb. *stood*. For the Levites performed their functions in the temple, especially such as were singers, standing, Ps. 134:1.

*{with their children}* By children are here meant their posterity, generation after generation.

*{Of the sons of the Kohathites}* As the high priests were of Kohath’s posterity, so also were such as had the chief functions among the Levites.

*{Heman}* There were two men of special account of this name; one of the tribe of Judah, ch. 2:6; another of the tribe of Levi, which was this man. He among the singers was the chiefest, and therefore stood in the midst, having some on his right hand, and others on his left, v. 39, 44. Some ascribe the penning of the 88th Psalm to this man; and the title of the psalm seems to intend as much: though his chief work therein might be only to set the tune, the psalm itself being indited by David.

*{a singer}* This implies that he was a chief, choice singer, a master of music. See ch. 15:17, 19.

*{the son of Joel}* Called Vashni, v. 28. Because Heman was a man of great note and name, his ancestors by a lineal ascent are set down till we come to Israel the father of the twelve tribes, v. 38. The genealogy from v. 22 to v. 29 is here repeated, from this verse to v. 39; only there is some alteration of names, which have been noted in the former catalogue: and whereas the pedigree is before set down by descent, here it is set down by ascent.

**V. 37.** *{Ebiasaph, the son of Korah}* Two generations betwixt Korah and Ebiasaph, v. 22, 23, namely Assir and Elkanah, are here omitted.

**V. 38.** *{The son of Izhar, the son of Kohath}* See v. 18.

*{the son of Levi, the son of Israel}* Ch. 2:1.

**V. 39.** *{And his brother Asaph}* Asaph is said here to be brother to Heman, because he was his kinsman, of the same stock or tribe, and brother in office.

*{who stood on his right hand}* The right hand was the second place; the midst being the first, and the left hand the third. See 1 Kings 2:19.

*{even Asaph}* A prime musician, especially on instruments. See ch. 15:19 and 16:5, 7, 37. Sundry psalms were committed to him to compose and tune, if not indited by him; as 50 and 73 and 77, with many others.

*{the son of Berachiah}* We read not anything of this man before, but he is here named for honor’s sake, and also for distinction.

*{the son of Shimea, etc.}* The ancestors of Asaph are set down by ascent lineally, till it come to Levi, v. 43. These mentioned in this catalogue, from v. 37 to 44, came from Levi by his eldest son Gershom. There is another catalogue of Gershom’s posterity, v. 20, 21, but most of the names in these catalogues are very different; and in this latter there are almost as many more as in the former.

**V. 44.** *{And their brethren}* This relative their has reference to Heman, v. 33, and to Asaph, v. 39, therefore the plural number is used. This word brethren is taken as brother, v. 39.

*{the sons of Merari, stood on the left hand}* See v. 39. Merari was Levi’s youngest son, and therefore is set in the lowest place.

*{Ethan}* This man also was a prime singer, ch. 15:19, and had psalms also committed to him, Ps. 89:1. There was another Ethan, ch. 2:6; 1 Kings 4:31.

*{the son of Kishi}* Or, *Kushaiah*, ch. 15:17. Such a genealogy of this man is set down as was before of Heman and Asaph. Here are many more ancestors than v. 29, 30.

**V. 48.** *{Their brethren also}* See v. 39, 44.

*{the Levites}* He means other sons of Levi than those reckoned up before, not brought up to be singers.

*{were appointed to all manner of service}* As to be porters, treasurers, and other officers, ch. 26:1, 20.

*{of the tabernacle of the house of God}* See v. 31, 32 and 1 Kings 8:4.

**V. 49.** *{But Aaron and his sons}* From v. 31 to this verse the offices and orders of Levites are set down; but here the offices of priests. For Aaron’s sons were all priests.

*{offered upon the altar of the burnt offering}* Num. 18:7; Lev. 1:9. First, upon that great brass altar which Moses made, while the tabernacle stood, Exod. 38:1, etc., but afterwards on that greater altar which Solomon made, 2 Chr. 4:1. Hereon were offered all manner of sacrifices and oblations, except incense only.

*{and on the altar of incense}* Exod. 37:27, 25. Hereon nothing but incense was offered, Exod. 30:7. Of incense see 1 Kings 9:25.

*{and were appointed for all the work of the place most holy}* Or, *holy of holies*. The further and innermost part of the temple had this title most properly given it. See 1 Kings 6:16. It was also called the oracle. See 1 Kings 6:5. Into it the high priest only, and that once a year, was to go, Heb. 9:7. But because here is mention made of all sorts of priests, that part also of the temple where the priests did their services may be comprised under this title, *The place most holy*.

*{and to make an atonement}* This phrase is the interpretation of one short Hebrew word, *כפר* *Caphar*, which properly signifies *to cover*. The cover of the ark is set out by a word derived from that root, Exod. 25:17. Metaphorically it is applied to sin, and to wrath incensed by sin; and signifies *to cover* them, i.e., *to pardon sin*, and *to pacify wrath*: as Ps. 78:38, where it is translated, *He forgave iniquity*, the Hebrew says, *He covered iniquity*. And where in relation to the wrath of the king it is said, *A wise man will pacify it*, in Hebrew it is, *A wise man will cover it*, Prov. 16:14. It oft signifies *to be propitious, favorable, or merciful*; as Deut. 21:8, *Be merciful to thy people Israel*. The word is frequently translated *to expiate*, i.e., *to purge or take away any uncleanness*, so as it may not be imputed. It is also used, *to make fit for holy uses*. Thus it is applied to things under the law, and to persons; as, *to the holy sanctuary, to the tabernacle of the congregation, and to the altar*; *to the priests, and to all the people of the congregation*, Lev. 16:33. Atonement, according to the English notation of the word, implies being at one, and may be opposed to two or more being at odds or variance. Such an atonement is as much as agreement or reconciliation. This in regard of that odds which is between God and man is done two ways: 1. By taking away sin, the cause of wrath; 2. By pacifying wrath, the effect of sin. Under the law this was typified by offerings for sin, and incense; by the former whereof sin is removed, and by the latter wrath is pacified. Though these may thus be distinguished, yet can they not be severed: for except sin be taken away, wrath will not be pacified: and were it possible that wrath could be pacified, if sin remained, it would be incensed again. By the priests making atonement legally, Christ’s making reconciliation betwixt God and man was typified.

*{according to all that Moses the servant of God}* This attribute is oft applied to Moses in regard of his faithfulness to God, Num. 12:7; Heb. 3:2. In this respect also David is likewise oft styled *servant of God*. See 1 Kings 8:24.

*{commanded}* That which Moses delivered to the people, he received from God, and delivered in the name of God; so it was the Lord that commanded by him.

**V. 50, 51, 52, 53.** *{And these are the sons of Aaron, etc.}* The same very catalogue of high priests that was before registered v. 3, etc., from Aaron, that first ministered in the tabernacle, to Ahimaaz, that ministered in the temple when it was first built, is here repeated, from this verse to v. 54. Succession of high priests served for distinction of times in ecclesiastical affairs, as succession of kings for distinction of civil affairs. There is the like catalogue set down Ezra 7:1, etc.

**V. 54.** *{Now these are their dwelling-places}* From hence to the end of the chapter the third point about the tribe of Levi is prosecuted, namely their several and distinct habitations. For they had not their inheritance entire by itself, but dispersed up and down in other tribes. See ch. 2:1. It appears that the Levites had at this time LXVIII cities, twenty more than were appointed by Joshua.

*{throughout their castles}* These were fair houses built of stone. For they had not small, mean cottages, but spacious and strong places for their better commodity and security.

*{in their coasts}* In such parts of the tribes as were allotted to them.

*{of the sons of Aaron}* These being the priests had their habitations first assigned them.

*{of the families of the Kohathites}* Kohath was one of the sons of Levi from whom descended Aaron, v. 12. And the sons of Aaron were heads of the chiefest families that descended from him.

*{for theirs was the lot}* It fell out to them to have the first and choicest portion, Josh. 21:10.

**V. 55.** *{And they gave them}* It is expressly said, *the children of Israel gave unto the Levites these cities*, Josh. 21:8.

*{Hebron in the land of Judah}* Of old called Kiriath-arba. There Sarah died; and there was the cave wherein the ancient patriarchs with their wives were buried, Gen. 23:2, 19 and 49:30, 31. See ch. 3:10.

*{and the suburbs thereof round about it}* By suburbs are meant such houses as were without, near to the city, and also such pasture grounds adjoining as were useful for cattle.

**V. 56.** *{But the fields of the city, and the villages thereof}* I.e., such fields and villages as were more remote from Hebron, but appertained thereto. Of villages see ch. 4:32.

*{they gave to Caleb the son of Jephunneh}* This was done by Joshua, with the consent of the people, according to the commandment of the Lord, Josh. 15:13.

**V. 57.** *{And to the sons of Aaron they gave the cities of Judah}* He means such sons of Aaron and such cities of Judah as are hereafter mentioned.

*{namely Hebron, the city of refuge}* There were in sundry tribes certain cities called cities of refuge, set apart for such as killed any person unwittingly. Hereof see Num. 35:6; Josh. 20:2, etc. Of these there were six: I. Hebron. II. Shechem, v. 67. III. Golan, v. 71. IV. Kedesh, v. 76. V. Bezer, v. 78. VI. Ramoth, v. 80.

*{and Libna, etc.}* Josh. 21:13, 14.

**V. 58.** *{And Hilen}* Or, *Holon*, Josh. 21:19.

**V. 59.** *{And Ashan, etc.}* Or, *Ain*, Josh. 21:16.

*{and Beth-shemesh}* Before this Juttah is set down, Josh. 21:16, and thus they make nine cities. All these were not out of Judah alone, but some of them out of Simeon, Josh. 21:9.

**V. 60.** *{And out of the tribe of Benjamin, Geba}* Benjamin bordered upon Judah, Josh. 18:11, and therefore is here set next. Before Geba Gibeon is set down, Josh. 21:17.

*{and Alemeth}* Or, *Almon*, Josh. 21:18.

*{and Anathoth}* This city was honored by Jeremiah the prophet, who was born there, Jer. 1:18.

*{All their cities throughout their families were thirteen cities}* There are but eleven here set down, eight out of Judah, and three out of Benjamin; but Juttah being added to the cities of Judah, and Gibeon to the cities of Benjamin, they make thirteen.

**V. 61.** *{And unto the sons of Kohath, which were left of the family of that tribe}* Kohath had many sons, v. 2. His posterity from Amram by Aaron were all priests; others, called Levites, in distinction from priests. To such as were priests the cities before mentioned, v. 55, etc., were given. The others are meant by this phrase, *which were left of the family of that tribe*. The family of Kohath is here meant, and the tribe of Levi.

*{were cities given out of the half-tribe, namely out of the half-tribe of Manasseh}* They had also cities out of Ephraim, v. 66; Josh. 21:20, 21.

*{by lot}* Casting lots was used about dividing the whole land of Canaan to the several tribes, Num. 26:55; Josh. 13:6, 7 and 15:1, and also for apportioning out cities to the Levites, Josh. 21:4, etc. This means, I say, was used, to show that the Lord ordered to every one his distinct part, Prov. 16:33. See ch. 24:5.

*{ten cities}* I.e., the cities mentioned v. 67, 68, 70 and Josh. 21:21, etc.

**V. 62.** *{And to the sons of Gershom, etc.}* V. 1, 20, etc.

*{out of the tribe of Issachar, and out of the tribe of Asher, and out of the tribe of Naphtali}* These three tribes bordered one on another on this side Jordan.

*{and out of the tribe of Manasseh in Bashan}* That part of Manasseh is here meant which was on the other side Jordan, as is evident by its situation, in Bashan, Josh. 17:1, 5.

*{thirteen tribes}* Mentioned v. 71, etc., and Josh. 21:27, etc.

**V. 63.** *{Unto the sons of Merari}* See v. 1, 29, etc.

*{were given by lot}* See v. 61.

*{throughout their families, out of the tribe of Reuben, and out of the tribe of Gad}* These were beyond Jordan, Josh. 13:8.

*{and out of the tribe of Zebulun}* On the hither side of Jordan.

*{twelve cities}* Mentioned v. 77, etc., and Josh. 21:34, etc.

**V. 64.** *{And the children of Israel gave to the Levites these cities, with their suburbs}* This verse is inserted as a transition betwixt the forenamed generations and the particulars which follow.

**V. 65.** *{And they gave}* Namely to the priests, v. 54, 57.

*{by lot}* See v. 61.

*{out of the tribe of the children of Judah, etc.}* These three tribes, Judah, Simeon, Benjamin, bordered together. See 1 Kings 11:13.

*{these cities, which are called by their names}* This has reference to v. 57, etc., for there these cities are expressly named.

**V. 66.** *{And the residue of the families of the sons of Kohath, etc.}* See v. 61.

**V. 67.** *{And they gave unto them of the cities of refuge}* See v. 57.

*{Shechem in mount Ephraim}* The second city of refuge. See v. 57.

*{with her suburbs}* See v. 55.

*{They gave also Gezer, etc.}* Of Gezer see 1 Kings 9:15.

**V. 68.** *{And Jokmeam}* Or, *Kibzaim*, Josh. 21:22.

*{and Beth-horon}* See 1 Kings 9:17.

**V. 69.** *{And Aijalon, etc., and Gath-rimmon}* These two, with Eltekeh and Gibbethon, were the four cities out of the tribe of Dan, Josh. 21:24, 25. There was another Ajalon, in Zebulun, where Elon was buried, Judg. 12:12, and another in Benjamin, ch. 8:13; 2 Chr. 11:20, famous by the manifestation of the sun’s standing there, Josh. 10:12.

**V. 70.** *{And out of the half-tribe of Manasseh, Aner}* Or, *Taanach*.

*{and Bileam}* Or, *Gath-rimmon*, Josh. 21:25. Or, *Ibleam*, Josh. 17:11. These were on this side of Jordan. For that part of Manasseh is here meant which had their inheritance on this side, Josh. 17:7, etc.

*{for the family of the remnant of the sons of Kohath}* See v. 61.

**V. 71.** *{Unto the sons of Gershom}* See v. 1, 20, etc.

*{were given out of the family of the half-tribe of Manasseh}* Which were on the other side of Jordan.

*{Golan}* This was the third city of refuge. See v. 57; Deut. 4:43; Josh. 20:4 and 28:27.

*{in Bashan}* See ch. 5:11; Josh. 21:27.

*{and Ashtaroth}* Or, *Beeshterah*. See Deut. 1:4; Josh. 13:31. Ashtaroth was also the name of an idol, whereof see 1 Kings 11:5, 33.

**V. 72.** *{And out of the tribe of Issachar}* This was on this side Jordan. For the families of the Levites, even such as came from the same stock, were dispersed into several tribes, some on this, others on that side Jordan.

*{Kedesh}* There was another city of this name, which was a city of refuge in the tribe of Naphtali, v. 76. This city was also called Kishon, Josh. 21:28.

*{Daberath}* Or, *Dabareh*, Josh. 21:28.

**V. 73.** *{Ramoth}* Or, *Jarmuth*, Josh. 21:29.

*{Anem}* Or, *Engannim*, Josh. 21:29.

**V. 74.** *{And out of the tribe of Asher}* This tribe bordered on Issachar.

*{Mashal}* Or, *Mishal*, Josh. 21:30.

*{and Abdon}* This was also the name of a judge, Judg. 12:13.

**V. 75.** *{And Hukok}* Or, *Helkath*, Josh. 21:31.

*{and Rehob}* This also was the name of a man, 1 Sam. 8:3; Neh. 10:11.

**V. 76.** *{And out of the tribe of Naphtali, Kedesh in Galilee}* This was the fourth city of refuge. See v. 57; Josh. 21:32. Of Galilee see 1 Kings 9:11.

*{and Hammon}* Or, *Hammoth-dor*, Josh. 21:32.

*{and Kirjathaim}* Or, *Kartan*, Josh. 21:32. From this city upward to Golan there are thirteen cities; two out of Manasseh, v. 71, four out of Issachar, v. 72, 73, four out of Asher, v. 74, 75, and three out of Naphtali.

**V. 77.** *{Unto the rest of the children of Merari}* Or, *unto the children of Merari*, which are left. I.e., which are last reckoned up.

*{were given out of the tribe of Zebulun, Rimmon}* Or, *Dimnah*, Josh. 21:35. Rimmon was also the name of a man, 2 Sam. 4:2, and of an idol, 2 Kings 5:18.

*{Tabor}* Or, *Nahalal*, Josh. 21:35. There are reckoned up two other cities given out of Zebulun to the children of Merari, namely, Jokneam, and Kartah; so as they are four in all, Josh. 21:34, 35.

**V. 78.** *{And on the other side Jordan by Jericho}* Some say that Jordan did flow about Jericho. Others, that Jericho was on this side Jordan: which is manifest, in that the Israelites set upon it after they were come over Jordan, Josh. 6:1, etc. Thus they take the city here named to be over against Jericho. But that which is here intended is, that Jericho was near to Jordan.

*{on the east side of Jordan}* That side that was toward the wilderness wherein the Israelites traveled forty years.

*{were given them out of the tribe of Reuben, Bezer}* Josh. 21:36. This was the fifth city of refuge. See v. 57. Bezer was also a man’s name, ch. 7:37.

*{in the wilderness}* That wilderness mentioned before.

**V. 79.** *{Jahzah, Kedemoth, and Mephaath}* Josh. 21:36, 37.

**V. 80.** *{And out of the tribe of Gad, Ramoth in Gilead}* See 1 Kings 4:13. This was the sixth city of refuge. Of Gilead see 1 Kings 4:13.

*{and Mahanaim}* Of this name see Gen. 32:4.

**V. 81.** *{And Heshbon}* In this city the king of the Amorites dwelt, Num. 21:34.

*{and Jazer}* The whole land about this city was also called Jazer. It was a land fit for cattle, Num. 32:4. Before the ten cities last mentioned there are two which are here omitted (see v. 77), and these make up twelve, v. 63; Josh. 21:40.

## CHAPTER 7

**Verse 1.** *{Now the sons of Issachar}* Issachar was the fifth son of Leah Jacob’s first wife, Gen. 30:17, 18. Therefore his genealogy is set next to the genealogies of her four first sons.

*{were Tola}* Gen. 46:13. There was another of this tribe of the same name, who was a judge in Israel, Judg. 10:1.

*{and Puah}* Or, *Phuvah*, Gen. 46:13.

*{Jashub}* Or, *Job*, Gen. 46:13. This cannot be that Job whose history carries his name; for he was of another stock, Job 1:1. Besides there is a difference in the Hebrew letters of each name.

*{and Shimron, four}* These went with Jacob into Egypt, Gen. 46:7, 13.

**V. 2.** *{And the sons of Tola, Uzzi, etc.}* These six sons of Tola are they whose posterity were called Tolaites, Num. 26:23.

*{heads of their father’s house, to wit, of Tola}* They were such as propagated the posterity of their father Tola; which posterity was distinguished by the children that came from each of them, and made distinct families.

*{they were valiant men of might}* This doubling of words signifying the same thing, *valiant of might*, is a Hebraism that carries much emphasis. It imports men very valiant and strong.

*{whose number was in the days of David}* This has relation to that history 2 Sam. 24:1, etc., when David caused Joab to number the people. Or to David’s numbering of the people according to their monthly courses, ch. 27:1.

*{two and twenty thousand and six hundred}* If we compare this tribe with the others, when all were numbered, we shall find this was a numerous tribe. See Num. 1:27 and 26:25. See their whole number v. 5.

**V. 3.** *{And the sons of Uzzi, Izrahiah}* Either Uzzi had more sons, and among them Izrahiah was one; or else the plural number sons is synecdochically put for the singular.

*{and the sons of Izrahiah, Michael, and Obadiah, and Joel, Ishiah, five}* The father must be comprised; for there are mentioned but four sons. See the like ch. 5:22.

*{all of them chief men}* Men in authority, heads of families, and of excellent parts for wisdom and valor.

**V. 4.** *{And with them}* Namely, with the sons of Uzzi.

*{by their generations, after the house of their fathers}* Their posterity was distinguished by the several families that descended from each of the forenamed fathers.

*{were bands of soldiers for war}* Though they were of a gentle disposition, and industrious in tillage of land, and other callings that made to their profit, as may be gathered out of Jacob’s blessing, Gen. 49:14, 15, yet prudently they prepared for war, and trained up their young men thereunto, insomuch as they had many expert soldiers.

*{six and thirty thousand men}* These make a great army; yet they all belonged to the families that descended from Uzzi. See v. 5.

*{for they had many wives and sons}* Heb. *they multiplied wives and children*. This was the reason of their numerous posterity.

**V. 5.** *{And their brethren among all the families of Issachar}* Such as came from other sons of Issachar beside Tola.

*{were men of might}* See v. 2. They were such as by education were skillful in war, and also had courageous minds.

*{reckoned in all by their genealogies fourscore and seven thousand}* This is a distinct number from those mentioned v. 2 and 4. The first number was of other sons of Tola besides Uzzi; the second, of the sons of Uzzi; and this third, of other sons of Issachar besides Tola. If all the three be joined together, namely, two and twenty thousand and six hundred, v. 2, six and thirty thousand, v. 4, and fourscore and seven thousand in this verse, the whole sum of all amounts to a hundred forty-five thousand six hundred. This account was taken in David’s time. And it is no wonder that there were so many valiant men in one tribe, seeing Joab brought the sum of fifteen hundred and seventy thousand men that drew sword, out of all Israel, and yet the Levites and Benjamites were not counted among them, ch. 21:5, 6.

**V. 6.** *{The sons of Benjamin}* Benjamin was Jacob’s youngest son, Gen. 35:18 and 42:13. In order the genealogy of Zebulun should here have been placed: for he was the sixth son of Jacob by Leah: but his genealogy is not set down at all in this catalogue of genealogies recorded in the nine first chapters of this book. Yet ch. 12:33 there is mention made of fifty thousand expert in war, of Zebulun; and also of four cities given to the Levites out of this tribe, ch. 6:63. Dan’s genealogy is also wanting. See v. 12, 13.

*{Bela, and Becher, and Jediael}* Or, *Ashbel*, Gen. 46:21.

*{three}* Ten sons of Benjamin are mentioned Gen. 46:21. But some of them might be grandchildren. Or these three may be here set down as special and prime men. Or, when Joab took the number of the several tribes, he might take the number of the posterity of these three only in the tribe of Benjamin; for it is said that *he counted not Levi and Benjamin*, ch. 21:6. Though he might begin with Benjamin, yet he might leave off before he had finished that tribe. More of Benjamin’s sons are set down in the reiteration of Benjamin’s genealogy, ch. 8:1, 2.

**V. 7.** *{And the sons of Bela, Ezbon, etc.}* There are five sons of Bela here mentioned, out of whom issued a great posterity.

*{heads of the house of their fathers, mighty men of valour}* See v. 2.

*{and were reckoned by their genealogies, twenty and two thousand and thirty four}* This number of Bela was in all probability taken when the number of Tola was, v. 2.

**V. 8.** *{And the sons of Becher}* Benjamin’s second son, v. 6.

*{Zemira, etc.}* There are nine of these reckoned up.

**V. 9.** *{And the number of them, after their genealogy, etc.}* See v. 2.

**V. 10.** *{The sons also of Jediael}* Benjamin’s third son, v. 6.

*{Bilhan}* See v. 3.

*{and the sons of Bilhan, Jeush, etc.}* These six sons of Bilhan added much to the increase of the tribe.

**V. 11.** *{All these were the sons, etc.}* See v. 2.

*{soldiers, fit to go out for war and battle}* Heb. *going out into the army to war*. Strong of body, of a valorous mind, and trained up to the war.

**V. 12.** *{Shuppim also}* Or, *Muppim*, Gen. 46:21.

*{and Huppim}* Shuppim and Huppim are called *Shupham* and *Hupham*, and are made heads of families, Num. 26:39. Because Huppim and Shuppim are of the plural termination, some make them the names, not of particular persons, but of families, and translate them *Shuppims* and *Huppims*, and bring them in as a posterity, which increased the number aforesaid. These are over and above the seventeen thousand and two hundred, v. 11.

*{the children of Ir}* Some take *Ir* to be the same that is called Iri, one of the sons of Bela, v. 7. But Ir in Hebrew signifying *a city*, some take it here appellatively, and translate, *Sons of the city*, i.e., born, brought up, and dwelling in the city.

*{and Hushim}* This name is given to the sons of Dan; and because no mention is elsewhere made of the genealogy of Dan, they conceive his posterity to be comprised under it. This name is given also to a woman, ch. 8:8, 11. We read not the name elsewhere given to any of the posterity of Benjamin. Some take Hushim, as Shuppim and Huppim, to be of the plural number, and translate it *Hushims*, setting forth a family.

*{the sons of Aher}* Or, *Ahiram*, Num. 26:38. We read not of this name Aher anywhere else. In Hebrew it signifies *another*. And so it may be referred to Dan; *q.d.*, *The Shuppims and Huppims were of Benjamin; but the Hushims, of another, namely Dan*. Some say, that for their ancient idolatry the Danites were very few in number, Judg. 18:30, 31, and that by reason of their paucity their genealogy is here but touched by the way, in one clause, and that obscurely. Zebulun’s is also omitted. See v. 6. There is no mention of Dan, where all the other tribes are reckoned up to be sealed, Rev. 7:5, etc.

**V. 13.** *{The sons of Naphtali, Jahziel, and Guni, and Jezer, and Shallum, the sons of Bilhah}* These four last were Bilhah’s grandchildren: for she had but two sons, Dan and Naphtali, Gen. 30:6, 8. Some refer the four last before mentioned to Naphtali, but comprise the posterity of Dan under that phrase, *sons of Bilhah*. But if Dan’s posterity were comprised under Hushim, v. 12, surely they would not again thus obscurely be intimated.

**V. 14.** *{The sons of Manasseh}* The eldest son of Joseph, Gen. 41:51.

*{Ashriel}* Under the word sons here applied to Manasseh his posterity must be meant, for Ashriel was sundry degrees from Manasseh, Num. 26:29, 30, 31. See v. 3.

*{whom she bare}* Namely, his wife.

*{but his concubine}* Of concubines see 1 Kings 11:3.

*{the Aramitess}* By birth she was a heathen, of Syria; but it may be by profession, a proselyte.

*{bare Machir the father of Gilead}* Machir the eldest son of Manasseh was father of the Machirites; and Gilead his son, of the Gileadites, Num. 26:29.

**V. 15.** *{And Machir took to wife the sister of Huppim and Shuppim}* See v. 12. An Israelite might marry one of another tribe, provided that the woman married were not an heir to land, Num. 36:8.

*{whose sister’s name was Maachah}* David had a wife of this name, 2 Sam. 3:3.

*{And the name of the second was Zelophehad}* The word second has reference to Ashriel. Zelophehad was in descent after Ashriel. For Ashriel was in the first degree from Manasseh, Zelophehad in the fourth, Num. 26:33.

*{and Zelophehad had daughters}* This is to be taken exclusively, daughters only, and no sons. See Num. 26:33. Hereupon a law was made for daughters when they had no brothers to inherit, Num. 27:1, etc., and another law also to restrain such daughters from marrying out of their tribe, Num. 36:1, etc.

**V. 16.** *{And Maachah the wife of Machir}* See v. 15.

*{bare a son, and she called his name Peresh}* There is no mention made anywhere else of this Peresh, nor of his brother Sheresh.

*{and the name of his brother was Sheresh}* It is probable these two brothers had either two fathers, or two mothers, because the name son is set down in the singular number.

*{and his sons were Ulam and Rekem}* His has reference to Sheresh.

**V. 17.** *{And the sons of Ulam, Bedan}* Mention is made of Bedan, 1 Sam. 12:11. He is taken to be Jair the judge, Judg. 10:3. And he was a Gileadite, so as he may be the man here meant.

*{These were the sons of Gilead}* These whose names are before recorded were the posterity of Gilead: at least they were so accounted to be of his posterity as they had inheritance amongst them.

*{the son of Machir, the son of Manasseh}* See v. 14.

**V. 18.** *{And his sister Hamoleketh}* She was Gilead’s sister, the daughter of Machir.

*{bare Ishod, and Abiezer, and Mahalah}* These are accounted to be of the stock of Gilead.

**V. 19.** *{And the sons of Shemida were Ahian, etc.}* Shemida is not mentioned before; yet he and his four sons, Ahian, Shechem, Likhi and Aniam, are reputed to be of Gilead’s genealogy. See Num. 26:30, 31, 32.

**V. 20.** *{And the sons of Ephraim}* Ephraim was Manasseh’s younger brother, Gen. 42:52, but by old Jacob preferred before him, Gen. 48:19, 20.

*{Shuthelah}* In what respect the word sons is used in the plural number, when only one son is mentioned, see v. 3. It may here comprise under it grandchildren also. Of Ephraim’s posterity see Num. 26:35, etc.

*{and Bered his son}* With Shuthelah, there are seven several generations mentioned in order descending from Ephraim. Of Shuthelah came the family of the Shuthalites, Num. 26:35. The other eight also were in their generations heads of families.

**V. 21.** *{Whom}* This has most proper reference to the three sons of Zabad, mentioned in this verse, namely, Shuthelah, and Ezer, and Elead. But against this it is objected, that Ephraim was their father, v. 22, and this Zabad was the sixth from Ephraim; 1 Shuthelah, 2 Bered, 3 Tahath, 4 Eladah, 5 Tahath, 6 Zabad: now it is not probable that Ephraim should live to see so many generations. But I answer, I. In our days many have lived to see a fourth, yea, and a fifth generation issuing from them: which may be done within the space of a hundred years and less. Joseph, Ephraim’s father, lived a hundred and ten years, Gen. 50:26. Levi, his uncle, a hundred thirty and seven years; and Kohath, his cousin-german, a hundred thirty and three years, Exod. 6:16, 18. And others, born sundry generations after him, lived above a hundred years: so as Ephraim might live till the three sons of Zabad were born, and grown up to some ripe age. II. He that is called Ephraim, v. 22, might be the same that is here called Zabad: for two names are frequently given in sacred Scripture to one and the same man.

*{the men of Gath that were born in that land slew}* The land wherein the city of Gath was situated is here meant, i.e. the land of the Philistims. And such Philistims are here meant as were born or brought up, or dwelt in the city of Gath. See on Exod. 13:17. Psal. 78:9.

*{because they came down to take away their cattle}* Here are two relatives, they, and their; and there is much question whether the reference be to the Philistims or to the Israelites. In the former respect it implies that the Philistims coming to rob the Ephraimites of their cattle, the Ephraimites were slain in resisting them; in the latter, that the Ephraimites going out to spoil the Philistims of their cattle, were overmatched and slain by them.

**V. 22.** *{And Ephraim their father}* If this be taken of Ephraim the son of Joseph, it has reference to some history acted while the children of Israel sojourned in Egypt: for all the twelve patriarchs died in Egypt, Act. 7:15. The Philistims bordering upon Egypt, oft made inroads into that land, and plundered goods, and took away cattle; hereupon the Ephraimites inhabiting there might offer to resist them, and so be slain.

*{mourned many days}* Death of children, especially of many together, and that after so violent a manner, cannot but pierce the heart of a tender father.

*{and his brethren came to comfort him}* Under this word brethren may his kindred be comprised. See Gen. 37:35. Job 2:11.

**V. 23.** *{And when he went in to his wife}* This phrase intimates the peculiar marriage duty, Gen. 16:4 & 29:23, 30, and following upon the slaughter of Ephraim’s children, it infers the reason, namely, that he might have more children instead of those that were slain.

*{she conceived and bare a son}* He could not but take God’s giving him another son for a great blessing.

*{and he called his name Beriah}* The meaning of this word is, in-affliction. See the like ch. 4:9.

*{because it went evil with his house}* He means his children, the stay of his house or family.

**V. 24.** *{And his daughter was Sherah}* Mention is made of her to show that some comfort remained to him in this daughter after he had lost all his sons.

*{who built Beth-horon}* She was a woman of good account, seeing she could build so much as is here mentioned. These places were in Canaan, and built when the Israelites were first settled in that land, if not before. Build is oft put for repair, beautify, or fortify, as 1 King. 9:15, 17 & 15:17, and thus might these cities here be said to be built.

*{the nether, and the upper}* See 1 King. 9:17.

*{and Uzzen-Sherah}* These two words are the name of one place nowhere else mentioned.

**V. 25.** *{And Rephah was his son}* i.e. Ephraim’s, v. 22, or Beriah’s, v. 23.

*{also Rezeph}* Rezeph was Rephah’s brother.

*{and Tela his son}* Some count Tela also Beriah’s son.

*{and Tahan his son}* The head of the Tahanites, Num. 26:35.

**V. 26.** *{Laadan his son, Ammihud his son, Elishama his son}* This Elishama was prince of the tribe of Ephraim when the Israelites came out of Egypt, Num. 1:10 & 2:18 & 7:48, 53, by which it appears that all of Ephraim before mentioned were born and brought up in Egypt.

**V. 27.** *{Non his son}* Or, *Nun*, Num. 13:8.

*{Jehoshua his son}* This is that valiant Joshua who vanquished the Canaanites, and settled Israel in their land.

**V. 28.** *{And their possessions and habitations}* Their has reference to the Ephraimites.

*{Bethel, and the towns thereof}* Bethel lay betwixt Ephraim and Benjamin, Josh. 16:1, 2 & 18:13, 22.

*{and eastward Naaran}* Called Naarath, Josh. 16:7.

*{and westward Gezer}* 1 King. 9:15.

*{with the towns thereof}* Heb. *the daughters thereof*. Towns and villages belonged to these greater cities, as daughters to their parents; and they were also maintained by them.

*{Shechem, etc.}* See 1 King. 12:1.

*{unto Gaza, etc.}* Or, *Adasa*, 1 Mac. 7:45. One of the five principalities of the Philistims bore this title, 1 Sam. 6:17.

**V. 29.** *{And by the borders}* Heb. *bands*.

*{of the children of Manasseh}* i.e. That part of Manasseh that was on this side Jordan.

*{Beth-shean, etc.}* Here are but four cities mentioned, appertaining to Manasseh, Beth-shean, Taanach, Megiddo and Dor: but two more are added, Josh. 17:11, namely Ibleam and Endor.

*{In these dwelt the children of Joseph the son of Israel}* These has reference to all the cities before mentioned, appertaining to Ephraim and Manasseh, both sons of Joseph.

**V. 30.** *{The sons of Asher}* Asher being the youngest son of Jacob by Zilpah Leah’s maid, is brought in in the last place.
