By this pronoun my he does not appropriate God to himself, as if he excluded others, (see Joh. 20:17.) but only presses his own right and interest in him, 1 Chron. 17:25.

*{that I should do this thing}* I. e. drink the water.

*{shall I drink the blood of these men}* That which might have cost them their blood.

*{that have put their lives in jeopardy}* Heb. *with their lives*. They ventured their lives with fetching that water.

*{for with the jeopardy of their lives they brought it}* This being a matter very remarkable, he mentions it again and again.

*{therefore he would not drink it}* He shows that he was far from desiring that they or any other should hazard themselves for it.

*{these things did these three mightiest}* The word mightiest being of the superlative degree shows that the forementioned exploit was acted by the first three worthies. See v. 15.

**V. 20.** *{And Abishai the brother of Joab}* See ch. 2:16. The relation, brother of Joab, is added for honor's sake. It was an honor to Abishai to have such a brother as Joab; and to Joab, to have such a brother as Abishai.

*{he was chief of the three}* I. e. of the three worthies of the second rank, whose famous acts follow.

*{For lifting up his spear against three hundred, he slew them}* See v. 11. Many memorable acts are recorded of Abishai both in the time of David's troubles and also of his reign, but this being most remarkable is here set down.

*{and had a name among the three}* To wit, of the second degree.

**V. 21.** *{Of the three he was more honourable than the two}* I. e. the two other of the second degree. He had a greater name, and was more honored by others than they.

*{for he was their captain}* The Hebrew word implies dignity or authority, or both: he might be more excellent in parts than the other two; or he might have a command over them, he being a colonel, and they lieutenant colonels or captains under him; or he might both be more excellent than they, and also have a command over them.

*{howbeit he attained not to the first three}* I. e. those of the first rank. They were more excellent in parts and more eminent in place than he.

**V. 22.** *{Benaiah the son of Jehoiada}* See 1 King. 1:8. This man served David all his reign, and served also his son Solomon many days, who made him general of the army in Joab's room, 1 King. 2:35.

*{the son of a valiant man}* This is added for the more commendation. It is the greater glory when both father and son be excellent in any commendable thing.

*{of Kabzeel}* One of the frontier cities of Judah, Josh. 15:21. In that respect the inhabitants were the more exercised in warlike employments.

*{who had done many acts}* Heb. *great deeds*. Some refer the relative who to Benaiah's father; but it is set down as an amplification of Benaiah's valor, *q. d.* Besides many other great deeds he did these things that follow.

*{he slew}* Heb. *he smote*. I. e. mortally. See 1 King. 15:29.

*{two lion-like men of Moab}* Heb. *Ariel*, that is, *the lions of God*, or *strong lions*. But questionless men are meant thereby; such men as were strong, fierce and cruel as lions. Some take them for two strong places called Ariel, which he overcame, and destroyed the enemies therein.

*{also he went down and slew a lion}* A lion is the strongest, fiercest, and most ravenous of all beasts.

*{in a pit}* Lions use to lie in dens and pits under the earth. This aggravates the danger: for in a pit he could neither have so much room to bestir himself as if it had been in an open field, nor so fair a way of escape.

*{in a snowy day}* There are two reasons of adding this circumstance; 1. The more to amplify his valor: for seeing in snowy weather beasts use not to be abroad, this lion must needs be the more hunger-bit at that time for want of prey, and in that respect the more fierce. 2. To show the occasion of his being in the pit. The ground was then covered with snow, so as Benaiah could not see the pit, but unawares fell into it; and there finding a lion, so defended himself that he slew the lion.

**V. 23.** *{And he slew an Egyptian}* In those days there were giants in sundry nations, as in Moab and Ammon, Deut. 2:11, 20, among the Philistims, 1 Sam. 17:4, and here among the Egyptians.

*{a man of great stature}* Heb. *a man of measure*. I. e. of extraordinary measure, far taller and bigger than other men. He is said to be *a goodly man*, 2 Sam. 23:21. Heb. *a man of countenance, or sight*. That Hebraism may imply a man of a terrible countenance as well as of a goodly countenance. It sets out such an one as every man's eye was upon, wondering at his great stature.

*{five cubits high}* I. e. two yards and an half. See 1 King. 6:2. This according to our measure was a very great height, but it is probable that cubits in those days were larger than now.

*{and in the Egyptian’s hand was a spear like a weaver’s beam}* A weaver's beam uses to be about twenty-four inches round. A spear so much in compass must needs be very long, for in such instruments there uses to be a proportion betwixt the length and breadth. Giants use to be set forth by this metaphor, as ch. 20:5; 1 Sam. 17:7. It is said of Goliath's spear, that *the head thereof weighed six hundred shekels of iron*, i. e. about five and twenty pound; the spear itself therefore must needs be a very massy one.

*{and he went down to him with a staff}* There was as great a disproportion betwixt the weapons, an ordinary staff and a huge spear, as betwixt the men, a man of ordinary stature and a giant. All this is to amplify Benaiah's valor.

*{and plucked the spear out of the Egyptian’s hand}* His courage made him not fear to grapple with a giant.

*{and slew him with his own spear}* As this was honorable to himself, so disgraceful to the giant: it was like David's cutting off Goliath's head with his own sword, 1 Sam. 17:51.

**V. 24.** *{These things did Benaiah the son of Jehoiada}* To wit, the three exploits before mentioned; but he did many memorable acts besides.

*{and had the name among the three mighties}* He was counted one of the three worthies in the second rank; and as the others, so he, had a famous report: but his name was not the greatest; for Abishai was the more honorable, v. 21.

**V. 25.** *{Behold, he was honourable among the thirty}* I. e. the thirty mentioned v. 26, etc. In relation to them we may say of him as it was said of Abishai, v. 21, *he was more honorable than they*.

*{but attained not to the first three}* To such a name and esteem as the three of the first rank had.

*{and David set him over his guard}* Heb. *over his hearing*. I. e. over such soldiers as were always present and attending at court, and so ready upon all occasions to execute the king's command. These are called Cherethites and Pelethites, 2 Sam. 8:18 and 20:23. See 1 King. 1:38. Mention has been made of twice three worthies; the former three more excellent than the latter: but there have only five persons with their valiant facts been set down: I. Jashobeam, v. 11, taken to be the same with Adino, 2 Sam. 23:8. II. Eleazar, v. 12. III. Shammah, supposed to be of the first rank, 2 Sam. 23:11. IV. Abishai, the first of the second rank, v. 20. V. Benaiah, v. 22. Many add to these Asahel, v. 26. But he is there said to be one of the valiant men of the armies. Now all the rest were commanders over these. If Shammah be put to the second rank, and joined with Abishai and Benaiah, the first rank may be made up two ways: 1. Jashobeam and Adino may be two persons, and Eleazar the third. 2. If the two former names belong to one person, Joab, whose worthy exploit is set down v. 6, may be the first of the three worthies: and thus the number of thirty-seven 2 Sam. 23:39 may be made complete: but without Joab there are but thirty-six.

**V. 26.** *{Also the valiant men of the armies}* I. e. the armies under the command of the six forementioned worthies. And the valiant men are those which follow, which are said to be thirty, 2 Sam. 23:24. There are one and thirty with Asahel named; whereupon many refer Asahel to the former worthies, and make him the last of them: but by this account there will want of the thirty and seven, 2 Sam. 23:39. But if there be six worthies reckoned beside Asahel, then the one and thirty following make up the just number of thirty-seven. And the number of thirty 2 Sam. 23:24 may be set down as a round number, one being left out: which is usual in sacred Scriptures. Compare Gen. 15:13 with Exod. 12:40.

*{Asahel the brother of Joab}* See 2 Sam. 2:18; ch. 27:7. It appears by the mention of this man that all these valiant men were with David before he came to the crown, and at the time of Saul's death; for Asahel was slain not long after, 2 Sam. 2:23.

*{Elhanan the son of Dodo of Bethlehem}* *Dodo* signifies *a friend*, Cant. 5:9, and is oft put for *an uncle*, Lev. 25:49; 1 Sam. 10:15. Some therefore here translate it appellatively, *q. d.* *the son of the uncle of Bethlehem*.

**V. 27.** *{Shammoth the Harorite}* Or, *Harodite*, 2 Sam. 23:25. He is so called of Harod, Judg. 7:1.

*{Helez the Pelonite}* Or, *Paltite*, 2 Sam. 23:26. Before this man is set down Elika the Harodite, 2 Sam. 23:25.

**V. 28.** *{Ira the son of Ikkesh the Tekoite}* He was of Tekoa, 2 Sam. 14:2; 2 Chr. 11:6; Amos 1:1.

*{Abiezer the Antothite}* There was also one Jehu of this family, ch. 12:3.

**V. 29.** *{Sibbecai}* Or, *Mebunnai*, 2 Sam. 23:27.

*{the Hushathite}* Of Hushah, of the tribe of Judah, ch. 4:4.

*{Ilai}* Or, *Zalmon*, 2 Sam. 23:28.

*{the Ahohite}* See v. 12.

**V. 30.** *{Maharai the Netophathite}* See ch. 25:4.

*{Heled}* Or, *Heleb*, 2 Sam. 23:29.

*{the son of Baanah the Netophathite}* Netophathite has relation to Heled; so as Heled and Maharai were both of one family.

**V. 31.** *{Ithai}* Or, *Ittai*, 2 Sam. 23:29.

*{the son of Ribai of Gibeah, that pertained to the children of Benjamin}* See Judg. 20:4.

*{Benaiah the Pirathonite}* Of Pirathon in the tribe of Ephraim, Judg. 12:15.

**V. 32.** *{Hurai}* Or, *Hiddai*, 2 Sam. 23:30.

*{of the brooks}* Or, *valleys*.

*{of Gaash}* A mountain in the tribe of Ephraim, Josh. 24:30.

*{Abiel}* Or, *Abialbon*, 2 Sam. 23:31.

*{the Arbathite}* Hebron was of old called Arba, Gen. 35:27; Josh. 14:15, and it may be this title Arbathite was thence raised.

**V. 33.** *{Azmaveth the Baharumite}* Or, *Barhumite*, 2 Sam. 23:31.

*{Eliahba the Shaalbonite}* Of Shaalabbin, Josh. 19:42.

**V. 34.** *{The sons of Hashem the Gizonite}* Or, *of the sons of Jashen*.

*{Jonathan the son of Shage the Hararite}* 2 Sam. 23:32. Jonathan was the son of Shage, of the family of the Hararites, but reckoned among the sons of Hashem the Gizonite. Shage was his immediate father, Hashem either his father-in-law, or one of his former predecessors.

**V. 35.** *{Ahiam the son of Sacar the Hararite}* *Harar* in Hebrew signifies *a mountain*. Thence it is inferred that they who are called Hararites came from the mountains of Ephraim. Before this man is inserted Shammah the Hararite, 2 Sam. 23:33. One of that name is reckoned among the chief worthies, 2 Sam. 23:25. But he was of another family, for he is styled the Harodite.

*{Eliphal the son of Ur}* Called *Eliphelet the son of Ahasbai, the son of the Maachathite*, 2 Sam. 23:34. He was of the Maachathites beyond Jordan, Josh. 13:11.

**V. 36.** *{Hepher the Mecherathite}* This man is not reckoned in the catalogue 2 Sam. 23:34.

*{Ahijah the Pelonite}* Called Eliam, 2 Sam. 23:34, and said to be *the son of Ahithophel the Gilonite*. This Ahithophel was counted a very wise statesman, but revolted from David, and took part with Absalom, 2 Sam. 15:12, 31 and 16:23.

**V. 37.** *{Hezro the Carmelite}* Or, *Hezrai*, 2 Sam. 23:35. He came from Carmel, a city of Judah, Josh. 15:52.

**V. 38.** *{Joel the brother of Nathan}* Called *Igal the son of Nathan of Zobah*, 2 Sam. 23:36. Both father and son might be called Nathan and Joel. Igal (for one man might have two names) the son of one Nathan, and brother of another.

*{Mibhar the son of Haggeri}* Or, *the Haggerite*. He is called *Bani the Gadite*, 2 Sam. 23:36. He might be of the tribe of Gad and of the family of Haggeri, and have two names.

**V. 39.** *{Zelek the Ammonite}* He might come from the Ammonites, and yet be incorporated among the Israelites.

*{Naharai the Berothite}* Of Beeroth, a city of Benjamin, Josh. 18:25.

*{the armourbearer of Joab the son of Zeruiah}* Joab being a valiant man, would have such as were of his spirit to attend him.

**V. 40.** *{Ira the Ithrite}* Of Jattir a city in Judah, Josh. 15:48.

*{Gareb the Ithrite}* These two last were of the same family.

**V. 41.** *{Uriah the Hittite}* 2 Sam. 11:3, etc. Here ends the catalogue 2 Sam. 23:8, etc. thirty and seven in all. See v. 26.

*{Zabad the son of Ahlai}* This is taken to be that Zabad whose pedigree is registered ch. 2:37. His father might have two names; or his father be named in one place, and his grandfather in another. He may be added to the former catalogue instead of Elika left out. See v. 27.

**V. 42.** *{Adina the son of Shiza the Reubenite}* This Adina and the rest that follow were captains in the tribes of Reuben, Gad, and Manasseh beyond Jordan, in the confines of those tribes, and therefore are added to the rest.

*{a captain of the Reubenites}* He had a special command over such soldiers as were of the tribe of Reuben.

*{and thirty with him}* This may be taken of thirty captains of the tribe of Reuben; he being their colonel. Or it may be thus read, *but the thirty were over him*, i. e. the thirty before-mentioned were more excellent than he.

**V. 43.** *{Hanan the son of Maacah}* There was a Benjamite called Hanan, ch. 8:23. He might be this man. The family of the Maachathites, 2 Sam. 23:34, may arise from one named Maacah.

*{and Joshaphat the Mithnite}* This family is here only mentioned.

**V. 44.** *{Uzzia the Ashterathite}* This Uzzia may be he whose son was over King David's treasures, ch. 27:25.

*{Shama and Jehiel the sons of Hothan}* It was the greater honor to Hothan that two of his sons should be such worthies. So were Abishai, Joab, and Asahel an honor to their mother Zeruiah, ch. 2:16. See the like v. 45, 46.

*{the Aroerite}* There was a place called Aroer, ch. 5:8. From thence might the name of his family be taken.

**V. 45.** *{Jediael}* One of Benjamin's sons carried this name, ch. 7:6.

*{the son of Shimri}* Or, *Shimrite*.

*{and Joha his brother, the Tizite}* See v. 44.

**V. 46.** *{Eliel the Mahavite}* Another of David's worthies was called Eliel, v. 47. But this man's family distinguishes him from the other. There is also mention made of this name ch. 8:20, 22.

*{and Jeribai, and Joshaviah, the sons of Elnaam}* See v. 44.

*{and Ithmah the Moabite}* That which was said of the Ammonite v. 39 may be applied to this Moabite.

**V. 47.** *{Eliel}* See v. 46.

*{and Obed}* Besides Obed the father of Jesse, Ruth 4:22, there was another also of the tribe of Judah, ch. 2:37, 38.

*{and Jasiel the Mesobaite}* We read nowhere else of this family.

## CHAPTER 12

**Ver. 1.** *{Now these are they that came to David to Ziklag}* In the former chapter are recorded such as settled David in his throne after the house of Saul was utterly ruined: here is a catalogue of such as came to him in his troubles, when he was forced to fly from Saul. These were some of those six hundred mentioned 1 Sam. 27:2, 6. Of Ziklag see ch. 4:30.

*{while he yet kept himself close}* Heb. *being yet shut up*. Or, *being yet excluded from his own country and from the house of God*; whereof he much complains Psal. 84:1, etc.; 1 Sam. 26:19.

*{because of Saul the son of Kish}* See ch. 8:33. Saul so persecuted David as he was forced to hide himself in caves and rocks and mountains, 1 Sam. 22:1 and 24:3 and 23:25, 26.

*{and they were among the mighty men}* They were for valor and courage to be reckoned in the number of those whose catalogue is set down in the former chapter.

*{helpers of the war}* This may have relation to the wars betwixt the house of Saul and David, yea and to other wars also.

**V. 2.** *{They were armed with bows}* They were archers and very skillful in shooting. See ch. 5:18.

*{and could use both the right hand and the left}* The Benjamites were trained up of old to use both hands, Judg. 20:16, whereby they had great advantage against an enemy.

*{in hurling stones}* See Judg. 20:16. Hereby David overthrew Goliath, 1 Sam. 17:49. Uzziah's men were trained up hereunto, 2 Chron. 26:14. The force of slinging stones is intimated Job 41:28.

*{and shooting arrows out of a bow}* See ch. 5:18.

*{even of Saul’s brethren of Benjamin}* The last word shows that they were of Saul's tribe, 1 Sam. 9:1, 2, the other, that they were also of his kindred and alliance. And this much amplifies their humanity and kindness to David. They observed that Saul unjustly persecuted him, and hereupon pitying his case, they went to succor him, notwithstanding those near bonds of relation wherewith they were knit to Saul.

**V. 3.** *{The chief was Ahiezer}* He was the head or captain of those who are called Saul's brethren v. 2.

*{then Joash, the sons of Shemaah}* Or, *Hashmaa*. Joash and Ahiezer were brethren, therefore it is said sons, in the plural number.

*{the Gibeathite}* Gibeath was a city of Benjamin, Josh. 18:28, thence came Shemaah.

*{and Jeziel, and Pelet}* These two also were brethren.

*{the sons of Azmaveth}* Azmaveth is reckoned up in the genealogy of Benjamin, ch. 8:36, and among David's worthies, ch. 11:33.

*{and Berachah, and Jehu the Antothite}* This note of the family, Antothite, has reference to Berachah as well as to Jehu.

**V. 4.** *{And Ismaiah the Gibeonite}* He was of the strangers which made peace with the Israelites, Josh. 9:8, etc.

*{a mighty man among the thirty}* I. e. the thirty captains which came out of Benjamin to David; many of which are here named.

*{and over the thirty}* They were captains over other soldiers, but he was as a colonel over them.

*{and Jeremiah, etc.}* With Ismaiah and Jeremiah there are seventeen Benjamites set down, v. 4, 5, 6, 7, and there are six others v. 3, in all twenty-three. The other seven, which should make up the thirty, are not set down.

*{Josabad the Gederathite}* Of Gederah a city of Judah Josh. 15:36. Seeing Judah and Benjamin joined together, no marvel that this family arising from a city of Judah is reckoned among the Benjamites.

**V. 5.** *{And Shephatiah the Haruphite}* This was a family in the tribe of Benjamin.

**V. 6.** *{The Korhites}* The families of the Korhites came from Kore who descended from Levi, ch. 9:19; Exod. 6:24. But there might also be one of the tribe of Benjamin called Kor, or Korah, and from him this family might come. Or else by affinity with that family of Levites this family of Benjamin might be called Korhites. The plural number here used shows that the five mentioned immediately before were all Korhites.

**V. 7.** *{Jeroham of Gedor}* See ch. 4:18.

**V. 8.** *{And of the Gadites there separated themselves unto David}* They departed from the other Israelites that adhered unto Saul, and took part with David.

*{into the hold, to the wilderness}* This hold was Ziklag, v. 1, a city in the desert of Judah; or the cave of Adullam, 1 Sam. 22:1, 2, or Engedi, 1 Sam. 22:29, or some other strong place to which David betook himself, 1 Sam. 23:19, 25.

*{men of might}* I. e. of great bodily strength and courage of mind.

*{and men of war}* Heb. *of the hosts*. Such as had been trained up to war, able to stand against an host of enemies.

*{fit for the battle}* Fit to order a battle.

*{that could handle shield and buckler}* I. e. warlike weapons. See v. 14.

*{whose faces were like the faces of lions}* I. e. they manifested much courage, fierceness and terror in their countenance.

*{and were as swift as the roes upon the mountains}* Heb. *as the roes upon the mountains to make haste*. An hyperbolical expression of more than ordinary swiftness. This commendation was given to Asahel, 2 Sam. 2:18. Some take the word translated roes for *goats*; which very readily and swiftly climb over high rocks: so as hereby is implied that these Gadites could easily scale high walls, forts or towers, and soon take them.

**V. 9.** *{Ezer the first, etc.}* There are eleven in all.

**V. 14.** *{These were of the sons of Gad}* I. e. the eleven aforesaid.

*{Captains of the host}* Such as had command over several companies of soldiers.

*{one of the least was over an hundred, and the greatest over a thousand}* Or, *one that was least could resist an hundred, and the greatest a thousand*. The former reading shows the great command they had: some of them were over companies of an hundred soldiers, others over regiments consisting of a thousand soldiers, whom we call colonels. The latter reading sets out their valor: and therein is accomplished that which God promised Deut. 32:30; Josh. 23:10.

**V. 15.** *{These are they that went over Jordan}* The tribe of Gad having their inheritance beyond Jordan, these must needs come over, to help David who was on this side.

*{in the first month}* Called Abib, Exod. 12:2. See 1 King. 6:1.

*{when it had overflown all his banks}* See Josh. 3:15 and 4:18, 19; Jer. 12:5. This is here set down to amplify their courage and settled resolution. Nothing could hinder them from coming to David.

*{and they put to flight all them of the valleys}* We heard before, how the Philistims having slain Saul and his sons, and routed his whole army, the Israelites whose habitations were in the valley fled, and the Philistims came and dwelt in their cities and towns, ch. 19:7. It may be these were they whom the Gadites put to flight. Or it may be this was before that victory, and Saul having appointed some garrisons in those valleys purposely to keep all on the other side Jordan from coming to aid David, these Gadites put them to flight.

*{both toward the east, and toward the west}* Every where from one end of the valleys to the other.

**V. 16.** *{And there came of the children of Benjamin and Judah}* These were other Benjamites than they v. 2, not so nearly linked to Saul. These also bordered nearer upon Judah than they, and therefore joined with the men of Judah. This they did the rather to gain the more credit and better respect with David, and to be the less suspected.

*{to the hold unto David}* See v. 8.

**V. 17.** *{And David went out to meet them}* Heb. *before them*. Or, *before their face*.

*{and answered and said unto them, If ye be come peaceably}* Heb. *for peace*.

*{unto me to help me}* David being persecuted by his sovereign had need of much help. If therefore for that end they came, he would not refuse them.

*{mine heart shall be knit unto you}* Heb. *shall be one with you*. Or, *my heart shall be together upon you*. My heart shall be towards you with an entire affection, as yours is towards me.

*{but if ye be come to betray me to mine enemies}* I. e. to discover where I am, or to take me and carry me to Saul and such as joining with him account me their enemy and seek my life.

*{seeing there is no wrong}* Or, *violence*.

*{in mine hands}* He means that he had done no wrong at all to Saul, or to any of the rest that persecuted him.

*{the God of our fathers}* David aggravates their treachery (if they intended any) by that common relation which was betwixt them. They all were children descending from the same fathers and professing the same God.

*{look thereon}* David seeing himself unable to right such a wrong calls God to take notice thereof.

*{and rebuke it}* I. e. manifest his displeasure against it, and revenge the wrong you do me. So Zech. 3:2; Jude 9.

**V. 18.** *{Then the spirit came upon Amasai}* Heb. *the spirit clothed Amasai*. See Judg. 6:34. Hereby is meant the Spirit of the Lord, which put a spirit of courage and a kind of life into him, that made him speak with such boldness and prudence as he did. The zeal and efficacy of that spirit was such as it exceedingly wrought upon David, and made him rest upon what Amasai said.

*{who was chief of the captains}* I. e. general over them all. This is he who in other places is called Amasa; whom Absalom made general of his army, 2 Sam. 17:25, and whom David intended to make general in the room of Joab, 2 Sam. 19:13.

*{Thine are we, David}* He speaks in his own name and in the name of those that came with him: for they had all consulted and consented together to hold with David.

*{and on thy side, thou son of Jesse}* See ch. 10:14.

*{Peace}* 1 Sam. 25:6. Under this word peace all manner of prosperity and good success is comprised. See 1 Sam. 25:6; Luke 10:5.

*{peace be unto thee}* This doubling of the phrase adds much emphasis. See 1 King. 8:13.

*{and peace be to thine helpers}* He wishes good success not only to David's person, but also to all on his side; and thereby gives him the more assurance that they would be true to him.

*{for thy God helpeth thee}* Amasai and others had observed how God had every way prospered David. By adding this he shows that with confidence he uttered his desire of peace to David.

*{Then David received them}* He gave such credit to him, as he received him, and them that came with him, into his army.

*{and made them captains of the band}* He so far trusted them as he gave every of them a place of command in his army. Or, *he set them among the captains of his bands*.

**V. 19.** *{And there fell some of Manasseh to David}* These are other than they who are set down v. 20.

*{when he came with the Philistines against Saul to battle}* 1 Sam. 29:2, etc.

*{but they helped them not}* David and his men helped not the Philistims.

*{for the lords of the Philistines}* They who had command in the several principalities of the Philistims, under King Achish.

*{upon advisement}* For there was a conference betwixt Achish and the lords about David's going with them, 1 Sam. 29:3.

*{sent him away, saying}* Though the king put confidence in David, and was willing that he should go with them, yet the lords were much displeased thereat, and would not suffer it.

*{He will fall to his master Saul}* When the battle comes to be set, and the fight begun, he will run from our side to Saul's, and take part with him against us.

*{to the jeopardy of our heads}* Heb. *on our heads*. He will fall upon us, and we thereby shall be in danger to lose our lives. By parting head and body one from another, the life is lost.

**V. 20.** *{As he went to Ziklag}* See v. 1. After David was dismissed from going with the Philistims against Saul, he went to Ziklag, and finding it sacked and burnt, he pursued the Amalekites, that did it, and destroyed them. After that, he returned to Ziklag, 1 Sam. 30:1, 26.

*{there fell to him of Manasseh}* Then, when he returned from the Philistims. Thus some of Manasseh fell to him before he went from the Philistims, v. 19, and others afterwards.

*{Adnah, etc.}* With these seven there came sundry others.

*{captains of the thousands that were of Manasseh}* This shows they were all colonels over regiments: for a regiment uses to consist of a thousand soldiers.

**V. 21.** *{And they helped David against the band of the rovers}* Or, *with a band*. These words, *of the rovers*, are not in the Hebrew. They who inserted them in the translation suppose the forementioned Manassites came to David before he set upon the Amalekites, and joining with him helped him against them. But the other reading, *they helped David with a band*, does only imply, and that indefinitely, that they increased David's army, and were an help unto him: but it has no special relation to any particular battle.

*{for they were all mighty men of valour}* See ch. 5:24 and 7:2, 40.

*{and were captains in the host}* They were captains before they came to David, and David made them captains in his army.

**V. 22.** *{For at that time, day by day, there came to David to help him}* Now was the time come that David should be set upon the throne, therefore God inclined the hearts of the people daily more and more to come in to him.

*{until it was a great host}* Until such a number of valiant men assembled together and put themselves into military companies as made a very great army.

*{like the host of God}* Such an host as God by inclining their hearts to David had brought together. Or rather this phrase, *host of God*, is an Hebraism whereby the excellency of the host is set forth. For excellent things in Scripture are said to be the things of God; whether the excellency be in worth, royalty, greatness, tallness, loudness, fruitfulness, largeness, number, or any other matter: as, a worthy prince, *Prince of God*, Gen. 23:6, a glorious throne, *a throne of the Lord*, ch. 29:23; Jer. 3:17, a great mountain, *hill of God*, Psal. 36:6, tall cedars, *cedars of God*, Psal. 80:10, high trees, *trees of the Lord*, Psal. 104:16, a loud voice, *the voice of God*, Ezek. 10:5; Exod. 9:28, a fruitful or pleasant garden, *the garden of the Lord*, Gen. 13:10, a large and fair city, *a city of God*, Psal. 46:4; Jon. 3:3, a numerous army, *an host of God*, Gen. 32:2. So here in this place.

**V. 23.** *{And these are the numbers}* Whereas he had said ch. 11:1 that *all Israel gathered themselves to David*, to settle him on the throne, from this place to v. 39 he sets down the number of those who out of the several tribes came to him.

*{of the bands}* Or, *captains, or men*. Heb. *heads*. The Hebrew word v. 3 is translated *chief*. And being taken for such as were commanders in war, it here comprehends, with them, the several companies that they brought.

*{that were ready armed to the war}* Or, *every way prepared thereto*. They came with their armor, weapons, and ammunition.

*{and came to David to Hebron}* See ch. 3:1; 11:3.

*{to turn the kingdom of Saul unto him}* See ch. 10:14.

*{according to the word of the LORD}* See ch. 11:3.

**V. 24.** *{The children of Judah that bare shield and spear}* That were trained up to the war, and able to manage military weapons. For all sorts of warlike instruments are synecdochically comprised under these two, shield and spear; the one whereof was especially for defense, the other for offense. See v. 8.

*{were six thousand and eight hundred}* These were but few in comparison of the number which came out of some other tribes, v. 33, 36. But the men of Judah came most of them to David before the other tribes came, and anointed him king, 2 Sam. 2:4. So as these were of the backwarder sort.

*{ready armed to the war}* Or, *prepared to the war*. See v. 23.

**V. 25.** *{Of the children of Simeon}* This tribe lay within Judah, and thereupon is set next to it, ch. 4:34.

*{mighty men of valour}* See v. 21.

*{for the war}* Men may have courage of mind and strength of body, and yet not be fit for the war, unless they had been exercised thereto. But these Simeonites were both able and fit to wage war.

*{seven thousand and one hundred}* They being as one tribe with Judah, might, for the most part of them, do as their brethren of Judah did. See v. 24.

**V. 26.** *{Of the children of Levi}* Though this tribe were set apart to the service of the Lord, yet many of them being mighty men of valor, as other Israelites, did oft in God's cause use weapons of war for executing of God's judgments and accomplishing his will. See Exod. 32:27, 28; Num. 25:7, 8; 2 King. 11:9, etc. Now David being ordained by God to be king, the Levites would help to establish him in the kingdom.

*{four thousand and six hundred}* Many of this tribe might be as forward to crown David as the men of Judah were, 2 Sam. 2:4. For when Judah alone cleaved to the house of David upon the revolt of the ten tribes, the Levites also joined with the men of Judah, 2 Chron. 11:13, 14. This may be the cause that so few of them are here reckoned; they being a numerous tribe, as appears by their genealogy, ch. 6. Besides there are more reckoned up, v. 27.

**V. 27.** *{And Jehoiada was the leader of the Aaronites}* Though this Jehoiada were a priest, and here said to be the leader of the Aaronites, yet was he not the high priest: for he is not reckoned in their catalogue, ch. 6:4, etc. And Abiathar had that place till Solomon put him out of it, 1 King. 2:35, and was the first priest that came to David in his troubles, 1 Sam. 22:20 and 23:6. But this Jehoiada was a conductor of such priests as are here said to come to David; and it may be he was a captain over them. Aaronites descending from Aaron, were priests.

*{and with him were three thousand and seven hundred}* These must be added to the former number of the Levites v. 26, and not swallowed up therein. The two numbers joined together make eight thousand three hundred of that tribe.

**V. 28.** *{And Zadok, a young man}* This questionless was that Zadok who all David's time was joined with Abiathar to be principal priest, 2 Sam. 8:17; 20:25, who also was by Solomon put into Abiathar's room, 1 King. 2:35. He being then at least thirty-three years after this, (see 2 Sam. 5:5.) put into the other's room, was but young at this time.

*{mighty of valour}* See v. 21. His priestly function was no hindrance to the strength of his body or courage of his mind.

*{and of his father’s house twenty and two captains}* These were priests also, as well as Zadok; in which respect they are said to be of his father's house: so as he and they came with the other children of Levi to David. See v. 26.

**V. 29.** *{Of the children of Benjamin}* Though they bordered upon Judah, yet because Saul was of their tribe, and they were not willing to let the royal dignity go from them, therefore they were the more backward in coming to David.

*{the kindred of Saul}* Heb. *brethren of Saul*. See v. 2.

*{three thousand}* It was a greater wonder that so many of Saul's kindred would come, than that so few came.

*{for hitherto the greatest part of them}* Heb. *multitude of them*. Them has reference to the children of Benjamin indefinitely.

*{had kept the ward of the house of Saul}* Had stood to continue the kingdom in Saul's stock, and did what they could to maintain it.

**V. 30.** *{And of the children of Ephraim, twenty thousand and eight hundred}* This number far exceeded any of the former. And there were special reasons given why Judah, Simeon, Levi, and Benjamin brought no more. Yet a less tribe than Ephraim, v. 33, brought more than as many again. Ephraim was the greatest tribe in multitude, except Judah; and herein he answered his name, which signifies fruitful, Gen. 41:52. So as every thing duly considered, this number of twenty thousand and eight hundred was no great commendation to Ephraim.

*{mighty men of valour}* See v. 21. This and that which follows is a commendation of those of Ephraim which came to David.

*{famous}* Heb. *men of names*. Such as had done valorous acts, and thereupon had got a great name and good report.

*{throughout the house of their fathers}* In their several families; or, through all the tribe of Ephraim.

**V. 31.** *{And of the half-tribe of Manasseh}* I. e. that part which bordered upon Ephraim in the land of Canaan.

*{eighteen thousand}* This was a great multitude for half a tribe. Of the other half see v. 37.

*{which were expressed by name}* I. e. they were chosen out by the rest of the half-tribe, and set down by name.

*{to come and make David king}* This was it for which they were sent. See ch. 11:3.

**V. 32.** *{And of the children of Issachar}* This was one of the smallest tribes.

*{which were men that had understanding of the times}* It appears that they and their progenitors had much given themselves to observe seasons wherein matters of moment were meetest to be done; especially for husbandry. For according to the prediction of their father, Gen. 49:15, they enjoyed a fruitful land, and quietly lived therein; not giving themselves to war, but rather to tilling of the ground. By this means they might the better observe the course of the stars, the conjunctions of planets, and the influences that descended from thence. Besides, they being given, generation after generation, to observe occurrences of matters, might get much experience, and thereby much wisdom; and that not only in matters of husbandry, but also of state and war. See Esth. 1:13.

*{to know what Israel ought to do}* That understanding which they got was not for mere speculation, but to instruct others, and in weighty matters to direct any of the other tribes: who are comprised under this word, Israel.

*{the heads of them were two hundred}* By heads he means captains and commanders: of whom there being two hundred, there must needs be divers thousands under them.

*{and all their brethren}* All their tribe, or their several families.

*{were at their commandment}* Their prudence was so highly esteemed, that all that were under them, or any way belonged to them, or came to them for advice, were ready to do what they enjoined or advised.

**V. 33.** *{Of Zebulun}* This tribe was situated by the sea, and according to their father's prediction gave themselves to fishing, and other callings belonging to mariners; yet, as other seamen, they were stout warriors.

*{such as went forth to battle}* They could not but meet with many occasions of war by sea; yet by land also they exercised themselves in warlike employment.

*{expert in war}* Or, *rangers of battle, or, ranged in battle*. By exercising military arts they got great experience, and were thereby the better fitted to war.

*{with all instruments of war}* Such as were mentioned v. 24, shield and spear, yea and sword, bows, arrows, bills, and such other as were then in use.

*{fifty thousand}* This is the greatest number that came out of any one tribe.

*{which could keep rank}* Or, *set the battle in array*. This according to the second reading is a singular commendation, that so many thousands out of one tribe should be able to order an army. According to the former reading it implies that the fifty thousand had been exercised in training.

*{they were not of a double heart}* Heb. *without a heart and a heart*. This Hebraism *heart and heart* signifies *a deceitful heart*, Psal. 12:2. The negative therefore implies sincerity and singleness of heart. Men exercised in war may be upright and sincere in their dealings.

**V. 34.** *{And of Naphtali a thousand captains}* This is noted as a matter of commendation, that they had not only multitudes of soldiers, but also a thousand fit to be commanders in war.

*{and with them}* Or, *under them*.

*{with shield and spear}* Such as bare shields and spears, v. 24.

*{thirty and seven thousand}* This was the greatest number but that of Zebulun, v. 33, and that of Asher, v. 36.

**V. 35.** *{And of the Danites}* The tribe of Dan was the least of all the tribes.

*{expert in war}* See v. 33.

*{twenty and eight thousand and six hundred}* These were many out of so small a tribe.

**V. 36.** *{And of Asher, such as went forth to battle}* Who were so exercised in military discipline as they were fit to be sent out to war.

*{expert in war}* Or, *keeping their ranks*. This cannot all do that are not trained up thereto. This therefore is a commendation.

*{forty thousand}* This tribe afforded the greatest number next to Zebulun, v. 33.

**V. 37.** *{And on the other side of Jordan}* Num. 32:33.

*{of the Reubenites, and the Gadites, and of the half-tribe of Manasseh}* Here as in most of the verses before, the principal verb is to be understood, and to be fetched from v. 23, namely this, came, *q. d.* *there came of the Reubenites*, etc.

*{with all manner of instruments of war for the battle}* See v. 33.

*{an hundred and twenty thousand}* These two tribes and half sent out rate for rate as many as any of the other tribes, except only Zebulun.

**V. 38.** *{All these men of war}* See v. 8.

*{that could keep rank}* See v. 33.

*{came with a perfect heart}* An upright and sincere heart. They were all such as the men of Zebulun are set forth to be, v. 33.

*{to Hebron to make David king over all Israel}* See v. 23 and ch. 11:3, and on 1 Sam. 27:1.

*{and all the rest also of Israel}* Namely, such as tarried at home to till the land, to follow other needful callings, to look to affairs of state, and to household affairs, that went not out to war.

*{were of one heart to make David king}* God mightily wrought upon them, that they should all so agree together, and be, as it is noted of the first Christians, *of one heart and of one soul*, Act. 4:32.

*{to make David king}* This made much to the honor and security of David, and to the good and peace of the whole kingdom.

**V. 39.** *{And there}* Namely, in Hebron, v. 23.

*{they were with David three days}* It was usual with the Jews to spend more than a day in their public rejoicings upon great and weighty occasions. Solomon spent seven days, besides the seven days of the feast of Tabernacles, in rejoicing at the dedication of the temple, 1 King. 8:65.

*{eating and drinking}* He means such a liberal eating and drinking as is ordinarily called feasting. See Job 1:4, 18.

*{for their brethren had prepared for them}* The word brethren is here taken in a large extent, namely, for Israelites; because all the Israelites came from one father, Jacob. So Rom. 9:3. Now here are meant such Israelites as dwelt in Hebron; who prepared for all that came thither from every of the tribes. Some take brethren for such in every tribe as were of any kin to those captains and soldiers who came to David, and make the sense to be this, that the kinsmen of every of the captains in their several tribes prepared provision for them, and sent it to Hebron. By this means the greater provision might be made.

**V. 40.** *{Moreover they that were nigh them}* Some expound this of kindred and alliance; others, of place and habitation: which seems more agreeable to the words following.

*{even unto Issachar, and Zebulun, and Naphtali}* These tribes were towards the sea, and far from Hebron: so as it is here implied, that provision was brought from far and near.

*{brought bread on asses}* See on ch. 5:20.

*{and on camels}* See 1 King. 10:2.

*{and on mules}* See 1 King. 10:25.

*{and on oxen}* These beasts did use to draw carts and wagons, Numb. 7:3; 2 Sam. 8:3, 6. So as they had horse-loads and cartloads (as we speak) of all manner of provision.

*{and meat, meal}* Or, *victuals of meal*. Sundry kinds of dainties were made of meal; some baken in the oven, some in a pan, or on a flat plate or slice, or in the frying-pan. See Lev. 2:4, 5, 7.

*{cakes of figs}* For they used so to press figs together as to make round cakes of them. See 1 Sam. 25:18.

*{and bunches of raisins}* Bunches of raisins were oft sent for presents to eat, 2 Sam. 16:1. They are called *clusters of raisins* 1 Sam. 25:18.

*{and wine}* This was an usual drink in those countries, especially at feasts, 1 Sam. 10:3 and 25:18; 2 Sam. 6:19; Neh. 5:15, 18.

*{and oil}* They did use not only to anoint themselves with oil, Ezek. 16:9, but also mixed their flour and meal therewith, 1 King. 17:12. See 2 King. 4:2.

*{and oxen}* These oxen were for another use than they which were mentioned before. They were to draw carriages, these to be slain for meat.

*{and sheep abundantly}* These also were for meat.

*{for there was joy in Israel}* Joy, that they had another king for Saul that was slain. Joy, that they had such a king as David. Joy, that their civil wars were ended. Joy, that God's promise to David was accomplished. Joy, for that hope they had of a well-settled peace. Joy, for that hope they had of getting the better over all their enemies. Joy, for that union and unity that was among them. And joy, for that assurance they had of enjoying true religion and freedom of all God's ordinances. These were matters of great joy.

## CHAPTER 13

**Ver. 1.** *{And David consulted}* It was a weighty work that David here intended, and therefore he takes great advice about it. See Prov. 15:22 and 20:18 and 24:6. This consultation about settling the ark might be before the histories in ch. 14, though those histories be set down before the settling of the ark, 2 Sam. 5:11, etc. For the penman of that book of Samuel sets down the whole history together, namely the first attempt of David, upon which Uzzah was slain, and the perfecting of his intent, in settling the ark in the city of David; and therefore he sets down the histories of Hiram's sending to David, of David's taking more wives, and of his two victories before his first attempt about the ark, because they fell out before he had finished his intent. But those histories happened after the death of Uzzah, and before his settling of the ark in the city of David; and therefore they are inserted in their due place, ch. 14:1, etc. It much amplifies David's holy zeal, that so soon as ever he was settled on his throne his first care was about the ark of God.

*{with the captains of thousands}* I. e. colonels. See v. 20.

*{and hundreds}* These we call captains over companies; for a company consists of an hundred.

*{and with every leader}* So as none that had any command was excepted.

*{and David said unto all the congregation of Israel}* Under this title congregation more are comprised than the commanders. See 1 King. 8:5.

*{If it seem good unto you}* He so referred the case unto them that if they could show any just cause to the contrary he would no further prosecute his purpose.

*{and that it be of the LORD}* Though he and they both should well like the purpose, yet if it should appear that it were not agreeable to God's will, he would not go on therein.

*{our God}* See 1 King. 8:28. This word our implies both a restraint and an extent; a restraint excluding all that are not of the church; an extent, including all that are in the church. See Joh. 20:17.

*{let us send abroad}* Heb. *let us break forth and send*. The metaphor is taken from overflowing waters. They were now in the city, but they would send for those that were without. Yea, it further implies, that they would send speedy messengers abroad.

*{unto our brethren}* Though David was a king, yet he accounts all his subjects, who came from the same father Israel, to be his brethren.

*{everywhere}* In every part and corner of the land.

*{that are left in all the land of Israel}* For the Philistims had slain many in the last battle against Saul, and had driven many others out of the land.

*{and with them also to the priests and Levites}* To them especially belonged the ordering of the ark, and they could give best counsel thereabouts.

*{which are in their cities and suburbs}* Heb. *in the cities of their suburbs*. All their cities were compassed about with suburbs, and in this respect they might be called cities of their suburbs, or cities to which suburbs belonged.

*{that they may gather themselves unto us}* He took it for granted that so soon as they should have notice of his purpose they would come unto him.

**V. 3.** *{And let us bring again}* Heb. *bring out*. The ark was wont to be carried about; and in relation thereunto this phrase may be used.

*{the ark of our God}* See 1 King. 6:19 and 8:4.

*{for we}* That which was the common fault of all the people he charges upon himself; as Ezra 9:7, etc.; Dan. 8:5, etc.

*{enquired not at it in the days of Saul}* In Saul's time the ark was in a private place, 1 Sam. 11:12. Though the tabernacle was at a more public place, namely at Gibeon: whereof see 1 King. 3:4; ch. 16:39 and 21:29. To Gibeon, where was the great altar for burnt-offerings, the people frequently went, and contented themselves therewith, not regarding the ark, the principal evidence of God's presence, whereat also divine oracles and directions were given, Exod. 25:22 and 30:6, 36. He makes mention of the days of Saul, because then people grew most careless of God's worship, and many of the priests were then slain.

**V. 4.** *{And all the congregation}* All that were then present. They declared their minds by some of the chief among them.

*{said, that they would do so}* This shows their ready and willing submission to the king's will.

*{for the thing was right in the eyes of all the people}* It seemed good and right to them, both in regard of the matter itself that was moved, and also in that the motion came first from the king: for whatsoever the king did, pleased all the people, 2 Sam. 3:36.

**V. 5.** *{So David gathered all Israel together}* The four former verses are set down as a preparation to that act which follows. That preparation is omitted 2 Sam. 6:1, and the act directly fallen upon. This assembly of Israel was after David had sent his messengers abroad unto them, v. 2.

*{from Shihor of Egypt}* Oft called, as a river well known, *the river of Egypt*, Numb. 34:5; Josh. 15:41, 47.

*{even unto the entering of Hemath}* From south to north. See 1 King. 8:65. The number of all that met was thirty thousand, 2 Sam. 6:1.

*{to bring the ark of God from Kirjath-jearim}* Of Kiriath-jearim see ch. 2:53. The ark was first set in Shiloh, when the Israelites were settled in Canaan, Josh. 18:1. There it continued till it was taken by the Philistims, 1 Sam. 4:4, 11. They held it but seven months, 1 Sam. 6:1. From them it was brought to Bethshemesh, 1 Sam. 6:15, where it continued not long: for there being a great slaughter amongst the Bethshemites, because they had looked into it, it was removed to Kiriath-jearim, 1 Sam. 6:19, 21. All this was within the space of that one year in which Eli died. So as the ark continued in Kiriath-jearim nine and thirty years in the days of Samuel and Saul, Act. 13:21, and seven years in the days of David. For it was the year wherein David began to reign over Israel when he removed the ark from Kiriath-jearim, 2 Sam. 5:5. In this respect it is said, while the ark abode in Kiriath-jearim, *the time was long*, 1 Sam. 7:2. Whereas it is added that *it was twenty years*, those twenty years are the space betwixt the first taking of the ark by the Philistims and the Israelites' solemn repentance, 1 Sam. 7:3, etc.

**V. 6.** *{And David went up, and all Israel, to Baalah, that is, to Kirjath-jearim}* Kiriath-jearim and Baalah were names of one and the same city, Josh. 15:9, 29. It was also called Kiriath-Baal, Josh. 15:9, 29.

*{which belonged to Judah}* See Josh. 18:14; Judg. 18:12.

*{to bring up thence the ark}* See v. 5.

*{of God the LORD}* See 1 King. 18:21, 39.

*{that dwelleth between the cherubims}* See 2 King. 19:15.

*{whose name is called on it}* Or, *whose name is called by the name of the Lord of hosts*, 2 Sam. 6:2: (for the title Jehovah is given to the ark, Numb. 10:35, 36.) Or, *at which the name, even the name of the Lord of hosts, was called upon*. Or, *which is called the ark of the Lord*, 1 Sam. 4:6.

**V. 7.** *{And they carried the ark of God}* Heb. *made the ark of God to ride*. For that which is carried in a cart or wagon is said to ride.

*{in a new cart}* The word translated cart signifies any thing that is carried upon round wheels; the notation of the word being taken from round. It is translated *wagon*, Gen. 46:19; Numb. 7:3. So as this might be a wagon or a chariot as well as a cart.

*{out of the house of Abinadab}* Abinadab was of the tribe of Levi; and therefore the ark was left at his house. It is added, 2 Sam. 6:3, *that was in Gibeah*; or, *the hill*. Gibeah is taken for the proper name of a city in the tribe of Benjamin, Judg. 20:4. But Kiriath-jearim being in Judah, and Gibeah signifying *an hill*, it may imply an high place or hill whereon the house of Abinadab was built in Kiriath-jearim.

*{and Uzza and Ahio}* Sons of Abinadab, 2 Sam. 6:3.

*{drave the cart}* Or, *wagon*. They had the charge of attending the ark, and therefore took care of the carriage. It is said 2 Sam. 6:4 that *Ahio went before the ark*, namely to look to the oxen that drew the wagon; as his brother Uzza went behind to look to the ark.

**V. 8.** *{And David and all Israel played}* They danced and sang as they went along with the ark.

*{before God}* As in the presence of God; in uprightness of heart; to the honor of God. Or, *before the ark*, which was the manifestation of God's presence, ch. 16:1. See 2 King. 19:14.

*{with all their might}* Earnestly, zealously.

*{and with singing}* Heb. *songs*. Vocal music is here meant, men, women, children, and of all sorts singing together. Hereunto also they added instrumental music, and that both with wind and hand, as the particular instruments here mentioned do show.

*{and with harps}* An harp is a musical instrument somewhat large, broad at the top, and narrow at the bottom. It has many strings of wire, but some longer than others, which are struck with the fingers of such as play thereon.

*{and with psalteries}* A psaltery is also a stringed instrument, having strings almost all of the same length, and straight down. It has ten pins for pegs on the top, and as many strings: so as this is taken to be the ten-stringed instrument, Psal. 33:2 and 144:9. In playing thereon they did also sing unto it, which made the music the more melodious.

*{and with timbrels}* This instrument is taken to be that which we commonly call *a tabret*; and it is so translated, 2 Sam. 18:6. Women and damsels did use to play upon it, Exod. 15:20; Judg. 11:34; Psal. 68:25. The notation of the Hebrew word, which implies a striking or a beating upon it, shows it was beaten upon with sticks as a drum. They did use to pipe with the beating upon it, by which means the music was the more delectable. Dancing also used to be joined therewith, Exod. 15:20; Judg. 11:34; Psal. 150:4.

*{and with cymbals}* A cymbal was a round hollow instrument of fine sounding brass, ch. 15:19, and it had bullets, or a clapper of brass, or steel, in it. Being skillfully moved, it made a shrill sound. The heathen were wont to use it at offering their sacrifices. The Arabians are said to use it in war.

*{and with trumpets}* Some trumpets were of silver, Num. 10:2, not of brass. They are the loudest sounding instruments that are, used on all occasions of rejoicing. They ever were, and still are of great use in war. A cornet also was much used in their solemnities: of it see ch. 15:28.

**V. 9.** *{And when they came unto the threshing-floor}* A threshing-floor used to be a spacious place, where the owners thereof did not only cause their corn to be threshed, and laid up (whence it is called a corn-floor, Hos. 9:1.) but also feasted and slept therein, Ruth 3:7. They which carried Jacob in much solemnity to his grave rested at the threshing-floor of Atad, Gen. 50:10. It was the threshing-floor of Araunah where the destroying angel was, and where David built an altar, and offered burnt-offerings, 2 Sam. 24:16, 24, where also Solomon built the temple, 2 Chr. 3:1. So also this was some famous place.

*{of Chidon}* Called Nachon, 2 Sam. 6:6. This threshing-floor was not far from Abinadab's house: for God would not suffer them to go long in their error of drawing the ark with oxen.

*{Uzza put forth his hand to hold the ark}* This was a particular transgression of his own. For he being but a Levite, not a priest, ought not to have meddled with the ark, Num. 4:15. This his stepping out of his own calling is called his error, or rashness, 2 Sam. 6:7.

*{for the oxen stumbled}* Or, *shook it*, 2 Sam. 6:6. The oxen stumbling, the ark did shake. And that might seem a fair pretense for Uzza to do what he did: but no pretenses can justify an unlawful act.

**V. 10.** *{And the anger of the LORD was kindled}* See 2 King. 13:3.

*{against Uzza}* It is evident that the Lord was angry against all the priests and Levites present, yea and against the Israelites too, because they put the ark upon a cart or wagon, which should have been carried upon priests' shoulders, Num. 4:13; Josh. 6:6; ch. 15:2, 11, etc. imitating herein the example of the heathen, 1 Sam. 6:7, 8, and not of their ancient predecessors, Josh. 3:14. Yet God would not take vengeance on them all; but by judgment executed on one for a like offense he brought David and all the rest to see their sin, ch. 15:13.

*{and he smote him}* This was some sudden extraordinary stroke that slew him upon the place.

*{because he put his hand to the ark}* See v. 9. It was his own particular sin; at which God took the advantage to punish him.

*{and there he died before God}* Or, *by the ark of God*, 2 Sam. 6:7. Because God manifested his presence in the ark, that which was done before it or by it is said to be done before the Lord. Hereby we see that *our God is a consuming fire*, Deut. 4:24. See on Psal. 27:8.

**V. 11.** *{And David was displeased}* Heb. *anger was to David*. Or, *David was angry*. The Hebrew word is used for a disturbed passion, as Gen. 31:35, and that of anger or grief, and is here well translated *displeased*.

*{because the LORD had made a breach upon Uzza}* By separating Uzza's soul from his body, and him from their society.

*{wherefore that place is called Perez-Uzza}* Heb. *the breach of Uzza*. Judgments are remarkable as well as mercies. Both have their distinct uses; the former, to put us in mind of sin, and to make us fearful of committing the like; the latter, to quicken us up to thankfulness. This name was a memorial of God's judgment. So Num. 11:3 and 22:3; Josh. 7:26.

*{to this day}* See 1 King. 9:13 and 10:12 and 12:10.

**V. 12.** *{And David was afraid of God that day}* Tender hearts by judgments on others are brought the more to fear God, lest the like judgments should befall them. David discerned how failing about holy things much incensed God's wrath.

*{saying, How shall I bring the ark of God home to me?}* He began to think that God would not have the ark come to Jerusalem: but that was his weakness. It was not the act itself, but the ill manner of performing it, that displeased God.

**V. 13.** *{So David brought not}* Heb. *removed not*. Further than the next Levite's house.

*{the ark home to himself to the city of David}* This he first intended, v. 3. But that unexpected judgment made him lay aside that purpose.

*{but carried it aside}* Somewhat out of the way.

*{into the house of Obed-Edom}* A Levite, of the order of the porters and singers, ch. 5:18, 21. Levites were fittest to attend the ark.

*{the Gittite}* So called from the first syllable of Gath-rimmon, one of the cities of the Levites, Josh. 21:24.

**V. 14.** *{And the ark of God remained}* Obed-Edom well discerned that all the destructions that fell out by reason of the ark, either among the Philistims, 1 Sam. 5:2, etc. or at Beth-shemesh, 1 Sam. 6:19, or at Perez-Uzza, v. 11, were by reason of men's transgressions thereabouts, and not simply for the ark itself, which he believed would prove a blessing to such Levites as should rightly entertain it, therefore he is willing it should remain with him.

*{with the family of Obed-Edom}* With his sons and other Levites of his family.

*{in his house}* In the Hebrew family and house are set out by one word, but yet in a different sense. In one place it signifies the habitation, in the other the inhabitants.

*{three months}* Till David had heard that God had blessed the house of Obed-Edom because of the ark, 2 Sam. 6:12.

*{and the LORD blessed the house of Obed-Edom}* God prospered the things that were done in his house, even as he prospered Potiphar for Joseph's sake, Gen. 39:5, and Laban for Jacob's, Gen. 30:27.

*{and all that he had}* His wife, children, servants, kindred, cattle, and all his goods. God's blessing on him and all his was so great that not only he himself discerned it, but also all his neighbors, insomuch as they made it known to David, 2 Sam. 6:12.

## CHAPTER 14

**Ver. 1.** *{Now Hiram king of Tyre}* Of this Hiram see 1 King. 5:1. Of the order of the histories in this chapter see ch. 13:1.

*{sent messengers to David}* It may be David had first sent to Hiram for those trees and workmen which he here sends unto him: as Solomon did, 1 King. 5:6. But if Hiram of himself sent these ambassadors, he had certainly taken notice of the great exploits that David had done in Saul's time, of his prevailing against the house of Saul after Saul's death, of Israel's settling him on the throne over the whole land, and of God's manifold blessings on him; and thereupon being a neighboring king, and observing how God was with David, he sent his ambassadors to congratulate his settling upon the throne. Hiram is the first king we read of that sent ambassadors to David.

*{and timber of cedars}* Or, *cedar-trees*. See 1 King. 5:6.

*{with masons}* Heb. *with workmen in the wall*. These are called 2 King. 12:12, *hewers of stone*, and such as laid stones in the wall.

*{and carpenters}* Or, *workmen on trees, or timber*. See 2 King. 12:11, 12.

*{to build him an house}* God having kept David from settling the ark in his own city, he thinks of building an house for himself.

**V. 2.** *{And David perceived}* By God's blessing on him, the people's unanimous consent in anointing him, and Hiram's congratulating him.

*{that the LORD had confirmed him king over Israel}* For there was none now to oppose him, but he quietly enjoyed the whole kingdom. This is added as a reason of David's building him an house.

*{for his Kingdom was lift up on high}* He had greater glory than Saul ever had: and the kingdom was more famous and more highly accounted of than in Saul's time, or in the time that himself reigned in Hebron, 2 Sam. 5:5.

*{because of his people Israel}* This is added both to show how David's kingdom was exalted so high, namely, because all Israel much honored him; and also, why God so exalted it, namely, for the good of his people Israel. It is for the people's good that God gives them good governors.

**V. 3.** *{And David took more}* Heb. *yet*.

*{wives at Jerusalem}* Besides those he had before Saul's death, and at Hebron, 1 Sam. 18:27 and 25:42, 43; 2 Sam. 3:2, etc. That particle yet implies a fault. David was not content with those wives he had before added, but yet added more unto them, which was against the express law of God, Deut. 17:17. It is also said that *he took mo concubines*, 2 Sam. 5:13. See 1 King. 11:3. Among these he might have some properly called wives, and others concubines.

*{and David begat more sons and daughters}* Notwithstanding his sin, God increased his posterity, and gave him some children better than those he had before, as Nathan and Solomon.

**V. 4.** *{Now these are the names, etc.}* Of these thirteen sons of David here mentioned see ch. 3:5, etc.

*{Elpalet}* Or, *Eliphelet*, ch. 3:6.

**V. 8.** *{And when the Philistines heard that David was anointed king over all Israel}* While the kingdom was divided betwixt the house of Saul and the house of David, they might think that being divided it could not stand: but now discerning that all the Israelites were of one mind and one heart, and that with unanimous consent they had chosen so good an head as David, of whose wisdom, valor and good success they themselves had had so much experience, they think it wisdom in the beginning of his settlement over the whole kingdom, to do what they could to weaken his power.

*{all the Philistines}* All the five princes conspired against David, as five kings of Canaan did against Joshua, Josh. 10:3 and 11:1, 2.

*{went up to seek David}* The Philistims first warring against Israel in the reign of David, he is freed from all appearance of ingratitude against them, who had harbored him when he was forced by Saul's persecution to fly out of the land. They came to seek David that they might fight against him and spoil him of his kingdom.

*{and David heard of it}* David being a prudent prince, would not be without intelligencers and scouts, by whom he might understand the plots and practices of his enemies.

*{and went out against them}* He would not stay till they should enter his dominions, but went out to meet them, and fight against them. It is said, 2 Sam. 5:17, that *David went down to the hold*, i. e. some strong fort built at the borders of Judea, where David settled his army till he had inquired of the Lord what to do, v. 10.

**V. 9.** *{And the Philistines came and spread themselves}* I. e. settled their camp, and set their army in array.

*{in the valley of Rephaim}* See Gen. 14:5; Deut. 2:11. This valley lies north on Judea, Josh. 15:8.

**V. 10.** *{And David enquired of God}* David's dependence was so on God as he would not attempt any weighty matter without asking counsel of him, 1 Sam. 23:2, 4, 12.

*{saying, Shall I go up against the Philistines}* Questionless he used the help of the priest in propounding this question to the Lord, as 1 Sam. 23:9, 10.

*{and wilt thou deliver them into mine hand?}* Herein he deals prudently. For what good had it been for him to have gone against the Philistims, if they should not have been delivered into his hands? Therefore he inquires after the issue.

*{And the LORD said unto him}* God is ready to answer them that in truth and faith seek to him.

*{Go up, for I will deliver them into thine hand}* God so answers both questions, as David could not but be much encouraged to go on.

**V. 11.** *{So they came up}* The Philistims came up from the valley before mentioned.

*{to Baal-Perazim}* So called by anticipation.

*{and David smote them there}* He routed the whole army, and slew many of them.

*{Then David said, God hath broken in upon my enemies}* This pious man ascribes the victory unto God, as the principal cause thereof. The metaphor breaking in upon implies the routing, dispersing or scattering the enemies.

*{by mine hand}* He makes himself only God's instrument.

*{like the breaking forth of waters}* This metaphor may be taken two ways: 1. For a breach of a great river through a bank, which sweeps away whatsoever is in the field before it. 2. For a vessel full of water, which being broken all the water flows out. Thus their army being broken, they and all they had was lost.

*{Therefore they called the name of that place}* David and his men gave a name to the place for a perpetual memory of that victory. It is said, 2 Sam. 5:20, that he, namely David, called the name of that place. God's great deliverances are to be had in perpetual remembrance.

*{Baal-Perazim}* Heb. *i*. e. a place of breaches, or, a master of breaches. The name is taken from the idol Baal, who with his idolatrous worshipers were there destroyed.

**V. 12.** *{And when they had left their gods there}* The word translated gods is the same that is used ch. 10:9, and signifies such things as affright men. It seems they were in a great fear, in that they left their gods behind them. Men use to have most care of their gods, and to carry them with them.

*{David gave a commandment}* It was God's commandment that the Israelites should burn the images of the heathen with fire, Deut. 7:5. Therefore David gives this in special charge to his soldiers.

*{and they were burnt with fire}* Herein those soldiers testified their obedience to their sovereign, their indignation against idols, and their zeal of God's glory.

**V. 13.** *{And the Philistines yet again}* Enemies will not cease upon one defeat. See 1 King. 20:22.

*{spread themselves abroad}* They recruited their army, and set themselves in battle-array.

*{in the valley}* This was the valley of Rephaim, 2 Sam. 5:22. See v. 9.

**V. 14.** *{Therefore David enquired again of God}* Because David's first inquiry, v. 10, was but for that once, he does not thereupon wax over-confident, as the Benjamites, when they had once and again overthrown their brethren, Judg. 20:31, but there being a new occasion, he does again ask counsel of the Lord.

*{and God said unto him, Go not up after them, Turn away from them}* This advice is given; not that he should clean give over, and no more fight against the Philistims, but to direct him to such a course as might more clearly manifest the victory to be of God. Besides, the Philistims might hereby be the more encouraged to draw all their army together, and so receive the greater defeat. See Josh. 8:14, etc.

*{and come upon them}* Namely upon their rear.

*{over against the Mulberry trees}* This was that place where God intended to give David a sign of victory. So Judg. 7:9, etc.

**V. 15.** *{And it shall be when thou shalt hear a sound of going}* I. e. a sound of men and horses as it were trampling upon the ground.

*{in the tops of the Mulberry trees}* This sound of going was on the top of trees, to show that it was no human succor that God afforded him, but succor from heaven, even by angels. Elisha saw angels on a mountain, 2 King. 6:17, but David heard the presence of the host of God. It may be that by this sign the Philistims were affrighted. So 2 King. 7:6.

*{that then thou shalt go out to battle}* Here again God gives him a warrant and encouragement to go against his enemies, as v. 10.

*{for God is gone forth before thee}* This shows that it was God's host that caused the sound in the mulberry-trees.

*{to smite the host of the Philistines}* This was done by affrighting them and making them fly before David and his army.

**V. 16.** *{David therefore did as God commanded him}* An evidence of his obedience and faith.

*{and they smote the host of the Philistines}* As before v. 11.

*{from Gibeon}* Called Geba, 2 Sam. 5:25. It is taken to be the same that is called Gibeah. Geba, Gibeah, and Gibeon were all in Benjamin, Josh. 21:17; Judg. 20:4.

*{even to Gazer}* This is supposed to be the same that is called Gezer, which was in Ephraim, Josh. 16:10. These two cities were far distant one from another, so as the pursuit of the enemy was very far. See on Josh. 10:33.

**V. 17.** *{And the fame of David went out into all lands}* By fame is meant a good and great report of David's valor and victories, spread far and near through all the nations round about Israel.

*{and the LORD brought}* It is God that by his providence causes a man's name to be spread abroad.

*{the fear of him upon all nations}* The Lord so wrought upon the hearts of the very heathen that they stood in a kind of awe of David, and were afraid to oppose against him, but rather sought his friendship and favor. See 2 Chr. 17:10.

## CHAPTER 15

**Ver. 1.** *{And David made him houses}* I. e. a royal palace fit for a king, and all sorts of edifices appertaining thereunto.

*{in the city of David}* See 1 King. 2:10.

*{and prepared a place for the ark of God}* This is an evidence of David's piety. See ch. 13:3.

*{and pitched for it a tent}* This he did for the present: but he had a purpose to build a temple for it, ch. 17:1.

**V. 2.** *{Then David said}* There had been three months betwixt the setting of the ark in the house of Obed-Edom and David's giving the advice following; so as he had been some time inquiring after the cause of God's displeasure, ch. 13:10.

*{None ought to carry the ark of God but the Levites}* Heb. *it is not to carry the ark of God but for the Levites*, Num. 4:5, 15. By the law he discerned their former error in setting it upon a cart, ch. 13:7.

*{for them hath the LORD chosen to carry the ark of God}* They should have carried it on their shoulders, and not have put oxen to have drawn it. See ch. 13:9.

*{and to minister unto him}* To do all holy services about the tabernacle, ark, altar, and other holy things.

*{for ever}* So long as the polity of the Jews should remain. In a large extent Levites may be put for all who are set apart to perform ministerial functions, Isa. 66:21. And thus this phrase for ever is to be extended to the church of God in all ages, even to the end of the world. See 1 King. 8:13.

**V. 3.** *{And David gathered all Israel together to Jerusalem}* This was a second assembling them together, and that to the same end, even after three months. See ch. 13:5; 2 Sam. 6:11.

*{to bring up the ark of the LORD}* Though God's displeasure at first had discouraged David from bringing the ark up to his own city, yet he now attempts it again, moved, no doubt, by these reasons; 1. Piety to God, and zeal of his glory. 2. That knowledge he now had of the cause of God's displeasure, and of the right manner of carrying the ark. 3. That report he heard of God's blessing on the house of Obed-Edom, where the ark rested, 2 Sam. 6:12.

*{unto his place which he had prepared for it}* See v. 1.

**V. 4.** *{And David assembled the children of Aaron}* All these were priests, Numb. 18:1, 2.

*{and the Levites}* I. e. all the other children of Levi, besides the Aaronites. These in special he assembled, because the work he intended did specially belong to them.

**V. 5.** *{Of the sons of Kohath}* See ch. 6:2. By sons are here and in the verses following meant posterity.

*{Uriel the chief}* See ch. 6:24. Uriel was at this time the head or governor of the posterity of Kohath.

*{and his brethren}* Or, *kinsmen*. Such as descended from Kohath. See ch. 9:6.

*{an hundred and twenty}* These could not be all the posterity of Kohath; for in Moses his time they were two thousand seven hundred and fifty; but these were only the chief of this family. See v. 13, 16.

**V. 6.** *{Of the sons of Merari, etc.}* See v. 5 and ch. 16:1, 29.

**V. 7.** *{Of the sons of Gershom, etc.}* See v. 5 and ch. 6:1, 16, and Num. 4:40.

**V. 8.** *{Of the sons of Elizaphan}* The chief of the Kohathites, Num. 3:30.

*{Shemaiah}* See ch. 9:14.

**V. 9.** *{Of the sons of Hebron}* Another of Kohath's sons, ch. 6:2, 18.

*{Eliel}* See ch. 6:34.

**V. 10.** *{Of the sons of Uzziel}* This also was a son of Kohath, ch. 6:2.

*{Amminadab}* See ch. 6:22.

**V. 11.** *{And David called for Zadok and Abiathar the priests}* See ch. 12:28; 1 King. 1:7 and 2:27, 35. These two were governors over all the other priests and Levites, and had a kind of joint commission, and thereupon are oft joined together, as 2 Sam. 8:17 and 15:29, 35 and 17:15 and 19:11.

*{and for the Levites}* Such as were to attend the priests, Num. 18:2, and to do the services about the temple.

*{for Uriel, etc.}* These six in the former verses were every one called the chief.

**V. 12.** *{And said unto them, Ye are the chief of the fathers of the Levites}* The king gives this direction to these heads of the Levites, that they might direct such as were under them.

*{sanctify yourselves}* His meaning is, that they should both outwardly according to the rites of the law, and also inwardly by renewing their repentance, prepare themselves for that holy work whereunto they were deputed, 2 Chron. 29:5; Exod. 19:10, 15.

*{ye and your brethren}* Both the chief, and others also that had any thing to do about the ark, were to be sanctified.

*{that ye may bring up the ark}* You yourselves; some of you upon your shoulders, others attending upon it.

*{of the LORD God of Israel}* See 1 King. 8:4, 15.

*{unto the place that I have prepared for it}* See v. 1, 3.

**V. 13.** *{For because ye did it not}* Not bear it yourselves, nor sanctify yourselves. You inquired not into the law, to learn what was to be done about so great a work.

*{at the first}* When we first began to remove the ark, ch. 13:7.

*{the LORD our God made a breach upon us}* By slaying Uzza, and taking him from among us, ch. 13:10, 11.

*{for that we sought him not after the due order}* Heb. *according to right*. The thing they did was in the substance of it good, but they failed in the manner of doing it. An ill manner of doing a good thing provokes God's anger.

**V. 14.** *{So the priests and the Levites sanctified themselves}* They cleansed themselves after the ordinances of the law, and prepared themselves to that intended solemn work. See some rites appertaining hereunto, Gen. 35:2; Exod. 19:14, 15. To legal rites fasting and prayer were added upon extraordinary occasion, 1 Sam. 7:6; Joel 1:14, 15 and 2:15.

*{to bring up the ark of the LORD God of Israel}* See v. 12.

**V. 15.** *{And the children of the Levites}* I. e. the sons of Kohath, whose office it was to bear the ark, Num. 4:15, though sometimes in great solemnities the priests themselves did bear it, Josh. 3:31; 1 Sam. 4:4.

*{bare the ark of God upon their shoulders}* Thus were all the holy things of the sanctuary carried from place to place upon men's shoulders, Numb. 7:9, and this for the greater honor.

*{with the staves thereon}* See Exod. 25:14, etc.

*{as Moses commanded}* In that Moses commanded the ark to be made with rings in the corners thereof, and staves to be put into those rings to bear the ark with them, Ex. 25:14, he may be said to command the Levites to bear the ark of God. But an express charge is given them by Moses to bear the sanctuary and the things appertaining thereunto, Numb. 4:15.

*{according to the word of the LORD}* This is added both to manifest the faithfulness of Moses, Numb. 12:7, and also to move those to whom David in particular gave this charge, the rather to observe it, because the Lord appointed it so to be done.

**V. 16.** *{And David spake to the chief of the Levites}* See v. 11, 12.

*{to appoint their brethren to be singers with instruments of musick}* To join voices and instruments together, to make the music the more melodious.

*{Psalteries, and harps, and cymbals}* Of these see ch. 13:8.

*{sounding}* Heb. *causing to be heard*.

*{by lifting up the voice}* He would have the music so ordered as both instruments and voices also might be heard.

*{with joy}* As the melody was of force to quicken others' spirits, and make them rejoice, so he would have the Levites do what they did with cheerful and joyful spirits.

**V. 17.** *{So the Levites}* I. e. the chief of the Levites, v. 16.

*{appointed Heman the son of Joel}* See ch. 6:33.

*{and of his brethren}* Or, *kinsmen*. See v. 5.

*{Asaph the son of Berechiah}* See ch. 6:39, 43.

*{and the sons of Merari}* Or, *of the sons of Merari*.

*{their brethren}* This has reference to the sons before mentioned. They were all of the same tribe and office, and in that respect brethren. See ch. 9:6.

*{Ethan the son of Kushaiah}* Or, *Kishi*, ch. 6:44.

**V. 18.** *{And with them their brethren of the second degree}* There were among the Levites, even of those that were of the same family and office, divers orders or degrees. Now these here meant were of that order that was next those mentioned v. 17, who are styled the chief, v. 16, and were of the first degree.

*{Zechariah, etc.}* Here are fourteen by name reckoned up.

*{the porters}* This may have reference to all the fourteen here expressed by name; or to Obed-Edom and Jeiel only, who are named immediately before. This office, porters, may be taken in a general relation to a continual office of standing at the entrance to the ark, to take care who entered into that tent, or what was brought thereinto. Obed-Edom was one of them, ch. 16:38. Or it may be taken in a particular relation to the ark, and the carrying of it at this time: one or more of them went before the ark, another or more of them followed the ark, to keep off people both before and behind from pressing too near. See ch. 26:1.

**V. 19.** *{So the singers, Heman, Asaph, and Ethan}* These were the three chief of the Levites of several families, set down v. 17.

*{were appointed to sound with cymbals of brass}* See ch. 13:8. Several musicians had their several instruments.

**V. 20.** *{And Zechariah}* Next to Zachariah is added Ben, v. 18, but here left out; unless Azzaziah, who is not in the number, v. 18, were the same with Ben. And two names are oft attributed to the same person.

*{and Aziel}* Or, *Jaaziel*.

*{and Shemiramoth, etc.}* This and the five following are the very same that are named v. 18, only there Benaiah is put before Maasiah, v. 18.

*{with psalteries}* See ch. 13:8.
