God had of old declared that he would have an especial house for his service, Deut. 12:11, and had also told David that his son should build that house, ch. 17:12. And now by the forementioned evidences of God’s accepting his sacrifice in this place, together with the charge the prophet gave him to erect an altar there, ch. 21:18, 19, he gathered that there must be the house of God. And this he might the rather do by calling to mind the altar which Abraham erected in this place, and the sacrifice which he thereupon offered, Gen. 22:2, 9, 13. Besides, being a prophet, he might have some special revelation of God’s mind concerning this. Or Gad might from the Lord declare it to him.

*{and this is the Altar}* i.e. In this place shall an altar be built. For Solomon made another altar, 2 Chron. 4:1.

*{of the burnt offering for Israel}* Whereon the people of God shall time after time offer all their burnt-offerings.

**V. 2.** *{And David commanded to gather together the strangers that were in all the land of Israel}* i.e. Such as were not descended from Abraham, Isaac and Jacob, but were of other nations, and dwelt among the Israelites, and were tributary unto them. See 1 Kings 9:20, 21. Among these, Sidonians and Tyrians are especially meant. See 1 Kings 5:6, 18. The employing of strangers about building of the temple was a type of the calling of the Gentiles unto the church of Christ.

*{and he set Masons to hew wrought stones}* Heb. *stones of cutting*. See 1 Kings 5:15, 17, 18.

*{to build the house of God}* See 1 Kings 3:1. David was not to build the temple himself, ch. 17:4, but he prepared fit materials for his son to build it withal.

**V. 3.** *{And David prepared iron in abundance}* For there is great use of iron in all sorts of building.

*{for the nails for the doors of the gates}* The gates here meant were at the entrances into the courts. For the doors of the most holy and holy places were hung on hinges of gold and silver; of which metal were the nails also, 1 Kings 7:50; 2 Chron. 3:8.

*{and for the joinings}* Hereby are meant such hinges as the leaves of the doors did hang and turn upon.

*{and brass in abundance without weight}* i.e. The brass provided for the house of God was laid up and committed to overseers without weighing it. For it would have been too great a task to have weighed all, and too great a charge to have been accountable for it by weight. See 1 Kings 4:47; 2 Kings 25:16.

**V. 4.** *{Also Cedar-trees in abundance}* The greatest part of the timber used about the temple was of cedar. See 1 Kings 5:6, and 6:9.

*{for the Zidonians and they of Tyre brought much Cedar-wood}* Lebanon, where cedars grew, was in the dominion of the king of Tyre. See 1 Kings 5:6.

*{to David}* For Hiram, the king of Tyre and Zidon, was ever a lover of David. See 1 Kings 5:1; 2 Sam. 5:11.

**V. 5.** *{And David said}* This might be an holy soliloquy betwixt David and his own soul. Or otherwise he might say it to such a congregation as is mentioned ch. 29:1.

*{Solomon my son is young and tender}* In what respect he is said to be young, see 1 Kings 3:7. He is said to be tender in regard of the mildness and meekness of his disposition, and in that he had not been accustomed to war or any great employments.

*{and the house that is to be builded for the LORD}* For his honor, the evidences of his presence, and means of worshiping him. See 1 Kings 3:1.

*{must be exceeding magnifical}* Heb. *to make great, to be above*. Such as may appear to be very great indeed, excelling all others. Thus it was in the service itself; and in the ornaments appertaining thereunto. It was one of the world’s wonders. It was far more excellent than the tabernacle: for that was but cloth, this of hewn stone; and this in all dimensions far exceeded that: So the cherubims, sea, and other things in the temple were much larger and fairer than those in the tabernacle. See 1 Kings 6:23, &c. and 7:23. Besides, there were in the temple ten tables and ten candlesticks to one in the tabernacle, 1 Kings 7:49; 2 Chron. 4:8. As for the other buildings, though there might be some higher, or longer, or wider, yet there was never any that had such costly materials, such curious workmanship, such divine services, such representations of God’s majesty and mercy, as this; and which prefigured such mystical, spiritual and celestial truths as this did.

*{of fame}* Heb. *for a name*. To be highly accounted of all, and to be of greater repute than any other building.

*{and of glory}* Or, *and for ornament*. Some join this epithet with the former, *q. d.* *a most glorious name*. He meant such an excellency as by words could not be sufficiently expressed, and therefore heaps up these many epithets one upon another.

*{throughout all countries}* He would have it such an house as should not only be admired by the Israelites, among whom it was, but also among other nations, to whom the report thereof should come. The fame of this house spread abroad throughout all nations. See 1 Kings 8:41, 42. It was one thing that struck the queen of Sheba into a great amazement, 1 Kings 10:4.

*{I will therefore now make preparation for it}* See v. 2. Because it was to be such an house as is before described, he thought it would be too great a task for his son both to prepare materials, and also to build it. Therefore he does the former, and this was a point of great prudence and zeal.

*{So David prepared abundantly before his death}* He had not only purposes to do such a thing, but while he had time he indeed did what he purposed.

**V. 6.** *{Then he called for Solomon his son}* It may be he spoke this to Solomon alone, that so he might give the more heed to his words.

*{and charged him to build}* He left it not to his son’s choice, but laid an express command upon him, that he might not fail to do it.

*{an house for the LORD God of Israel}* See v. 5, and 1 Kings 8:15.

**V. 7.** *{And David said to Solomon, My son}* This title uttered by a father is an evidence of great favor; and thereby draws his son to attend to him the more diligently. Some read it in the third person, *to Solomon his son*: but the former reading has the greater emphasis.

*{as for me}* This shows how greatly he desired if he might have had his own will, to have done it himself.

*{it was in my mind to build an house}* See ch. 17:1. This he makes known to his son, to move him the rather to do that which he himself could not do, though he desired it.

*{unto the name of the LORD}* See 1 Kings 5:3.

*{my God}* See ch. 13:2; 1 Kings 8:28.

**V. 8.** *{But the word of the LORD came unto me, saying}* This has reference to ch. 17:3, &c.

*{Thou hast shed blood abundantly}* This is not expressed in that answer which Nathan brought to David, but is implied by the mention of those enemies which were cut off before him, ch. 17:8. The blood here said to be shed abundantly is not upbraided unto David as a matter of injury or cruelty, but alleged only as a reason of his unfitness to build the temple. Saul and Ahab for sparing lose their kingdoms, 1 Sam. 15:23; 1 Kings 20:32, 42. David for shedding blood may not build the temple; yet the Levites for their fact, Exod. 32, and Phinehas for his, Num. 25, have the priesthood settled on them. See Photius Epist. 219.

*{and hast made great wars}* No king of Israel fought more battles, nor had more mighty enemies than David. He began with a giant, 1 Sam. 17:50, and he subdued all the nations round about him, see ch. 14:8, &c. and 18:1, &c. and 19:1, &c. and 20:1, &c.

*{thou shalt not build an house to my name}* See v. 7; ch. 17:4. This inference implies that a man of war, who sheds much blood, is not, in that respect, fit to do that which should typify Christ our peace.

*{because thou hast shed much blood upon the earth in my sight}* Either this is set down as a repetition of the same thing, and so it adds much emphasis: See 1 Kings 8:13. Or it has reference to another kind of bloodshed, namely, to shedding of innocent blood: of which David was guilty, 2 Sam. 11:15, 16, 17, and this clause, upon the earth in my sight, may seem to intend as much; It being oft used to set forth an over-great boldness in sin, without any fear of the presence of God, in whose sight it is committed, 2 Kings 21:20, 25. At least hereby is implied, that albeit just war and just execution of death on capital malefactors be lawful, yet God takes not delight in shedding of blood, simply and indefinitely considered. See on Num. 31:19.

**V. 9.** *{Behold, a son shall be born to thee}* This has reference to the promise made before Solomon was born, 2 Sam. 7:12, 13. For at this time Solomon was present before him.

*{who shall be a man of rest}* Free from wars and all molestation of enemies; for by rest peace is here meant.

*{and I will give him rest from all his enemies round about}* It is God that causeth wars to rest, and settles peace in a land, Ps. 46:9.

*{for his name shall be called Solomon}* That is, Peaceable. Questionless this name was given him in relation to the peace of the land in his time. David gave it him, 2 Sam. 12:24, but by a prophetical spirit.

*{and I will give peace and quietness unto Israel in his days}* This promise was verified, 1 Kings 4:25. In regard of this peace Solomon was a fit type of Christ the Prince of peace, Isa. 9:6, and fit to erect that temple which was a type of the church, the proper subject of peace, whose true members are all peacemakers, in which the glad tidings of peace is preached, and wherein we are brought to eternal peace.

**V. 10.** *{He shall build an house for my Name}* See v. 8, and ch. 17:12.

*{and he shall be my son, and I will be his father}* See ch. 17:13.

*{and I will establish the throne of his kingdom over Israel for ever}* See ch. 17:12, and 1 Kings 9:5.

**V. 11.** *{Now my son}* See v. 7. Hitherto David has related what God said to him concerning the building of a temple: Now he directs his speech to Solomon, by way of prayer and advice.

*{the LORD be with thee}* By his gracious favor and assistance. This phrase sets out the fountain of all good success, safety and security, Rom. 8:31.

*{and prosper thou}* The word signifies good success in the things a man undertakes, Gen. 39:3; Deut. 29:9; Josh. 1:7; 1 Kings 2:3. And, as here used, it implies both a prayer for good success, and also a prophecy that Solomon should have good success.

*{and build the house of the LORD}* This also may be taken as an exhortation to do his best, or as a promise of God’s enabling him to do it.

*{thy God}* David puts his son in mind of that special relation that was betwixt God and him, both to quicken him up the more to do that work, and also to strengthen his faith in God.

*{as he hath said of thee}* As he has foretold you should do, ch. 17:12.

**V. 12.** *{Onely the LORD give thee}* This word only is not to be taken exclusively, as if David desired nothing else from God; but by way of excellency, *q. d.* *Above all*. And he directs his prayer to God, because he knew God only could give those gifts which were requisite for such a work, Eccles. 6:2.

*{wisdom and understanding}* It may be Solomon from hence learned to ask these gifts of God, 2 Chron. 1:10. See 1 Kings 3:9, 12. As these graces were in general necessary for ordering the affairs of his estate, so in special for building of the temple, wherein not only much human art and skill was to be used, in appointing curious matters to be made and fitly set one by another, but also divine knowledge in the mysteries of godliness, for making things fit for the present worship of God, and also to be pertinent types of future truths.

*{and give thee charge concerning Israel}* Here David extends his prayer further than the building of the temple, namely, to the good of his whole kingdom. By giving him charge he means divine direction and counsel, with which he desires his son may be so furnished as thereby to instruct and direct all his people.

*{that thou mayest keep the law of the LORD}* Here is a prayer for Solomon, that he may keep the law; and a direction to him how to prosper, namely, by keeping the law; together with an incitation to do it.

*{thy God}* See v. 11.

**V. 13.** *{Then shalt thou prosper}* See v. 11.

*{if thou takest heed to fulfil the Statutes and Judgments}* See 1 Kings 2:3.

*{which the LORD charged Moses with}* Which God first revealed to Moses, with a charge that he should declare them to others, Deut. 5:31; Mal. 4:4.

*{concerning Israel}* David teaches his son to apply in particular to himself the laws which were given to all the people of God.

*{Be strong, and of good courage}* The former word implies a putting forth all the strength he had to that work whereunto he was set apart; the other, a confident and undaunted spirit. See ch. 19:13; 1 Kings 2:2.

*{dread not, nor be dismayed}* These negatives added to affirmatives directly opposite and contrary to them, show that corruptions and infirmities must be removed, for the more kindly work of grace in us. The former phrase dread not, may have relation to oppositions that might be met withal; the other, nor be dismayed, to the difficulty of the work.

**V. 14.** *{Now behold, in my trouble}* Or, *in my poverty*. The Hebrew word is used for any kind of trouble or affliction, and here implies those continual molestations which David had by wars with enemies abroad, and by rebellions in his own land; as a long while against the house of Saul, 2 Sam. 3:1, his son Absalom, 2 Sam. 15:11, &c. and Sheba, 2 Sam. 20:1, &c. The three years famine also, 2 Sam. 21:14, and the plague, ch. 21:14, may be comprised under this word. It is used Gen. 29:31; Deut. 26:7; Exod. 3:7.

*{I have prepared for the house of the LORD}* See v. 5.

*{an hundred thousand talents of gold, and a thousand thousand talents of silver}* Because all estimations must be according to the shekel of the Sanctuary, Lev. 27:25, the shekel is fittest to be the rule of all accounts. Our English word scale seems to be thence derived; *שקל* *shakal*, signifying *to weigh*. Many make two kinds of shekels; one sacred, the other common. The sacred was as much more as the common, and is for the most part meant in Scripture. The shekel contained two bekahs, Exod. 38:26, and weighed three hundred and twenty barleycorns, i.e. half an ounce of Troy weight; and was worth two shillings and six pence. Now the talent contained three thousand shekels, as may be collected from Exod. 38:25, 26, where six hundred thousand and three thousand and five hundred and fifty men being taxed by the poll at a bekah or half-shekel for a man, the whole sum of the tax was an hundred talents and a thousand seven hundred threescore and fifteen shekels. A shekel then being half an ounce, three thousand shekels of silver amount to three hundred seventy-five pound sterling at five shillings the ounce, which is the value of a talent in silver. Now the Hebrews valuing gold at ten times the rate of silver, a talent of gold with them was worth three thousand seven hundred and fifty pounds: which proportion of ten to one is evident in this verse: The hundred thousand talents of gold and the thousand thousand talents of silver are ten for one, and both of one value, namely, three hundred seventy-five millions, or three hundred seventy-five times a thousand thousand pounds. And both sums of silver and gold together, amount to seven hundred and fifty millions. Gold is now in Christendom at a higher rate; after which if the talent should be reckoned, the sum would be so much the greater. We do not read elsewhere of so great a treasure laid up upon any occasion: So that David herein showed the exceeding great respect that he bore to God and his service.

*{and of brass and iron without weight}* That which was before v. 3 spoken of brass, is here also spoken of iron.

*{for it is in abundance}* There was so much of those metals prepared as they exceeded all weight.

*{timber also and stone have I prepared}* For these materials also were requisite.

*{and thou mayest add thereto}* He supposed that notwithstanding the great provision he had made, yet something might be wanting, and therefore he advises his son to add more as he should see occasion.

**V. 15.** *{Moreover there are workmen with thee}* Heb. *doers of the work*. Under these are comprised all that might do any service thereabout.

*{in abundance}* David had as great a care about the multitude of workmen as about the abundance of materials.

*{hewers}* This is applied to hewers of timber, 2 Chron. 2:10, and also to hewers of stone, 2 Kings 12:12.

*{and workers of stone and timber}* That is, masons and carpenters. Such as might not only square and otherwise fit these materials, but also carve and grave them.

*{and all manner of cunning men}* Which might work all curious works that were requisite.

*{for every manner of work}* For there was great variety of workmanship about the things of the temple, 1 Kings 7:17, 24, &c.

**V. 16.** *{Of the gold, the silver, and the brass, and the iron there is no number}* What was said before of brass and iron v. 14 is here also said of gold and silver, to amplify the provision that David made.

*{Arise therefore}* This is a phrase of quickening. See 1 Kings 14:2.

*{and be doing}* This implies that he should take the first opportunity of setting upon that great work. Such abundance of materials being provided, he would not have his son wanting in his endeavor.

*{and the LORD be with thee}* See v. 11.

**V. 17.** *{David also commanded all the Princes of Israel to help Solomon his son}* He knew the work was too great for one man’s head and hands, and therefore takes care that all that were of place and parts might be helpful to his son.

**V. 18.** *{Is not the LORD your God with you?}* He uses that word of relation to them, your God, both to show that they had as great reason to add their help as he and his son had, and also to assure them that God would be assistant to them if they used their endeavor and put forth their strength.

*{and hath he not given you rest on every side?}* Freedom from all enemies; so as they were not like to meet with any impediments. See v. 9. These interrogatives are strong affirmations, and add much emphasis.

*{for he hath given the inhabitants of the land into mine hand}* So as they are subject to my government, and not forced to serve others.

*{and the Land is subdued before the LORD}* It is freed from all enemies; so as it may freely give itself to God’s service, and to his work.

*{and before his people}* So as God’s people may peaceably and quietly enjoy their own possessions.

**V. 19.** *{Now set your heart and your soul to seek the LORD your God}* In general to learn to do his will; and in particular to pray for his grace and aid. See ch. 16:10, and 28:9.

*{arise therefore}* See v. 6.

*{and build ye}* Help on the building what you can.

*{the Sanctuary of the LORD God}* See ch. 9:29.

*{to bring the Ark of the Covenant of the LORD}* See 1 Kings 8:4; 2 Sam. 7:2.

*{and the holy vessels of God}* Such as Moses made, and such as were afterwards to be made by Solomon. See 1 Kings 8:4.

*{into the house that is to be built to the name of the LORD}* See 1 Kings 5:3.

## CHAPTER 23

**V. 1.** *{So when David was old and full of days}* See ch. 25:28; 1 Kings 1:1.

*{he made Solomon his son king over Israel}* He declared or appointed him to be his successor after his death. See ch. 28:5. This verse is to be referred to the former chapter, and that as a reason of the charge which David gave both to his son Solomon, and also to all the princes, ch. 22:6, 17. For those things that follow concerning the officers of church and state were set down at sundry times before the forementioned charge. See ch. 28:1.

**V. 2.** *{And he gathered together all the Princes of Israel}* These were governors of the civil state, and had sundry offices committed unto them, ch. 21:1, &c. Besides, David would have them to be witnesses of such things as he gave in charge to the priests and Levites: for they also were to come to their temple, to bring their sacrifices and to see them offered up in a right manner.

*{with the Priests and the Levites}* Most of the things given in charge concerned the priests and Levites, who were to attend the services of the house of God. Priests were such as descended from Aaron; Levites, all the others that descended from Levi, who in their several functions attended upon the priests, v. 28.

**V. 3.** *{Now the Levites were numbered from the age}* Heb. *from a son*.

*{of thirty years}* According to the law, Num. 4:3. At thirty years they were capable of an office, but at five and twenty they might come into the temple, and do inferior services therein, Num. 8:24. See v. 24.

*{and upward}* Some take this without any limitation, as if they were numbered to the uttermost of their age. But the law restrains it to fifty years, Num. 4:3. After which age, having attained to good experience and maturity of judgment, they went to their cities in the several tribes, and there tried and judged matters brought before them: For Levites were well experienced in the judicial laws. So as the law of ceasing to work in the house of God might well be observed in David’s time, and after also.

*{and their number by their polls}* Heb. *skulls, or, heads*, v. 24.

*{man by man}* This phrase is an exposition of the former. This numbering of the Levites was not as the numbering of the people ch. 21:1, out of pride or curiosity, but piety and prudence, and that by a divine instinct. See 2 Chron. 2.92,5. The end thereof was, that knowing the number of the persons, he might the better distribute them into several orders.

*{was thirty and eight thousand}* This was the total sum; the distribution whereof follows.

**V. 4.** *{Of which twenty and four thousand were to set forward}* Or, *to oversee*.

*{the work of the house of the LORD}* These attended about sacrifices, lights, washings, and other things to be done in the temple.

*{and six thousand were Officers and Judges}* These had a charge over others, ch. 26:29. Of these some might be such as were sent abroad into the land to hear and determine causes, Deut. 16:18; 2 Chron. 19:11.

**V. 5.** *{Moreover four thousand were porters}* Of their office, see ch. 9:17; of their orders, ch. 26:1, &c.

*{and four thousand praised the LORD with instruments}* These were musicians. The subject of their music was divine psalms and hymns, which set forth the praise and glory of God. They are called singers, ch. 9:33, and *singers with instruments of musick* ch. 15:16. For they praised God both with vocal and instrumental music. See the orders of these ch. 25:1, &c. The twenty and four thousand overseers, the six thousand officers, the four thousand porters, and the four thousand musicians make up the thirty-eight thousand Levites, v. 3.

*{which I made (said David) to praise therewith}* David was a skillful musician from his youth, 1 Sam. 16:18, 23, and as he increased in other endowments, so in this skill: and being a man filled with the zeal of God’s glory, he turned all his skill in music to the setting forth of God’s praises. Witness the book of Psalms. In this respect he is styled *the sweet Psalmist of Israel*, 2 Sam. 23:1. What he did in this kind, he did by the instinct of God’s Spirit, 2 Sam. 23:2; 2 Chron. 29:25.

**V. 6.** *{And David divided them into courses}* Heb. *divisions*. They were so divided into certain ranks and distinct functions as every one might know what work he had to do, and what time he ought to wait. David did this as a prophet, and that also by advice and counsel with two other prophets, Nathan and Gad; and all by God’s command, 2 Chron. 29:25. The courses or orders here set down Solomon his son punctually retained, 2 Chron. 8:14.

*{among the sons of Levi}* For they only were to attend the service in the house of God, Num. 1:5.

*{namely, Gershon, Kohath, and Merari}* Exod. 6:16; ch. 6:1, &c.

**V. 7.** *{Of the Gershonites}* Ch. 26:21. That general which was before set down of the Levites’ courses begins here particularly to be applied to the several sons of Levi.

*{were Laadan}* Or, *Libni*, ch. 6:17; Num. 3:18.

*{and Shimei}* There were two of this name living together, and near of kin: One was Laadan’s brother, whose posterity is set down, v. 10; the other Laadan’s son, whose posterity is set down, v. 9.

**V. 8.** *{The sons of Laadan}* i.e. The posterity of Laadan living in David’s time. So is the word sons to be taken in the verses following. See v. 17, and ch. 26:22.

*{the chief was Jehiel, and Zetham, and Joel, three}* This word chief, Heb. *head*, is to be applied to every of these three. Every of them were heads in their several families.

**V. 9.** *{The sons of Shimei}* This Shimei was either one of the three sons of Laadan mentioned v. 8, one man having two names; or else he was a fourth son of Laadan.

*{Shelomith, and Haziel, and Haran, three. These were the chief of the fathers of Laadan}* He means the families that came from Laadan, whereof these three were the heads. By this it appears that Laadan’s stock was divided into two sorts of families, one sprouting out from himself, another from Shimei.

**V. 10.** *{And the sons of Shimei were Jahath, Zina}* Or, *Ziza*, v. 11.

*{and Jeush, and Beriah. These four were the sons of Shimei}* This was another Shimei than he that is mentioned v. 9. See v. 7.

**V. 11.** *{And Jahath was the chief}* For he was the eldest.

*{and Zizah the second}* Called Zina, v. 10.

*{but Jeush and Beriah had not many sons}* Heb. *did not multiply sons*. This is to be taken comparatively in relation to their two elder brethren, who had many more sons than these two younger.

*{therefore they were in one reckoning}* Reputed for one family.

*{according to their fathers house}* That one family came under their father’s name and title.

**V. 12.** *{The sons of Kohath, Amram, &c.}* See ch. 6:2.

**V. 13.** *{The sons of Amram, Aaron, and Moses}* See ch. 6:3; Exod. 6:20.

*{and Aaron was separated}* He and his posterity, Exod. 28:1, &c.; Heb. 5:4.

*{that he should sanctify the most holy things}* Heb. *holy of holy things*. Such things as belonged to the most holy and holy places, and to the holy services.

*{he and his sons for ever}* Exod. 28:43, and 29:28, 44.

*{to burn incense before the LORD}* See 1 Kings 9:25.

*{to minister unto him}* To do such services in the temple as had immediate respect unto God, as to offer all kind of sacrifices, to dress the lamps, to set the showbread in order before the Lord, and other like duties, Heb. 5:1.

*{and to bless in his name for ever}* Hereof see Num. 6:23.

**V. 14.** *{Now concerning Moses the man of God}* Of this title, Man of God, see 1 Kings 13:1. Of all that were called men of God Moses had the greatest honor, especially in regard of the sundry laws which by him God gave to his people, which are in that respect called the laws of Moses, Lev. 26:46. See 1 Kings 2:3. Besides, God never spoke with any man so familiarly as he did with him, Num. 12:8. See Ezra 3:2.

*{his sons were named of the tribe of Levi}* Though Moses were a prince, Acts 7:35, and instead of God to Aaron, Exod. 4:16, yet his sons were inferior to Aaron’s. For Aaron’s sons were priests, but Moses his sons ordinary Levites, ch. 26:24, 25. They were not named of Moses, but of Levi.

**V. 15.** *{The sons of Moses were Gershom and Eliezer}* See Exod. 2:22, and 18:3, 4.

**V. 16.** *{Of the sons of Gershom}* The eldest son of Levi had a name very near to this, differing only in the last letter, v. 6, yet sometimes this very name Gershom is given to him, ch. 6:17, 20.

*{Shebuel was the chief}* This implies that Gershom had more sons than Shebuel, though he only be mentioned.

**V. 17.** *{And the sons of Eliezer were Rehabiah the chief}* Or, *the first*. Under this word sons grandchildren and other posterity must be meant. Divers of these are by name set down ch. 26:25.

*{and Eliezer had no other sons}* This confirms the former note.

*{but the sons of Rehabiah were many}* Heb. *were highly multiplied*. God in this made a kind of compensation to Eliezer. Though he had but one son, yet that one son had many sons.

**V. 18.** *{Of the sons of Izhar}* The second son of Kohath, v. 12.

*{Shelomith the chief}* See v. 16.

**V. 19.** *{Of the sons of Hebron}* Another of Kohath’s sons, v. 12.

*{Jeriah the first, &c.}* This has relation to the three that follow.

**V. 20.** *{Of the sons of Uzziel}* Kohath’s youngest son, v. 12.

*{Micah the first, and Jesiah the second}* Some had more, some fewer families, according to the blessing of God.

**V. 21.** *{The sons of Merari}* The youngest son of Levi, v. 6.

*{Mahli and Mushi}* Exod. 6:16. See ch. 6:29.

*{The sons of Mahli, Eleazar, and Kish}* These two came to be one family. See v. 22.

**V. 22.** *{And Eleazar died, and had no sons, but daughters}* Such was the case of Zelophehad, Num. 27:3.

*{and their brethren}* Or, *kinsmen, or, cousin-germans*.

*{the sons of Kish took them}* Married them according to the law, Num. 36:10, 11.

**V. 23.** *{The sons of Mushi, Mahli, and Eder, and Jeremoth, three}* This Mahli was nephew to Mahli mentioned v. 21.

**V. 24.** *{These were the sons of Levi}* Num. 10:17, 21. This has reference to those who are reckoned up from v. 6 to this.

*{after the house of their fathers}* See v. 11.

*{even the chief of the fathers}* Chief in dignity and authority.

*{as they were counted by number of names by their polls}* See v. 3.

*{that did the work for the service of the house of the LORD}* See v. 4.

*{from the age of twenty years and upward}* Of other tribes, all that went forth unto the war were numbered from twenty years old and upward; but Levites were not admitted into the tabernacle till they were at least five and twenty. See v. 3. Indeed we read that the number of them was taken from a month old and upwards, Num. 3:15, but this was only to know how many there were of that tribe, and to consecrate them to God’s service: But now it may be that David, guided by God’s Spirit, saw cause to admit them into the house of God at twenty years old, either because their services required not so great strength when the temple was built, as they did when the tabernacle and all the holy things belonging thereto were to be carried upon men’s shoulders from place to place. See ch. 6:31. Or because now more were put into service than in former times. Or, David might ordain that the Levites at twenty years old should be admitted into the temple to behold the service of the Lord, and so to acquaint themselves the better therewith; and from five and twenty till thirty to assist elder Levites in their services, and after thirty to bear offices. See on Num. 3:15.

**V. 25.** *{For David said, The LORD God of Israel}* See 1 Kings 8:15.

*{hath given rest unto his people}* See ch. 22:9.

*{that they may dwell in Jerusalem}* Or, *and he dwelleth in Jerusalem*. The former reading implies that the children of Israel were now so settled as they should not be forced to go from place to place, and to carry the ark with them; the other, that the ark of God, and other representations of his presence were so settled in Jerusalem as not to be removed thence: In this respect God himself is said to dwell there. From both readings the same thing may be inferred, namely that Levites of twenty years old and upward might be admitted into the temple.

*{for ever}* See 1 Kings 8:13.

**V. 26.** *{And also unto the Levites}* Here must somewhat be supplied to make up the sense, namely, that which is in the beginning of v. 25, David said, *q. d.* *And also David said unto the Levites*.

*{they shall no more carry the Tabernacle, nor any vessels of it, for the service thereof}* This has reference to their custom in the wilderness, and afterwards, Num. 4:5, &c.

**V. 27.** *{For by the last words of David}* Hereby is meant David’s last ordinance about settling the Levites in their several courses. And mention is here made thereof, because the thing next following was then new, and had not been done before.

*{the Levites were numbered}* Heb. *the sons of Levi were a number*. They were taken into the catalogue of Levites that were admitted into the house of God.

*{from twenty years old and above}* See v. 24.

**V. 28.** *{Because their office was to wait on the sons of Aaron}* Heb. *their station was at the hand of the sons of Aaron*. A like phrase is used Neh. 11:24. The Levites were to fit and prepare such things as belonged unto the priests. Sundry particulars are mentioned in the words following wherein they were to attend the priests.

*{for the service of the house of the LORD}* Such public services as were done in the temple are here meant, as preparing oil for the lamps, bringing incense to the priests, together with the censers, and the like.

*{in the courts}* See 1 Kings 6:36.

*{and in the chambers}* See 1 Kings 6:5.

*{and in the purifying of all holy things}* Washing and cleansing all such things as were used in God’s house, is here meant.

*{and the work of the service of the house of God}* This general specified in the beginning of this verse, is here repeated as an head of those particulars which follow in the next verse.

**V. 29.** *{Both for the shewbread}* See ch. 9:32; 1 Kings 7:48.

*{and for the fine flour for meat offering}* See ch. 9:29; Lev. 2:1, &c. Of meat-offerings see 1 Kings 3:15.

*{and for the unleavened cakes}* See Exod. 12:39; Lev. 2:4, 5.

*{and for that which is baked in the pan}* Or, *flat-plate*. See Lev. 2:5, and 6:21.

*{and for that which is fried}* See Lev. 7:9, 12. These were several kinds of meat-offerings; whereof some were baked in the oven, others on a slice, others in a frying-pan, Lev. 2:4, 5, 7.

*{and for all manner of measure and size}* The former word in Hebrew is put for such a *measure* as declares the quantity of things, whether dry or liquid: It is used for a measure of water, Ezek. 4:11, 16. The latter is put for such a measure as declares the length or breadth of a thing: It sets out the measure of the curtains, Exod. 26:2, and 36:9. Tall men are in Hebrew called *men of measures*, ch. 20:6; Num. 13:32. Where there is difference made of measures, the former of these words is translated measure, in distinction from weights and meteyards; and the latter is translated *meteyard*, Lev. 19:35. Here is implied that the Levites were to see that all things were done with a fit proportion; as so much flour, so much wine, so much oil; yea also such a length or breadth of all things that used to be measured with a meteyard. All manner of things appertaining to the house of God were to be tried and proportioned by the measures and sizes which the Levites kept; and these were called the measures of the sanctuary. Whether the Levites had the ordering of civil measures and sizes, or no, is uncertain.

**V. 30.** *{And to stand every morning}* At the time of offering the morning-sacrifice, Exod. 29:39.

*{to thank and praise the LORD}* See v. 5.

*{and likewise at even}* When the evening-sacrifice was offered, Exod. 29:39. Yea sometimes they did solemnly praise the Lord in the night-time, Ps. 134:1.

**V. 31.** *{And to offer all burnt sacrifices unto the LORD in the Sabbaths}* The priests were to offer them on the altar, Lev. 1:5, &c. yet the Levites were to fit and prepare all to their hands. Of burnt-offerings see 1 Kings 3:15. Of offerings on the Sabbath, see Num. 28:9, 10.

*{in the new moons}* Of offerings on the new-moons see Num. 28:11, &c. New-moons were the beginnings of months, and used to be festival. See Num. 10:10; Ps. 81:3; 2 Kings 4:23; 2 Chron. 2:4, and 8:13.

*{and on the set feasts}* Of the several solemn feasts under the law, see Lev. 23:4, &c.

*{according to the order}* This has relation to the several ranks, courses and orders which David has set down, in every of which there was a set number that waited and did the service belonging unto them. See v. 6.

*{commanded unto them}* David set down the order, and commanded it to be observed from time to time, both in and after his days, but by warrant from the Lord. See v. 6.

*{continually}* That is, every day, morning and evening, constantly, and at other times appointed. See ch. 16:6.

*{before the LORD}* In the house of the Lord before the ark.

**V. 32.** *{And that they should keep the charge}* That they should observe that duty and place which was given them in charge. See 1 Kings 2:3; Lev. 8:35; Num. 1:53.

*{of the Tabernacle of the Congregation}* See 1 Kings 8:4. The ordinances which the Levites were to observe were given by Moses; and they were to be observed in the tabernacle which he made, and therefore it is said, *the charge of the Tabernacle*; but that charge was also to be kept in Solomon’s temple.

*{and the charge of the holy place}* The word place is not in the Hebrew. Many therefore apply it to *holy things*; *q. d.* *the charge which was to be kept about holy things*.

*{and the charge of the sons of Aaron}* That was to be helpful to the priests about those things which they did, Num. 3:6, 9. See v. 28.

*{their brethren}* This has relation to Levi, from whom came both priests and Levites, and in that respect were all brethren.

*{in the service of the house of the LORD}* See v. 28.

## CHAPTER 24

**V. 1.** *{Now these are the divisions of the sons of Aaron}* In the former chapter the number, courses and services of the Levites being set down in general, in this the orders of the priests and governors of the families of the Levites are distinctly registered. By the divisions he means the distinct orders or courses whereunto they were divided; and they were in number four and twenty, v. 18. The sons of Aaron were all of them priests.

*{The sons of Aaron, Nadab and Abihu, Eleazar and Ithamar}* Exod. 28:1; Num. 26:60. These four sons are set down by couples. The first couple died together, Lev. 10:1, 2. The younger couple lived long together [to] execute the priest’s function, v. 2.

**V. 2.** *{But Nadab and Abihu died}* An extraordinary death, for offering incense with strange fire.

*{before their father}* While their father lived. Thus it is said that *Haran died before Terah his father*, Gen. 11:28. See Num. 3:4; Lev. 10:3.

*{and had no children}* Num. 3:4. Anon after they were consecrated priests, Exod. 28:1, even when they were young men, before they were married they were taken away.

*{therefore Eleazar and Ithamar executed the Priests office}* They alone with their father, while he lived, and after their death, their posterity only, were priests. Had not Nadab and Abihu died without issue, their posterity had been priests as well as Eleazar’s and Ithamar’s. Yea Nadab, if he had lived, had been high priest after the death of his father. Mention is here made of these two, Eleazar and Ithamar, because all the priests in Israel throughout their several generations descended from them.

**V. 3.** *{And David distributed them}* i.e. The posterity of Eleazar and Ithamar; particularly, such of them as were in David’s time. David is said to do this, because by his appointment and in his presence it was done. See v. 6, 31.

*{both Zadok of the sons of Eleazar}* Zadok was the eldest at that time of Eleazar’s posterity. His sons were distributed.

*{and Ahimelech of the sons of Ithamar}* Ahimelech was the eldest, then living of the posterity of Ithamar. And his sons were then distributed. See 2 Sam. 8:17.

*{according to their offices in their service}* There were sundry offices belonging to the priests. There was ever one high priest, whose office it was to offer incense, and to go into the most holy place, Lev. 16:3, 12, 13; Heb. 9:7. The other priests offered all manner of sacrifices, Heb. 1:5, dressed the lamps, set the showbread in order, Lev. 24:3, 6, and did other services in the holy place, Heb. 9:6.

**V. 4.** *{And there were more chief men}* Heb. *for heads*. Such as were fit to be governors over others.

*{found of the sons of Eleazar then of the sons of Ithamar}* More of Eleazar’s posterity were living together in David’s time than of Ithamar’s.

*{and thus they were divided}* According to the number of the chief men of either stock.

*{Among the sons of Eleazar there were sixteen chief men of the house of their fathers}* Governors of the families descended from Eleazar.

*{and eight among the sons of Ithamar according to the house of their fathers}* But half so many as the other.

**V. 5.** *{Thus were they divided by lot}* This means was used to take away matter of envy from those that did divide, and matter of discontent from those that were divided: For by lot God’s mind and will thereabout was manifested. See ch. 6:6.

*{one sort with another}* Or, *one from another*. They were presented all together before the Lord, but by lot were divided one from another. The distinction and distribution of the persons was referred to the Lord, which should be first, second, third, &c.

*{for the governors of the Sanctuary}* The word translated sanctuary properly signifies *holiness*, Exod. 28:16. It is sometimes put for *a place of holiness*, or *an holy place*, Exod. 25:8, sometimes for *a thing of holiness*, or *an holy thing*, Ezek. 22:26. See ch. 9:29. Being thus taken here it may the more fitly be distinguished from the next clause, *the house of God*. And in this signification the governors here meant were such as had the charge and oversight of holy things.

*{and Governors of the house of God}* The word house is not in the original; only God is expressed, thus, *governors of God*: which some expound, *about divine things*; others, *appointed of God*.

*{were of the sons of Eleazar, and of the sons of Ithamar}* Some of Eleazar’s, others of Ithamar’s posterity.

**V. 6.** *{And Shemaiah the son of Nethaneel}* Other Levites were called Shemaiah; as, the son of Hashub, ch. 9:14, the son of Elizaphan, ch. 15:8, the son of Obed-Edom, ch. 26:4. But this was the son of Nathanael.

*{the Scribe}* See 1 Kings 4:3.

*{one of the Levites}* This distinguishes him from Shemaiahs of other tribes, as 1 Chron. 4:37, and 5:4.

*{wrote them}* These distributions of priests and Levites were matters of public concernment: And such things use to be put into public records.

*{before the King}* He was the chief both for ecclesiastical and civil affairs.

*{and the Princes}* They were under the king for affairs of state.

*{and Zadok the Priest, and Ahimelech}* These were chief about the affairs of the house of God. See v. 3.

*{the son of Abiathar}* See ch. 18:16.

*{and before the chief of the fathers of the Priests and Levites}* There were sundry orders of priests and Levites, distinguished into families; which families had overseers or governors: And there were some over these who are here called the chief. It was a great honor to the scribe to have such honorable witnesses of the thing he recorded.

*{one principal household}* Heb. *house of the father*. Or, *family*. Under this phrase so many were comprised as were set apart to one of the four and twenty divisions.

*{being taken for Eleazar}* To be accounted in the number of those that descended from Eleazar.

*{and one taken for Ithamar}* In this latter clause the word translated taken is twice set down together, thus, *taken, taken*: Which doubling of the word has its emphasis. See 1 Kings 8:13. It implies that as great respect was had to Ithamar’s posterity as to Eleazar’s. There were not only taken for Eleazar, but also, and in like manner, for Ithamar. The emphasis uses to be thus expressed, *one taken, I say, one taken for Ithamar*.

**V. 7.** *{Now the first lot came forth to Jehoiarib}* Lots used to be mingled, and put into a vessel all together, and thence taken out one by one. In which respect the lot is said to come forth. This Jehoiarib may be he that is mentioned ch. 9:10.

*{the second to Jedaiah}* Jedaiah is set before Jehoiarib, ch. 9:10.

**V. 8.** *{The third to Harim}* Frequent mention is made of this man’s posterity after the captivity, Ezra 2:32, 39, and 10:21, 31; Neh. 3:11, and 10:5, 27.

*{the fourth to Seorim, &c.}* We find no other mention of this man, nor of the three next following, namely, Malchijah, Mijamin, and Hakkoz.

**V. 10.** *{The eighth to Abijah}* By this man’s name the long continuance of the courses is manifested. For it is said of Zacharias the father of John the Baptist, that he was *of the course of Abijah*, Luke 1:5. See Neh. 12:4, 17.

**V. 18.** *{The four and twentieth to Maaziah}* The number of the courses was four and twenty, twice as many as the months in the year. The course here intended is called *ἐφημερία*, Luke 1:5, which according to the notation of the word imports a daily attending upon one’s work while his course or turn lasts.

**V. 19.** *{These were the orderings of them}* This has reference to the four and twenty courses set down in the verses going before.

*{in their service to come into the house of the LORD}* When they were to perform those services which particularly belonged unto them according to their distinct families, they came severally in their courses into the temple.

*{according to their manner}* Their manner or custom was to come upon a Sabbath-day, and to continue their attendance in the temple a whole week together. Afterwards they went to their city, and the next company or course succeeded them in that service. See ch. 9:25; 2 Kings 11:5.

*{under Aaron their father}* As Eleazar and Ithamar did their services in the house of God under their father Aaron, so the posterity of Eleazar and Ithamar did their services under that son of Aaron which was high priest in their time.

*{as the LORD God of Israel}* See 1 Kings 8:15.

*{had commanded him}* That course which the inferior priest observed under the high priest, was no human invention, but God’s appointment.

**V. 20.** *{And the rest of the sons of Levi were these}* The priests reckoned up v. 7, &c. were all sons of Levi. They that follow were also sons of Levi, yet no priests, and therefore are said to be the rest. Or, whereas there were three sons of Levi, Gershon, Kohath, and Merari, ch. 23:6, and the posterity of Gershon were before set down, ch. 23:7, &c. therefore the posterity of Kohath and Merari, who specially attended upon the priests, may be comprised under this phrase, the rest of the sons of Levi.

*{of the sons of Amram; Shubael}* Or, *Shebuel*, ch. 23:16. Amram was the father of Moses, Moses of Gershom, Gershom of Shebuel; so as Amram was the great-grandfather of Shebuel.

*{of the sons of Shubael, Jehdeiah}* This was another Jehdeiah, than he, ch. 27:30, for they were of several tribes.

**V. 21.** *{Concerning Rehabiah}* Mentioned ch. 23:17.

*{of the sons of Rehabiah, the first was Isshiah}* He was the chief.

**V. 22.** *{Of the Izharites, Shelomoth}* Or, *Shelomith*, ch. 23:18.

*{of the sons of Shelomoth, Jahath}* He was also one of the chief.

**V. 23.** *{And the sons of Hebron, Jeriah the first}* Hebron is not in the original, but well supplied out of ch. 23:19, and 26:31. Neither is this phrase, the first, in the original, but well supplied by reason of the words of number following. The three other persons following are before set down, ch. 23:19.

**V. 24.** *{Of the sons of Uzziel, Michah}* Michah is said to be the first, and a second added to him, ch. 23:20.

**V. 25.** *{Of the sons of Isshiah}* Heb. *The sons of Isshiah*. The particle of is well supplied by our translators. See ch. 2:8. From v. 20 to v. 26 is set down the posterity of Kohath: Among whom there were four heads or chief fathers; I. Amram; under whom were Shubael, Jehdeiah, and Isshiah, v. 20, 21. II. Izhar; under whom were Shelomoth and Jahath, v. 22. III. Hebron; under whom, Jeriah, Amariah, Jahaziel and Jekameam, v. 23. IV. Uzziel; under whom, Michah, Shamir, Isshiah, and Zechariah: thirteen in all.

**V. 26.** *{The sons of Merari were Mahli and Mushi}* In this and the verses following the posterity of Merari is set down.

*{the sons of Jaaziah, Beno}* Either he had more sons, or the plural number is put for the singular. See ch. 2:8. It is supposed this Jaaziah descended from Mahli. Of Beno see v. 27.

**V. 27.** *{The sons of Merari by Jaaziah, Beno}* Beno may be taken appellatively: So it signifies *his son*, and may be thus translated, *The sons of Jaaziah his son, (namely, the son of Merari) were chief*. So in the former verse, *Jaaziah his son*.

*{and Shoham, and Zaccur, and Ibri}* Beno being taken appellatively, only three descended from Jaaziah.

**V. 28.** *{Of Mahli came Eleazar}* Ch. 23:21.

*{who had no sons}* See ch. 23:22.

**V. 29.** *{Concerning Kish}* The second son of Mahli, ch. 23:21.

*{the son of Kish was Jerahmeel}* Heb. *the sons of Kish*. The word sons is here taken as v. 26.

**V. 30.** *{The sons also of Mushi}* Mushi was the second son of Merari, and sons are here put for posterity.

*{Mahli, and Eder, and Jerimoth}* See ch. 23:23.

*{These were the sons of the Levites}* These has reference to all set down from v. 20 to this place; whereof there were thirteen Kohathites, v. 25, and eleven Merarites; in all four and twenty heads of priests, v. 7, &c.

*{after the house of their fathers}* See v. 4.

**V. 31.** *{These likewise cast lots}* Their courses were distinguished by lot, as the priests were, v. 5.

*{over against their brethren the sons of Aaron}* See ch. 23:32. The Levites before mentioned were divided into courses as the priests had been, that the first course of Levites might attend upon the first course of priests; the Levites coming into the temple, when the priests did, and leaving their attendance when the priests went out.

*{in the presence of David the King, and Zadok, and Ahimelech, and the chief of the fathers of the Priests and Levites}* See v. 6.

*{even the principal fathers over against their younger brethren}* Heb. *the chief against his younger brother*. The singular number is in Hebrew oft used for the plural, and implies every one of such and such a rank. The meaning of this latter clause is, that elder and younger, all, of all sorts, were chosen by lot to the places wherein they served.

## CHAPTER 25

**V. 1.** *{Moreover}* There were three general functions appertaining to the Levites. One was, to attend the priests, and to be helpful unto them in their several services. Of those there were sundry orders set down ch. 23 and 24. The second, to solemnize the service of God with all manner of music. Their orders are set down in this chapter. The third was to be porters. Of whom ch. 26.

*{David and the Captains of the host}* By captains of the host are meant heads and chief commanders amongst the Levites. This title captains is given them in relation to the word host; for the company of Levites were as an holy host. I. Because as soldiers, they had their watches and wards in and about the house of God, Num. 2:17, and 4:5, &c. II. Because there were many companies of them, as there use to be of an army. III. Because they were mighty men of valor, at least many of them, ch. 12:26, 27, 28, and 26:6. IV. Because they were ready, as occasion was offered, to execute the judgment of the Lord, Exod. 32:26, &c.; 2 Chron. 26:17. This title captains is oft given to the heads of Levites, as ch. 12:28; 2 Chron. 23:1, 9. These captains of the host are joined with David to show that the king did nothing about the courses of the Levites, without the advice of the chiefest of them. See ch. 24:6.

*{separated to the service of the sons of Asaph, and of Heman, and of Jeduthun}* These three were masters of music, and oft joined together, as 2 Chron. 5:12, and 35:15. The two former had many of the psalms committed unto them, to put into a fit tune, as Ps. 73:1, and 74:1, and 88:1. See on 1 Sam. 8:2. The division of these was made by lot, as the divisions of others, v. 8. But being so divided, they were put on to their service by David and these captains.

*{who should prophesy}* They are said to prophesy, I. in regard of the matter of their music, which was divine prophecies or predictions of things to come. II. In regard of the manner, in that their music was acted with an holy zeal, such as prophets in their prophecies used.

*{with harps, with psalteries, and with cymbals}* See ch. 13:8.

*{and the number of the workmen}* Heb. *men of work*. i.e. men skillful and fit for the task whereunto they were put.

*{according to their service, was}* There were different tasks appointed to several orders. Some were to sound with sounding instruments, others to play on stringed instruments, others to sing with the voice; every of these exercised their skill about that task which was imposed upon them.

**V. 2.** *{Of the sons of Asaph, Zaccur, and Joseph, and Nethaniah, and Asarelah}* Otherwise called Jesharelah, v. 14. Their courses did not fall out by lot according to the precedency one before another, as here they are set down. For Zaccur was of the third rank, v. 10. Joseph of the first, v. 9. Nethaniah of the fifth, v. 12. Asarelah of the seventh, v. 14.

*{the sons of Asaph under the hands of Asaph}* These four being sons of Asaph were by their father ordered to sing or play what psalms and after what manner he saw meet.

*{which prophesied}* See v. 1.

*{according to the order of the King}* Heb. *by the hand of the King*. See 1 Kings 8:53. King David appointed what psalms should be sung, and what musical instruments should be used by Asaph and such as were under him; and Asaph appointed such particular men as should observe the king’s rules.

**V. 3.** *{Of Jeduthun; the sons of Jeduthun, Gedaliah}* This is an Hebraism, which may thus plainly be expressed, *Concerning Jeduthun, his sons were Gedaliah*. Of Jeduthun see v. 1. Gedaliah was of the second rank, v. 9.

*{and Zeri}* Or Izri. He was of the fourth rank, v. 11.

*{and Jeshaiah}* He was of the eighth rank, v. 15.

*{Hashabiah}* He was of the twelfth rank, v. 19.

*{and Mattithiah}* He was of the fourteenth rank, v. 21.

*{six}* With Shimei mentioned v. 17, who was of the tenth rank: For without him there are set down but five sons of Jeduthun.

*{under the hands of their father Jeduthun}* As the sons of Asaph were under him, v. 2, so the sons of Jeduthun under him.

*{who prophesied with a harp}* See v. 1.

*{to give thanks, and to praise the LORD}* See ch. 23:5.

**V. 4.** *{Of Heman; the sons of Heman}* See v. 3.

*{Bukkiah}* He was of the sixth rank, v. 13.

*{Mattaniah}* He was of the ninth rank, v. 16.

*{Uzziel}* Or, *Azareel*. He was of the eleventh rank, v. 18.

*{Shebuel}* Or, *Shubael*. He was of the thirteenth rank, v. 20.

*{and Jerimoth}* He was of the fifteenth rank, v. 22.

*{Hananiah}* He was of the sixteenth rank, v. 23.

*{Hanani}* He was of the eighteenth rank, v. 25.

*{Eliathah}* He was of the twentieth rank, v. 27.

*{Giddalti}* He was of the two and twentieth rank, v. 29.

*{and Romamti-Ezer}* He was of the four and twentieth rank. Some divide this word, and make it two names. But we find it set down for one person, v. 31. And if so be it should make two names for two persons, there would be more than four and twenty ranks.

*{Joshbekashah}* He was of the seventeenth rank, v. 24.

*{Mallothi}* He was of the nineteenth rank, v. 26.

*{Hothir}* He was of the one and twentieth rank, v. 28.

*{Mahazioth}* He was of the three and twentieth rank, v. 30.

**V. 5.** *{All these were the sons of Heman}* The fourteen mentioned v. 4.

*{the kings Seer}* See ch. 9:22, and 21:9; 2 Kings 17:13.

*{in the words of God}* Or, *matters of God*. He was, as a prophet, to declare the will of God.

*{to lift up the horn}* This phrase is added to show that he was appointed to set out the mighty acts of God, and the great conquests that he had given to Israel. Such things are comprised under the metaphor of an horn; because that in horns the strength of horned beasts lies, 1 Sam. 2:1, 10; Luke 1:69. Some think that Heman is called a seer, because he was employed about such psalms as set forth predictions of future things and times, and that concerning the stability of David’s kingdom, and the power of Christ; in which respect he is said to lift up the horn. Others take this for lifting up a musical instrument called an horn.

*{and God gave to Heman fourteen sons and three daughters}* This is added to show God’s blessing on Heman: He had more sons than both Asaph and Jeduthun, v. 2, 3.

**V. 6.** *{All these were under the hands of their fathers}* See v. 2.

*{for song in the house of the LORD}* To sing with voice, and play on instruments.

*{with cymbals, psalteries, and harps}* See ch. 13:8.

*{for the service of the house of God}* Such service as belonged to their particular function, which was music.

*{according to the Kings order}* Heb. *by the hand of the King*. See v. 2.

*{to Asaph, Jeduthun, and Heman}* David gave instructions to them, and they to such as were under them.

**V. 7.** *{So the number of them}* Namely, of the four and twenty sons of Asaph, Jeduthun and Heman.

*{with their brethren}* He means in general such as were of the tribe of Levi, but particularly the twelve that were in each of the four and twenty ranks.

*{that were instructed in the songs of the LORD}* All that were trained up to sing, or play on instruments, such psalms as were indited by the Spirit of the Lord, and set forth to his honor.

*{even all that were cunning}* That excelled others in the art of music, and had more than ordinary skill therein. For of such as were not so cunning as these, (at least, of whom there were many not so cunning) there were four thousand, ch. 23:5.

*{was two hundred fourscore and eight}* Twelve in every of the four and twenty ranks make up so many.

**V. 8.** *{And they cast lots}* i.e. David and the captains, v. 1. See ch. 24:5.

*{ward against ward}* By ward is meant a distinct company or classis: So as hereby is implied that the several companies stood together, some on one side, some on another; and according to the drawing forth of the lot they were set in their several orders or ranks. Or this may have reference to the four and twenty orders of the priests, ch. 27:4, &c. answerably to which these musicians were placed; as the ranks of other Levites were before, ch. 24:31.

*{as well the small as the great}* In the lot there was no partiality at all, nor respect to greatness. Whatsoever they were, great or mean, they were ranked as the lot fell upon them.

*{the teacher as the scholar}* That that is said of great and mean is also to be applied to instructors and learners.

**V. 9.** *{Now the first lot came forth for Asaph to Joseph}* Joseph was Asaph’s second son, v. 2. He with his brethren and sons made twelve, as all the rest that follow did; or else the sum of two hundred fourscore and eight, v. 7, cannot be made up.

*{the second to Gedaliah}* He and all the rest that follow to v. 31 were the sons of Asaph, Jeduthun and Heman, v. 2, &c.

**V. 31.** *{The four and twentieth}* There were just as many companies of musicians as there were of priests, ch. 24:18, and of Levites attending on the priests and helping them, ch. 24:31.

## CHAPTER 26

**V. 1.** *{Concerning the divisions}* This was the third kind of division of the Levites. See ch. 25:1.

*{of the porters}* The notation of a porter in Hebrew, Greek, Latin and English, besides other languages, is taken from a door, or gate: And the phrase used implies that they were for the gates. Now there being many gates appertaining to the temple, porch, and several courts, these the porters were to shut and open at seasonable times. They were also to wait and watch at the gates, to keep out all unclean persons and things, that nothing might enter or be brought in but such as by law might. See 2 Chron. 23:4, 5, 6, 19, and 35:15. They were likewise to keep peace and to prevent tumults; and in case any were, to thrust the tumultuous persons out of the courts of God’s house. Porters also had the charge of the treasures of the house of the Lord, v. 20. Their work was a work of great industry and diligence, and therefore they had their times of waiting, as well as other Levites.

*{Of the Korhites}* The Korhites descended from Kore, and he from Korah the son of Kohath the second son of Levi. See ch. 6:1, 22, and 9:19.

*{was Meshelemiah}* Or, *Shelemiah*, v. 14.

*{the son of Kore}* Of him before.

*{of the sons of Asaph}* Or Abiasaph, ch. 6:37, and 9:19. The different functions show that this Asaph was not the same that is mentioned ch. 25:1.

**V. 2.** *{And the sons of Meshelemiah were Zechariah, &c.}* These seven sons of Meshelemiah were heads of so many divisions of porters.

**V. 4.** *{Moreover the sons of Obed-Edom}* This was he in whose house the ark rested for a time; whereupon God is said to bless his house, ch. 13:14. For he himself was a porter, ch. 15:18, and 16:38.

*{were Shemaiah, &c.}* These eight sons of Obed-Edom were also heads of other divisions of porters.

**V. 5.** *{For God blessed him}* i.e. Obed-Edom. This inference implies that the blessing here meant was his numerous progeny. For besides eight sons of his own, he had also six grandchildren, named in the verses following. They were in all fourteen, and all called the sons of Obed-Edom, v. 8. Yet without question other blessings are intended ch. 13:14 than this of children.

**V. 6.** *{Also to Shemaiah his son were sons born}* Shemaiah was Obed-Edom’s son; so as the six following, though reckoned in the number of Obed-Edom’s sons, were his grandsons.

*{that ruled throughout the house of their father}* Or, *were like to the house of their father*. The former reading implies that they were governors over all those that were of their course, such as their uncles were: The latter, that they were of such valor as their progenitors were.

*{for they were mighty men of valour}* See ch. 5:24; 2 Kings 24:14. This is brought in to show wherein they were like their fathers. Of all the Levites the porters did most need valor; for they might oft meet with affronts.

**V. 7.** *{The sons of Shemaiah, Othni, &c. whose brethren}* whose has reference to the four sons of Shemaiah before mentioned.

*{were strong men}* Heb. *men of strength*. Strong in their limbs and courageous in God’s cause, and in executing their office. It being before said of all Shemaiah’s sons that they were mighty men of valor, and here concerning two of them, that they were strong men, it is implied, that they herein were more excellent than their brethren.

*{Elihu and Semachiah}* These are the brethren that were so strong.

**V. 8.** *{All these of the sons of Obed-Edom}* The fourteen before mentioned are here meant.

*{they, and their sons, and their brethren}* Brethren is here taken in a large sense for kindred, or such as were of the same family.

*{able men for strength}* Heb. *men of valor in strength*. i.e. Very valiant and strong.

*{for the service}* They were the more fit for the duties of their function by reason of their great strength and valor.

*{were threescore and two}* These were under those before mentioned.

*{of Obed-Edom}* The threescore and two last mentioned, as well as the fourteen before, descended all from Obed-Edom.

**V. 9.** *{And Meshelemiah}* This was the son of Kore, v. 1.

*{had sons and brethren}* Brethren is here to be taken as v. 8.

*{eighteen}* Those seven v. 2, 3 help to make up these eighteen.

**V. 10.** *{Also Hosah of the children of Merari}* Hosah was one of the porters appointed to be before the ark, ch. 16:38.

*{had sons}* Namely four, which hereafter are named.

*{Simri the chief}* There was some excellency in Simri that made his father prefer him before his brethren; and it may be also some fault in the elder that moved their father to prefer the younger: for by the law the firstborn was to be the heir, and to have the preeminence. See Gen. 4:7; Deut. 21:16.

*{for though he was not the firstborn}* He was not chief by the ordinary course of law, but upon some special and extraordinary reason.

*{yet his father made him the chief}* He thrust not himself into the highest place, but his father so disposed it.

**V. 11.** *{Hilkiah the second}* Here are four sons of Hosah set down, every of which were chief in their course or division, though Simri had by his father a preeminence given unto him.

*{all the sons and brethren of Hosah}* Brethren are here taken as verse 8.

*{were thirteen}* These thirteen, as the threescore and two, v. 8, and the eighteen, v. 19, were under such as were called chief.

**V. 12.** *{Among these were the divisions of the Porters}* i.e. Such divisions as were before noted of those that attended on the priests, ch. 24:31, and of the singers, ch. 25:31.

*{even among the chief men}* Such as were heads over others in their several divisions. The chief men here meant were those before named; seven sons of Meshelemiah, v. 2, 3, eight of Obed-Edom, v. 4, 5, six of Shemaiah, v. 7, and four of Hosah, v. 10, 11. All these make five and twenty: but because Shemaiah who is reckoned one of the sons of Obed-Edom, v. 4, was the father of those six, v. 7, he is to be reputed, as Meshelemiah, v. 2, and Obed-Edom, v. 4, and Hosah, v. 10, to have been an head, not over one division, but over all the divisions whereof his sons were heads; as a colonel is over all the captains and companies in his regiment. And thus the number of courses among the porters will be just four and twenty.

*{having wards one against another}* Heb. *against their brethren*. This phrase admits a double interpretation: I. That the porters had their divisions for warding as the singers and other Levites, their brethren, had; namely, four and twenty distinct divisions. II. That the porters in warding stood one company against the other, some at one door, some at another.

*{to minister in the house of the LORD}* To do those special services that belonged to their function, in the temple and the courts thereof.

**V. 13.** *{And they cast lots}* See ch. 24:5.

*{as well the small as the great}* Or, *as well for the small as for the great*. See ch. 25:8.

*{according to the house of their fathers}* See ch. 24:30.

*{for every gate}* Heb. *for gate and gate*. Some at one gate, others at another gate; and that both in the temple itself, and also in all the courts appertaining thereunto.

**V. 14.** *{And the lot Eastward}* At the entering into the temple; for they entered in at the east.

*{fell to Shelemiah}* Or, *Meshelemiah*, v. 1. He was the father of the first seven porters, named v. 2, 3.

*{Then for Zachariah his son}* His eldest son, v. 2.

*{a wise Counsellor}* Heb. *a giver of counsel with wisdom*. This is another commendation given to a porter of the house of God. It was said of some of them, that they were mighty men of valor, strong men, v. 6, 7. Here it is said of another, that he was wise; and to show that he used his wisdom not only about his own affairs, he is also said to be a counselor. He did with others consult about public matters; yea, he gave counsel and advice to others.

*{they cast lots}* See ch. 25:8.

*{and his lot came out Northward}* That was on the left hand as they entered into the temple.

**V. 15.** *{To Obed-Edom, Southward}* On the right hand.

*{and to his sons the house of Asuppim}* Heb. *gatherings*. Because they were places for gathering together and laying up the treasures and utensils of the house of God, and also for men to assemble together in. This phrase is diversely taken by expositors. Some take it for a treasury, where the treasures of the temple were laid up. To this they are the rather induced, because it is said, 2 Chron. 25:24, that all the gold and silver vessels appertaining to the house of God were with Obed-Edom, i.e. under the keeping and charge of his posterity. Others take it for a convocation-house, where the chief senators of the kingdom, or chief governors of the house of God, used to meet together to consult about public affairs. For this sense Neh. 12:25 is noted, where it is said that *porters were keeping the ward at the thresholds of the gates*. The word *Asuppim* is there translated thresholds; but in the margin, *treasuries*, or *assemblies*. Many understand such assemblies as were before spoken of: And such used to be at the gates of places. See 2 Kings 10:8. Such a word is put for assemblies, Eccles. 12:11. And the verb, whence the noun is derived, is put for gathering men together, Num. 11:16; 2 Sam. 6:1; Gen. 49:1. Our English translators take it for a proper name, and so translate it Asuppim.

**V. 16.** *{To Shuppim and Hosah}* We read of a Benjamite called Shuppim, ch. 7:12, 15, but we find not this name elsewhere given to a Levite. Some take Shuppim in this place for the father of Hosah; others, for his brother, and conceive that the families were united in one, and in that respect only one lot given to them both.

*{the lot came forth}* See ch. 24:5, 7.

*{Westward}* At the west end of the temple was the most holy place, and into it there was no gate: Neither was there any gate westward in any of the courts, except the court of the Gentiles: So as they who were under Shuppim and Hosah had that gate committed unto them.

*{with the gate Shallecheth}* This word taken for the name of a place is nowhere else used in Scripture. It signifies *a casting out*; and it is taken for a gate out of which they cast or carried away all manner of rubbish, sweepings, ashes, and other soil that was not fit to remain in the house or courts of the Lord. Some hold this gate was distinct from that which is before said to be westward. See v. 18.

*{by the causeway of the going up}* The temple being on the side of an hill, they went up to it: And at this gate there was a fair causeway, by which not only passengers, but also such things as were brought in or carried out of the temple, did pass up and down. And in this sense it may be called *the causeway of going up*. There is mention made of a causeway, 1 Kings 10:5, and 2 Chron. 9:4, which in both places is translated *an ascent*: But that was from the king’s house towards one of the fore-gates of the temple; so as this causeway cannot be thought to be that. There is another causeway mentioned 2 Kings 12:20, and translated *Silla*. That and this may imply one and the same common way.

**V. 17.** *{Eastward were six Levites}* The royal gate, through which the prince passed, and where he sat, Ezek. 44:1, 3, was eastward. More therefore attended at this gate than at others.

*{Northward four a day, and Southward four a day}* The gates on these two sides were of like use, and had like attendants.

*{and toward Asuppim}* See v. 15.

*{two and two}* Some expound this of waiting in course, two one day, two another. Others, that two out of those that waited at the north and south gates, were taken to wait here. Or rather it may be taken, that two waited without, and two within; so as there might be as many at this western gate as at the north, or south gate. Or, if there were two gates westward, as some hold, v. 16, then two might wait at one, and two at the other.

**V. 18.** *{And Parbar Westward}* Some take this to be another gate than that which is called Shallecheth, v. 16. Others take the word appellatively, for a place where sundry things belonging to the temple were laid up. It is a Chaldee word, and compounded, and signifies *the outward part*; and thus it may well agree to that which was before spoken of the gate Shallecheth, being set westward.

*{four at the causeway}* These were the two and two mentioned v. 17.

*{and two at Parbar}* Which was the other gate or place that stood westward.

**V. 19.** *{These were the divisions of the Porters}* See v. 12.

*{among the sons of Kore}* See v. 1.

*{and among the sons of Merari}* See v. 10.

**V. 20.** *{And of the Levites}* The Levites here intended and hereafter mentioned were questionless porters, as well as those before mentioned. For make those porters to be as many as the singers were, namely, four and twenty courses of them, and twelve in a course, they amount only to two hundred fourscore and eight, as ch. 25:7. But there is mention made of four thousand porters, ch. 23:5. So as there must be many more than those four and twenty courses; among which these that follow are to be reckoned.

*{Ahijah}* Here others of the posterity of Merari are set down; some were mentioned v. 10. Ahijah is set down as an head over others, and those others are comprised under him. For he alone was not over all the treasures mentioned in this verse.

*{was over the treasures of the house of God}* There were sundry sorts of treasures of the house of God. There was a treasury wherein sacred vessels and all manner of utensils for the temple were laid up, 1 Kings 7:51. There was a treasury for all such things as were freely given and dedicated to the Lord. Yea, likewise there was a treasury for such money as was upon occasion gathered for repairing and maintaining the temple. See 2 Kings 12:4. There was need therefore of many treasurers.

*{and over}* Some make this that follows to import the same treasury, with that which goes before, *and* thus translate it, *even over*. Others turn the copulative and into the disjunctive *or*.

*{the treasures of the dedicate things}* Heb. *holy things*. See 1 Kings 15:15.

**V. 21.** *{As concerning the sons of Laadan}* Or, *Libni*, ch. 6:17.

*{the sons of the Gershonite Laadan}* Laadan is called a Gershonite, because he descended from Gershon the eldest son of Levi, ch. 23:6, 7. The word sons is to be taken for posterity.

*{chief fathers}* Such as had power and authority over others, and were heads of companies.

*{even of Laadan the Gershonite}* It is an Hebrew pleonasm, thus to redouble the same words and phrases. The meaning is, that they which follow descended from Gershon by Laadan his son.

*{were Jehieli}* Or, *Jehiel*, ch. 23:8.

**V. 22.** *{The sons of Jehieli, Zetham, and Joel his brother}* All these three are said to be the sons of Laadan, ch. 23:8, but there sons is taken for posterity. All three were of the posterity of Laadan, but by two descents; Jehieli his son, one; Zetham and Joel his grandchildren, another.

*{which were over the treasures of the house of the LORD}* This office was attributed to Ahijah v. 20. But there were many chiefs over those treasures: besides, there were many treasuries: So as these two might well be colleagues and joined in the same trust.

**V. 23.** *{Of the Amramites, &c.}* Kohath the second son of Levi had four sons, Amram, Izhar, Hebron, Uzziel, ch. 23:6, 12. Of those four came these here named, Amramites, Izharites, Hebronites, and Uzzielites. These here set down were porters, but employed about the treasuries.

**V. 24.** *{And Shebuel the son of Gershom the son of Moses}* This relation is here added to distinguish this Gershom from another Gershom, or Gershon, the eldest son of Levi, ch. 23:6. Of Shebuel see ch. 23:16.

*{was ruler of the treasures}* This implies that Shebuel had some authority over those that had a charge of the treasures, namely over those hereafter mentioned.

**V. 25.** *{And his brethren by Eliezer}* Eliezer being Gershom’s brother, ch. 23:15, his son and sons’ sons are here called Gershom’s brethren in a large extent, being his kindred.

*{Rehabiah his son}* Rehabiah was Eliezer’s only son, ch. 23:17.

*{and Jeshaiah his son, &c.}* Rehabiah, Jeshaiah, Joram, Zichri, and Shelomith are some of the brethren intended in the beginning of the verse; being all of the posterity of Eliezer.

**V. 26.** *{Which Shelomith and his brethren were over all the treasures}* These were other treasures than those mentioned v. 22.

*{of the dedicate things}* See v. 20.

*{which David the King}* See ch. 18:11; 1 Kings 7:51.

*{and the chief fathers}* Heb. *heads of the fathers*. Or, *heads of families*; princes and great men.

*{the Captains over thousands and hundreds}* i.e. Colonels over regiments, and captains over companies.

*{and the Captains of the host}* Generals.

*{had dedicated}* All that had any command in war did use, in way of thankfulness for such successes as God had given them, to return and consecrate, as holy to the Lord, something of what they had gotten by war. See Num. 31:50.

**V. 27.** *{Out of the spoils won in battles}* Heb. *out of the battles and spoils*. Spoils belonged to the soldiers that took them from the enemy. Among them they had many times gold, silver, precious jewels, and other commodities of worth, Judg. 8:21, 24, 26; 2 Chron. 20:25. And of these they did use voluntarily to give a portion to the Lord.

*{did they dedicate to maintain the house of the LORD}* Heb. *to confirm, &c*. See 2 Kings 12:6. This shows one end why they gave spoils to the Lord. There were also other ends, as sacrifices, and other services appertaining to God’s house.

**V. 28.** *{And all that Samuel the Seer}* See ch. 9:22; 2 Kings 17:13. Samuel was a judge as well as a prophet, and great victories over the enemies were obtained in his regency, 1 Sam. 7:10, &c. he might therefore well consecrate spoils to God.

*{and Saul the son of Kish}* He also had sundry victories, 1 Sam. 11:11, and 14:20, &c. and 15:5, &c. and 17:52, &c.

*{and Abner the son of Ner}* General of Saul’s host, 1 Sam. 14:50, and 17:55.

*{and Joab the son of Zeruiah}* General of David’s host, ch. 11:6.

*{had dedicated}* By the forementioned examples he proves that general noted v. 26.

*{and whosoever had dedicated any thing}* This implies that many others had brought treasures, and consecrated them to the Lord, besides those before mentioned.

*{it was under the hand of Shelomith, and of his brethren}* This shows in what respect Shelomith and his brethren are said to be over the treasures, v. 26.

**V. 29.** *{Of the Izharites}* See v. 23.

*{Chenaniah and his sons}* There was one Chenaniah a master of music, ch. 15:22, 27, but this was of another function.

*{were for the outward business}* i.e. Such things as in the country were to be done, gathered and prepared for the house of God and for the service thereof; as all manner of provision of wood, coal, oil, wine, tithes, firstfruits, with the like. See Neh. 11:16; 2 Chron. 34:13.

*{over Israel}* Such Israelites as dwelt in the country up and down. By comparing v. 32 with this we may gather that their jurisdiction was on this side Jordan. Though the number of them that were for these outward works be not expressly set down, yet by comparing v. 30, 32 with ch. 23:4 we may gather what their number was, namely a thousand six hundred. For this number added to a thousand seven hundred, v. 30, and two thousand seven hundred, v. 32, makes up the six thousand, ch. 23:4.

*{for officers}* To oversee the outward businesses before mentioned.

*{and Judges}* To hear complaints, resolve doubts, determine cases, right wrongs, and to render to every one his own. Now the Israelites being governed by the judicial law, and the Levites best exercised therein, therefore they were sent abroad among the several tribes to be judges, Deut. 16:18; 2 Chron. 17:8, 9, and 19:11.

**V. 30.** *{And of the Hebronites}* These were the posterity of Hebron the third son of Kohath. See v. 33.

*{Hashabiah and his brethren}* By brethren are meant such as were of his kindred and office.

*{men of valour}* See v. 6, 7.

*{a thousand and seven hundred}* There were many things to do, and that in many places, and some were to rest by course while others were employed in the service, and therefore there was need of many.

*{were officers}* Heb. *over the things*. Such as are mentioned v. 29.

*{among them of Israel}* See v. 29.

*{on this side Jordan Westward}* This is opposed to that part which Reubenites and others inherited on the other side Jordan, v. 32.
