*{in all the business of the LORD}* Such as were counted ecclesiastical, and appertained to the house of the Lord and the services thereof.

*{and in the service of the King}* This may be taken for civil affairs. Such a distinction betwixt the matters of the Lord and of the king is made 2 Chron. 19:11. Or this phrase may imply the king’s appointing them to such and such businesses of the Lord, which they doing upon the king’s command are said to do the service of the king.

**V. 31.** *{Among the Hebronites was Jerijah the chief}* See v. 10.

*{even among the Hebronites according to the generations of his fathers}* Of all that were descended from Hebron, and then living, he was in greatest place and of highest account.

*{In the fortieth year of the reign of David}* This was the last year of David’s reign, 2 Sam. 5:4, when he was seventy years old.

*{they were sought for}* At this time David deputed Solomon to be his successor, and thereupon appointed distinct orders among the Levites and others, for the well ordering of church and commonwealth; and having assembled them together he declared his mind to them, ch. 28:1, &c. In this respect they are said to be sought for, namely, to come together.

*{and there were found amongst them mighty men of valour}* See v. 6.

*{at Jazer of Gilead}* A city belonging to the Merarites, Josh. 21:34, 39.

**V. 32.** *{And his brethren}* The kindred and colleagues of Jerijah, v. 31.

*{men of valour}* See v. 6.

*{were two thousand and seven hundred}* These were a great many Levites to be in two tribes and a half. Though all the Izharites and Hebronites were joined together, yet these make more than a half part. See v. 29.

*{chief fathers}* Or, *chief of their fathers’ families*.

*{whom King David made Rulers}* He, as sovereign on earth, had the disposing of the orders even of the Levites: yet he did it not without divine instinct and counsel of the Lord’s prophets.

*{over the Reubenites, the Gadites, and the half-tribe of Manasseh}* These dwelt on the other side Jordan, Num. 32:33. Now because they were far remote from the house of God, therefore they had the more Levites among them to instruct and direct them.

*{for every matter pertaining to God}* See v. 30.

*{and affairs}* Heb. *thing*.

*{of the King}* See v. 30.

## CHAPTER 27

**V. 1.** *{Now the children of Israel after their number}* In the four chapters immediately before this the orders of the Levites are set down; herein David showed his piety: In this chapter the militia of the kingdom is set down; and here his prudence is demonstrated. He had vanquished all his enemies abroad, and suppressed sundry insurrections at home; yet he knew that new wars and conspiracies might be raised: therefore in this time of peace he so settles his soldiery as they might be in a readiness on all occasions to defend the kingdom. This first clause has reference to the last words of the verse.

*{to wit, the chief fathers}* Heb. *heads of fathers, or families*.

*{and Captains of thousands}* These we call colonels of regiments.

*{and hundreds}* These are most usually with us called captains of companies. According to the Latin notation they are called centurions.

*{and their officers}* Such as were under them, as lieutenant-colonels, majors, lieutenants, and divers others.

*{that served the King}* These attended the court, where the king was, they guarded his person, they were ready to be sent out at his command. See 2 Chron. 17:19.

*{in any matter of the courses}* In any employment that belonged to their function, for the time that their several companies were to wait in their course.

*{which came in and went out month by month}* They that had waited a month, returned to their own houses to follow their particular employments; and then another course or company came in their stead to wait.

*{throughout all the months of the year}* There were twelve companies or courses, v. 15, each of which attended one month, and were about their own employments eleven months.

*{of every course (or, of each course) were twenty and four thousand}* There being twelve courses, the total number arises unto two hundred eighty-eight thousand.

**V. 2.** *{Over the first course for the first month}* From hence to verse 16 is a particular exemplification of that which was generally noted of the last clause of the former verse.

*{was Jashobeam the son of Zabdiel}* This was that worthy captain whose acts are set forth ch. 11:11.

*{and in his course were twenty and four thousand}* See on v. 1.

**V. 3.** *{Of the children of Perez}* Or, *Pharez*. He was the son of Judah, Gen. 30:29, and 46:12. This has relation to Jashobeam, v. 2, who was of the posterity of Pharez.

*{was the chief of all the Captains of the host for the first month}* In every course there were sundry companies, which had their captains, but he was the general commander over them all.

**V. 4.** *{And over the course of the second month was Dodai an Ahohite}* Or, *Dodo*, ch. 11:12; 2 Sam. 23:9. His son was one of David’s worthies.

*{and of his course was Mikloth also the Ruler}* He was a chief commander over the second course after the death of Dodo; or else he was a lieutenant-colonel or some other officer under Dodo.

**V. 5.** *{The third Captain of the host for the third month was Benaiah the son of Jehoiada}* This man was a great commander in the beginning of David’s reign, 2 Sam. 8:18, and continued faithful to David all his days, and to Solomon also, 1 Kings 1:36, 38. He was made general in the room of Joab, 1 Kings 2:33. This relation, the son of Jehoiada, distinguishes him from another of David’s worthies, who was a Pirathonite, ch. 11:31, and from certain Levites, ch. 15:18; 2 Chron. 20:14.

*{a chief Priest}* Or, *principal officer*. See ch. 4:2.

**V. 6.** *{This is that Benaiah who was mighty among the thirty, and above the thirty}* See ch. 11:24, 25; 2 Sam. 23:20, 22, 23.

*{and in his course was Amizabad his son}* This is here to be taken as that was which is noted of Mikloth v. 4.

**V. 7.** *{Asahel the brother of Joab}* See ch. 2:16, and 11:26.

*{and Zebadiah his son after him}* Asahel was slain in the beginning of David’s reign, 2 Sam. 2:1, 8, 23. After his death his son was substituted in his room: but because Asahel was a valiant man, brother to Joab and Abishai, and of kin to David, his name is put both into the catalogue of David’s worthies, ch. 11:26, and here, into the catalogue of chief commanders.

**V. 8.** *{Shamhuth the Izrahite}* Some take this man to be the same that is called Shammoth, ch. 11:27, and Shammah, 2 Sam. 23:25. But they were of different families. He that is set down in the catalogue of David’s worthies was an Harodite; but this man was an Izrahite. There was also another called Shammah in the catalogue of David’s worthies, but an Hararite, 2 Sam. 23:33.

**V. 9.** *{Ira the son of Ikkesh the Tekoite}* Ch. 11:28.

**V. 10.** *{Helez the Pelonite}* Or, *Paltite*, 2 Sam. 23:26. See ch. 11:27.

*{of the children of Ephraim}* This is added to distinguish him from another Helez, who was of the tribe of Judah, ch. 2:39.

**V. 11.** *{Sibbecai the Hushathite}* Ch. 11:29, and 20:4.

*{of the Zarhites}* These descended from Zerah, who came from Simeon, Num. 26:13.

**V. 12.** *{Abiezer the Antothite}* See ch. 11:28.

**V. 13.** *{Maharai the Netophathite}* See ch. 11:30.

*{of the Zarhites}* There was such a family of the tribe of Judah, Num. 26:20; Josh. 7:17.

**V. 14.** *{Benaiah the Pirathonite}* See v. 5, and ch. 11:24, 31.

**V. 15.** *{Heldai the Netophathite}* Or, *Heled*, ch. 11:30, or Heleb, 2 Sam. 23:29.

*{of Othniel}* The first judge of Israel after Joshua was Othniel, Judg. 3:9. If this be the Othniel here intended, his stock continued many generations.

*{and in his course were twenty and four thousand}* This is the twelfth time that this clause is repeated. See v. 1.

**V. 16.** *{Furthermore over the tribes of Israel}* Hitherto the orders and courses of commanders in war, with the companies under them, are set down. In the following part of the chapter such governors as were over the several tribes in times of peace are distinctly set down. There were thirteen rulers, though but twelve tribes, and Gad and Asher are left out: For Gad bordered upon Reuben, so as these two tribes were ruled by one governor; and Asher was governed by the same ruler that the tribe adjoining to it was governed by. There were thirteen governors, because Joseph’s sons made two tribes, Ephraim, and Manasseh, and Manasseh being divided by Jordan into two parts, each part had its governor, v. 20, 21. And the priests and Levites were so distinguished as they had two several governors, v. 17.

*{the Ruler of the Reubenites was Eliezer the son of Zichri}* There were many in several tribes called Eliezer, but this relation, the son of Zichri, distinguishes this governor from them all.

*{Of the Simeonites, Shephatiah the son of Maachah}* This parentage distinguishes this governor from the son of David, 2 Sam. 3:4.

**V. 17.** *{Of the Levites, Hashabiah the son of Kemuel}* This may be he who is mentioned ch. 26:30. He was the third governor.

*{Of the Aaronites, Zadok}* Zadok was the chief priest, and so over the other priests, called Aaronites; for all Aaron’s sons were priests.

**V. 18.** *{Of Judah, Elihu, one of the brethren of David}* Called Eliab, 1 Sam. 16:6. This was the fifth governor.

*{of Issachar, Omri the son of Michael}* This parentage distinguishes him from another Omri the son of Becher, ch. 7:8.

**V. 19.** *{Of Zebulun, Ishmaiah the son of Obadiah}* See ch. 12:4. This was the only Ishmaiah that we read of.

*{of Naphtali, Jerimoth the son of Azriel}* This might be of those valiant men that came to David in Ziklag, ch. 12:4.

**V. 20.** *{Of the children of Ephraim, Hoshea the son of Azaziah}* He that conquered Canaan was of this name, Deut. 32:44. But besides the time wherein they lived, their parentage does distinguish them.

*{of the half-tribe of Manasseh}* Namely, on this side Jordan.

*{Joel the son of Pedaiah}* His parentage distinguishes him from sundry other Joels mentioned in Scripture.

**V. 21.** *{Of the half-tribe of Manasseh in Gilead}* Gilead was on the other side of Jordan, Josh. 17:1, 5.

*{Iddo the son of Zechariah}* There were many called Iddo, but they were of other parentage. This was the eleventh governor.

*{of Benjamin, Jaasiel the son of Abner}* Abner the general of Saul’s host was of the tribe of Benjamin, 1 Sam. 14:50, 51.

**V. 22.** *{Of Dan, Azareel the son of Jeroham}* There was another of this name, ch. 12:6. But we read of no other of the tribe of Dan. Azariel was the thirteenth governor.

*{These were the Princes of the tribes of Israel}* See v. 16.

**V. 23.** *{But David took not the number of them from twenty years old and under}* The law when it speaks of numbering people, brings it to this limitation, twenty years old and upward, Num. 1:18, &c. and 26:2.

*{because the LORD had said}* Though David in the pride of his heart commanded the people to be numbered, ch. 21:2, yet he retained so much faith as to believe God’s word.

*{he would increase Israel like to the stars of the heavens}* See Gen. 15:5. This is here brought in as a reason why David would not have the people numbered under twenty years, lest thereby he might seem to doubt the promise of God, Gen. 22:17, and 26:4. See on Num. 1:2.

**V. 24.** *{Joab the son of Zeruiah}* See 1 Chron. 2:16.

*{began to number}* See ch. 21:4, &c.

*{but he finished not}* Ch. 21:6, and 2 Sam. 24:5, &c.

*{because there fell wrath for it against Israel}* While Joab was numbering the people, a great plague, which was the effect of God’s wrath fell among them, which was one cause that kept him from finishing the work he had begun, ch. 21:14. Of wrath see 2 Chron. 16:13.

*{neither was}* Heb. *ascended*.

*{the number of the account put into the Chronicles of King David}* After David was sensible of his sin, and discerned how displeasing it was unto God, to show his detestation of the fact, he would not suffer the number that was taken to be put into his civil records, which were the public chronicles of those times. But God, for instruction to future ages, caused it to be put into an holy record, which is the sacred Scriptures. The Hebrews here set down this word *חצי*, whereby they signify that this is half of the book; namely of the whole history of the Chronicles: which is true in regard of the verses.

**V. 25.** *{And over the Kings treasures}* David had before set down orders for safe keeping and well using the treasures of the house of God, ch. 26:20, &c. That was an act of piety; after that he took care of his own treasures, which was an act of prudence. Treasuries and treasures are not unlawful.

*{was Azmaveth the son of Adiel}* The treasures here meant were such as were in Jerusalem the chief city, or in the king’s court. For these treasures are distinguished from such commodities as were abroad in the country.

*{and over the storehouses in the fields}* The king caused storehouses to be built up and down, even in the fields, that such commodities as belonged to him might be laid up near the place where they did arise.

*{in the cities}* In several tribes, distinct from Jerusalem.

*{and in the villages}* These were less places, small towns.

*{and in the castles}* Strong places where the garrisons were kept.

*{was Jehonathan the son of Uzziah}* He had the chief care and trust of such commodities committed unto him.

**V. 26.** *{And over them that did the work of the field for tillage of the ground}* Which were to plow, sow, dung, fence, or any other way look unto the king’s land.

*{was Ezri the son of Chelub}* He was over farmers, husbandmen, and such other persons, to see that they were diligent and faithful.

**V. 27.** *{And over the vineyards}* These being a great commodity in those days, care was to be had to see them well planted, dunged, pruned, fenced, and other needful things done to them.

*{over the increase of the vineyards}* Heb. *over that which was of the vineyards*. Hereby is meant gathering of grapes in time of vintage, pressing of them, and making good wine.

*{for the wine cellars}* That the king’s wine might be carried into them.

**V. 28.** *{And over the olive trees}* What care was had of vineyards v. 27 must also be had of olive-trees: for from thence they had their oil.

*{and the Sycamore-trees that were in the low plains}* See 1 Kings 10:27.

*{was Baal-hanan the Gederite}* Though sycamores were but ordinary trees, such as grew in hedgerows, yet the king would have one take care there should be no spoil made of them.

*{and over the cellars of oil was Joash}* As David had some to see oil beaten out, so he had others to take care of it when it was brought into cellars.

**V. 29.** *{And over the herds that fed in Sharon}* Sharon was very fertile, Song 2:1; Isa. 3:39, and 35:2, and so a fit place for herds of cattle to feed in; for there were very good pastures. Some take it to be a plain champaign country lying along by Libanus, even from Bashan, ch. 5:16.

*{was Shitrai the Sharonite}* He might have this epithet Sharonite given him from Sharon.

*{and over the herds that were in the valleys}* These were other pasture-grounds besides Sharon.

*{was Shaphat the son of Adlai}* See ch. 5:12.

**V. 30.** *{Over the Camels also}* See 1 Kings 10:2.

*{and over the Asses}* See 1 Kings 2:40, and 13:13; 2 Kings 7:7.

**V. 31.** *{And over the flocks}* These were of smaller cattle, as of sheep, goats, and other such like.

*{All these were the Rulers of the substance, which was King Davids}* The particulars before mentioned show there were officers to look to all manner of commodities that belonged to the king throughout his whole dominion. See Ezra 4:22.

**V. 32.** *{Also Jonathan Davids uncle}* This Jonathan was David’s brother’s son, 2 Sam. 21:21, so as he was only David’s kinsman or nephew, and David was his uncle: but the word uncle is here taken in a large sense, for cousin.

*{was a counsellor}* One of whom David used in weighty matters to take counsel.

*{a wise man}* This shows that he was able to give good counsel.

*{and a Scribe}* Or, *secretary*. To be a king’s secretary and of his privy council is a very honorable place. See 1 Kings 4:3.

*{and Jehiel the son of Hachmoni (or, Hachmonite) was with the Kings sons}* He was as a tutor to give them good breeding, and to instruct them in learning. Some join Jonathan and Jehiel in this office.

**V. 33.** *{And Ahithophel was the kings Counsellor}* He was so wise as his counsel was accounted an oracle, 2 Sam. 16:23, yet for all his wisdom he hanged himself, 2 Sam. 17:23. That which is here spoken of his being David’s counselor was before Absalom’s conspiracy, 2 Sam. 15:31.

*{and Hushai the Archite was the kings companion}* Or, *friend*, 2 Sam. 15:37. He was such an one to David as we now call the king’s favorite. And he was a most faithful favorite, 2 Sam. 17:7, &c.

**V. 34.** *{And after Ahithophel}* Namely, after his death.

*{was Jehoiada the son of Benaiah}* This was not that Jehoiada who was the father of the valiant Benaiah, v. 5, but rather that Jehoiada who among the valiant priests came to David in the beginning of his kingdom, ch. 12:27.

*{and Abiathar}* This might be he that conspired with Adonijah, 1 Kings 1:7, or his father who was called both Ahimelech and Abiathar. See ch. 18:16. These two were of the king’s privy council.

*{and the General of the kings army was Joab}* See ch. 11:6.

## CHAPTER 28

**V. 1.** *{And David assembled}* This has reference to the beginning of ch. 23. For all that is registered from ch. 23:3 to ch. 8:1 contains a narration of things that were done before the assembly here mentioned was gathered together.

*{all the Princes of Israel}* Those princes are especially here meant that are set down ch. 27.

*{the Princes of the tribes}* Set down ch. 27:16, &c.

*{and the Captains of the Companies}* i.e. Of those companies that served in their several courses, ch. 27:2, &c.

*{that ministered to the King by course}* See ch. 27:1.

*{and the Captains over the thousands, and the Captains over the hundreds}* See ch. 27:1.

*{and the stewards over the substance and possession of the king}* Or, *cattle of the king*. The Hebrew word is sometimes taken for the one, sometimes for the other. The particular charge of these stewards is expressly set down ch. 27:25, &c.

*{and of his sons}* This in the Hebrew is so set down as if hereby were meant the stewards of the substance of David’s sons; and so our English will well carry it. Others make these a part of the assembly; *q. d.* *David assembled the king’s sons, as well as the princes and others*.

*{with the officers}* Or, *eunuchs*. See 1 Kings 12:9.

*{and with the mighty men, and with all the valiant men}* These were they who are set down ch. 11:12, and others like to them.

*{unto Jerusalem}* This has reference to the first clause of this verse, *q. d.* *David assembled unto Jerusalem all the princes*, &c.

**V. 2.** *{Then David the king stood up upon his feet}* Many take that which follows to be done after Adonijah’s conspiracy, and David’s declaring Solomon to be king, 1 Kings 1:5, &c. and that David in so great a business as this was gathered his spirits together, and rose up in reverence to God, whose cause he had in hand; as Jacob in a like case did, Gen. 47:38, and as Eglon did, Judg. 3:20. They who take this assembly to be before David was so weak, say that he stood upon his feet partly to testify his good respect to that assembly, and partly that he might be the better heard of them all.

*{and said, Hear me}* By this phrase he stirs up their attention. So Gen. 23:6, and 49:2.

*{my brethren}* This phrase he uses in relation 1. to God and the church, in which respect king and subjects are as brethren; 2. to their father Jacob, from whom he as well as his subjects descended; 3. to the common condition, they were all flesh.

*{and my people}* This has relation to that sovereignty and charge which God has given him over them. By these two phrases he insinuates himself into their hearts.

*{As for me, I had in mine heart to build an house}* See ch. 17:1, &c.

*{of rest}* See ch. 6:31.

*{for the Ark of the Covenant of the LORD}* See 1 Kings 8:10.

*{and for the footstool of our God}* This is a description of the ark, which is oft styled God’s footstool, as Ps. 99:5, and 132:7; Lam. 2:1, and that in these respects: I. God being said to dwell between the Cherubims, and to speak from between them, Exod. 25:22; Num. 7:89; 2 Kings 19:15; 1 Chron. 13:6, the ark being under the cherubims, is said to be his footstool. II. To show that though the ark were a representation of his presence, yet it was but as his footstool on earth; his throne was in heaven, Acts 7:49; Matt. 5:34, 35. III. To show that they were not to rest in that evidence of God’s presence which was but his footstool, but from thence to raise up their hearts higher, even unto heaven, which was his throne. See v. 18.

*{and had made ready for the building}* What preparations David had made for this purpose may be seen ch. 22:2, 3, 4, 14, 15, 16.

**V. 3.** *{But God said unto me}* Namely by his prophet Nathan. See on 2 Sam. 7:5.

*{Thou shalt not build an house for my name}* Ch. 17:4.

*{because thou hast been a man of war}* See ch. 22:8.

*{and hast shed blood}* Heb. *bloods*. The plural number implies much blood. It is thus expressed ch. 22:8, *Thou hast shed blood abundantly*.

**V. 4.** *{Howbeit the LORD God of Israel}* See ch. 8:15.

*{chose me before all the house of my father}* Before all my brethren, 1 Sam. 16:12.

*{to be king over Israel for ever}* This may be taken either of David’s own person; and so it extends to the continuance of his life on earth, as Exod. 26:1. Or it may be extended to his seed; and thus it is to be taken of the polity of the Jews; so long as it continued: Or to Christ; and so the word ever is properly taken. See ch. 17:12.

*{for he hath chosen Judah to be the Ruler}* Among the tribes he chose Judah, that out of it a king should be taken to rule over Israel, Gen. 49:8; Ps. 78:68. David here sets down a gradation of four steps, wherein he does exemplify God’s free grace in making choice of whom he will: 1. the tribe of Judah; 2. the house of Jesse; 3. David; 4. Solomon. None of these, before God chose them, was the chief. Judah was the fourth son of Jacob, Gen. 29:35. The house of Jesse was none of the greatest families of Judah. See 1 Kings 12:16. David was the youngest of Jesse’s eight sons, 1 Sam. 16:11, and Solomon had at least six brethren elder than himself; See ch. 3:4, 5.

*{and of the house of Judah, the house of my father}* Ch. 2:3, 12, 15.

*{and among the sons of my father he liked me}* This is to be taken of God’s free grace and mere good pleasure, not of any desert of David.

*{to make me king over all Israel}* This has reference partly to the beginning of David’s reign, at which time he was king only over Judah, and partly to Rehoboam’s loss of ten tribes, ch. 14:2; 1 Kings 14:16.

**V. 5.** *{And of all my sons (for the LORD hath given me many sons)}* Nineteen sons of David are reckoned up together, ch. 3:1, &c.

*{he hath chosen Solomon my son}* See ch. 22:9, 10.

*{to sit upon the throne of the kingdom}* A throne is a proper seat for a king; and to sit upon it is to reign as a king.

*{of the LORD over Israel}* Israel was God’s peculiar people, Exod. 19:5, 6, and God’s only church at that time, and in that respect called the kingdom of the Lord. For though the whole world be God’s universal kingdom, yet the church is his special and peculiar kingdom, over which he has the greatest care. See ch. 29:23.

**V. 6.** *{And he said unto me, Solomon thy son}* This has reference to 2 Sam. 7:13, where David’s son is indefinitely mentioned, but Solomon not named; yet by the description of that son there set down it may well be gathered that Solomon is intended.

*{he shall build my house}* The temple which consisted of the most holy place, holy place and porch, is most properly styled God’s house. See 1 Kings 3:1.

*{and my courts}* See 1 Kings 6:36. In these courts were sundry gates, walls, partitions and other buildings; in which respect this phrase of building the courts is properly used.

*{for I have chosen him to be my son}* It was God’s free grace and love to Solomon that advanced him above his brethren, God chose him.

*{and I will be his father}* See ch. 17:13.

**V. 7.** *{Moreover I will establish his kingdom for ever}* See ch. 17:14.

*{if he be constant}* Heb. *strong*. The word has especial relation to that courage of mind which causes an unalterable resolution, whence follows constancy. The manner of setting down this inference shows that there be conditions to be observed on man’s part for obtaining God’s promises.

*{to do my Commandments and my Judgments}* See 1 Kings 2:3.

*{as at this day}* By this it appears that Solomon had been well instructed by his father in the commandments and judgments of God, and that he had in his father’s lifetime well observed them. See 1 Kings 8:61.

**V. 8.** *{Now therefore in the sight of all Israel}* He means the representative body of Israel there present, v. 1, and to them he directs this his exhortation: So as he makes all of them, one to another, witnesses of the charge he gave them, that they might be the more careful in observing it.

*{the Congregation of the LORD}* This sets out the church of God, as that phrase, the kingdom of the Lord, v. 5, and this makes the witnesses to be of greater account, and the charge more weighty.

*{and in the audience of our God}* Heb. *in the ears*. i.e. Who hears this charge that is given, and who is in special manner our God. This adds more weight to the charge.

*{keep and seek}* These words are of the plural number, and spoken to all the people. And they imply that the people must keep what they knew, and seek to be instructed in what more belonged to them.

*{for all the commandments}* We must labor both to be instructed in the whole will of God, and also to endeavor to keep all his precepts.

*{of the LORD your God}* See ch. 22:18.

*{that you may possess this good land}* i.e. The land of Canaan in which they dwelt. It was a very pleasant and fertile land, abounding with all manner of needful commodities; in which respect it is said to be *a land flowing with milk and honey*, Exod. 3:8; Deut. 26:9. See 1 Kings 14:15.

*{and leave it for an inheritance for your children after you}* God had given that land to Abraham and his seed for an inheritance, Gen. 15:7; Deut. 3:21, 38, but it was upon condition of observing God’s commandments, Deut. 4:1, 26. Therefore David calls upon the people to observe that condition, as they did desire to leave that land to their children.

*{for ever}* See 1 Kings 2:23, 45.

**V. 9.** *{And thou}* The exhortation v. 8 was made to the whole kingdom, but now David turns to his son, and gives him his charge.

*{Solomon my son}* David sweetly insinuates himself into the heart of his son, by calling him by his proper name, and by expressing that special relation which was betwixt them, in this phrase, my son, as if he had been his only son.

*{know thou the God}* Knowledge of God is the ground of all other duty; without which all obedience is but blind obedience.

*{of thy father}* David hereby means himself. Now David had taken the true God for his God, and that God had exceedingly blessed David. In which respect he here makes mention of that relation betwixt God and him, to quicken up his son thereby.

*{and serve him}* As this word in general comprises all manner of obedience of God, so in special it intends his worship, Exod. 10:7.

*{with a perfect heart}* See 1 Kings 8:61.

*{and with a willing mind}* Forced service is not acceptable to God.

*{for the LORD searcheth all hearts}* This is added as a reason to move Solomon to serve God sincerely and willingly, because the Lord does not only observe the outward service that is done, but also tries the very disposition of the soul, and knows with what intent and after what manner it is done.

*{and understandeth all the imaginations of the thoughts}* i.e. The first motions of the soul, which set men on to do what they do.

*{if thou seek him}* To seek God is either generally taken for the use of all means wherein and whereby we may come to the knowledge of him, Isa. 51:1, or else in particular for the calling upon his name, Ps. 34:10. It may be here taken in both senses. See ch. 16:10, 11.

*{he will be found of thee}* By careful using of right means we may attain to the right knowledge of God; in which respect he may be said to be found. Or when he hears our prayer, and grants our request, he is found.

*{but if thou forsake him}* They especially are said to forsake God who having once known and professed him do turn from their holy profession unto idolatry or any other impiety or iniquity. See 1 Kings 18:18.

*{he will cast thee off for ever}* He will renounce you, and not acknowledge you to be his, as Matt. 7:23. See 2 Kings 23:27. This phrase for ever implies that the forsaking of God before mentioned is meant of a total final apostasy.

**V. 10.** *{Take heed now}* This phrase implies a diligent circumspection over a man’s heart and life.

*{for the LORD hath chosen thee}* See v. 5, 6.

*{to build an house for the Sanctuary}* The Hebrew word may be applied to *a holy place*, or to *a holy thing*. It is oft put for the tabernacle, and for the temple. Here it is put for the ark, which was the most holy representation of God’s presence that the Jews had. See ch. 9:29, and 1 Kings 6:19.

*{be strong}* This has especial relation to courage of mind.

*{and do it}* They who take courage to themselves, will much better accomplish what they undertake. See ch. 22:13; 1 Kings 2:2.

**V. 11.** *{Then David gave to Solomon his son the pattern}* Though David were not suffered himself to build the temple, yet his heart was so set upon it as he meditated thereon continually, and in himself considered what kind of house it should be, what parts thereof, what partitions therein. And questionless he oft called upon God to direct him therein: and we cannot but think God heard his prayer, and by his Spirit enlightened his understanding, (for so it is expressly said, that he had a pattern of all by the Spirit, v. 12 and 19) so as he came to discern in every particular what kind of house it should be; and answerably he set it down in a platform which is here called a pattern, and delivered it to his son. That this pattern was made by divine instinct, is evident v. 12. By this means Solomon had such an help as Moses had, Exod. 25:40.

*{of the porch}* The porch is here synecdochically put for the whole temple, as the particulars following, which are said to appertain thereunto, do evidently demonstrate. Of the porch in particular see 1 Kings 6:4.

*{and of the houses thereof}* Hereby may be meant the distinct parts of the temple, which were within the porch, and into which the priests went through the porch, and also the rooms built round about the temple. Of which see 1 Kings 6:5, &c.

*{and of the treasuries thereof}* See ch. 9:16, and 26:20, 22, &c.

*{and of the upper chambers thereof}* See 1 Kings 6:5.

*{and of the inward parlours thereof}* Under this phrase such rooms as were within the walls of the temple for the priests’ use may be comprised.

*{and of the place of the mercy seat}* This was called the oracle, and most holy place, 1 Kings 6:5, 16. The mercy-seat is here synecdochically put for the ark and all things appertaining thereunto. The word translated mercy-seat signifies *a cover*: for it covered the ark. It is in Latin called *propitiatorium*, *a place or means of being appeased*. It was called a propitiatory or a mercy-seat, because there God showed himself appeased, merciful and gracious to his people. In the ark was the moral law, which denounced a curse against all mankind, because they were sinners: This cover kept the law within the ark, that it should not be produced against us, and in that respect it might well be called a propitiatory, or mercy-seat. God was set forth in his glorious majesty between the two cherubims, ch. 13:6; 2 Kings 19:15. Now this cover of the ark was betwixt the law, and the divine majesty; and it typified Christ, who is that mediator that keeps us safe from the curse of the law, and presents himself before God’s glorious majesty for us. It is called mercy-seat, because God in Christ sits on a seat of mercy and throne of grace. See 1 Kings 6:19.

**V. 12.** *{And the pattern}* See v. 11.

*{of all that he had}* Heb. *of all that was with him*. i.e. Of the main buildings, and several rooms appertaining to them, and of all the things that were therein to be employed about God’s service.

*{by the Spirit}* The divine Spirit is here meant, which instructed David’s spirit, and inwardly suggested unto him how all things should be made. See v. 19.

*{of the courts of the house of the LORD}* See 1 Kings 6:36. Here David begins to exemplify in particular that which in general was comprised under this phrase, all that he had.

*{and all the chambers round about}* See ch. 6:5, &c.

*{of the treasuries of the house of God}* See v. 11.

*{and of the treasuries of the dedicate things}* See ch. 26:20, 26, 27.

**V. 13.** *{Also for the courses of the Priests and the Levites}* Or, *of the courses*: For this has reference to v. 12. David gave the pattern of these things as well as of others. Of these courses see ch. 23, 24, 25, and 26.

*{and for all}* Or, *of all*. For this depends on the word pattern v. 12.

*{the work of the service of the house of the LORD}* What work the priests should do, what the Levites, what also was to be done of the people who were to bring sacrifices, and who were to be made clean.

*{and for all}* Or, *of all*.

*{the vessels of service in the house of the LORD}* Such as were used in any service in the temple. Of these see 1 Kings 7:40, &c.

**V. 14.** *{He gave of gold by weight}* Having set down what things should be made of gold, he weighed out so much as might be sufficient for them all.

*{for things of Gold}* All the things in the most holy place, yea and most of them that were in the holy place, were of gold. See 1 Kings 7:48, &c.

*{for all instruments of all manner of service}* Heb. *of service service*. The Hebraism is well expressed in our English translation. By instruments of service he means not only vessels before mentioned, but also hinges, locks, keys, and what else was used in the temple. The forementioned clause may thus be read, *even of the gold for all instruments of all manner of service*. If it be so read, the first words in this verse, *he gave*, need not be prefixed; for this verse also has reference to v. 12.

*{silver also for all instruments of silver}* There were sundry things in the temple made of silver, v. 15, 16, 17. So in the tabernacle, Exod. 36:30, 36, and 38:10, &c.

*{by weight for all instruments of every kind of service}* i.e. Such as were to be made of silver; as before he meant such instruments as were made of gold.

**V. 15.** *{Even the weight for the candlesticks of gold}* Solomon made ten several candlesticks of gold, 1 Kings 7:49. In the tabernacle there was but one, and that of a talent of gold, Exod. 25:31, 39. But every of Solomon’s ten candlesticks was far greater than that one which Moses made; so as there needed a great deal of gold for the candlesticks, besides other things.

*{and for their lamps of gold}* i.e. Those vessels whereinto the oil and wick of the lamps were put, and set upon the branches of the candlesticks. See 1 Kings 7:49.

*{by weight for every Candlestick, and for the lamps thereof}* David did proportion out the very matter and quantity for every of the holy things, as well as the fashion of them.

*{and for the Candlesticks of silver}* The candlesticks of gold were very great ones, and had many branches, and constantly remained in one place, 1 Kings 7:49; 2 Chron. 4:7, 20; Exod. 25:31, &c. But there was use of candlesticks to be carried up and down, in the courts and chambers where the priests and Levites were to be: and these were of silver.

*{by weight both for the Candlesticks, and also for the lamps thereof}* It appears that they used oil, whereof lamps were, and no candles in the house of God, yea, and in the chambers of the priests.

*{according to the use of every Candlestick}* Whether constantly settled in one place, or carried up and down.

**V. 16.** *{And by weight he gave gold}* *He gave* is not in the Hebrew, and may well be left out. For he continues to speak of a pattern, not of the gift itself. See v. 11, 12, 13, 14.

*{for the tables of shewbread}* See 1 Kings 7:48.

*{for every table}* There were ten tables, 1 Kings 7:48; 2 Chron. 4:8.

*{and likewise silver for the tables of silver}* There were tables in the priests’ courts to lay those things thereon which they there used; and these were of silver. It may be also the tables in the treasuries and priests’ chambers were of silver.

**V. 17.** *{Also pure gold}* See 1 Kings 10:21; 2 Chron. 4:20.

*{for the fleshhooks}* The proper use of flesh-hooks was to take meat out of the pan, or any other vessel, wherein any part of a sacrifice was sod, 1 Sam. 2:13, 14. We read of flesh-hooks of brass, Exod. 27:3; 2 Chron. 4:16, but nowhere of gold, except this place.

*{and the bowls, and the cups}* Cups and bowls were for the same use. See 1 Kings 7:50.

*{and for the golden basons}* See 1 Kings 7:50.

*{he gave gold by weight}* See v. 14, 16.

*{for every bason}* For they used many basins, Ezra 1:9; Neh. 7:70.

*{and likewise silver by weight for every bason of silver}* They used silver basins in the courts and chambers appertaining to the temple.

**V. 18.** *{And for the Altar of incense}* See ch. 6:44.

*{refined gold by weight}* Refined gold is that which is again and again tried in the fire, Zech. 13:9; Mal. 3:2, 3. It is called pure gold, v. 17, and fine gold, 2 Chron. 3:5, 8. See 1 Kings 9:28.

*{and gold for the pattern of the chariot of the Cherubims}* Because God is said to sit between the Cherubims, Ps. 99:1, and to dwell betwixt them, 13:6, and to be carried or to ride upon them, Ps. 18:10, this phrase, chariot of the cherubims, is used; which sets forth the majesty of God sitting or riding in a chariot. It also might give them to understand that God might ride away from them if they were not careful to give him good entertainment.

*{that spread out their wings}* See 1 Kings 6:24.

*{and covered the Ark of the Covenant of the LORD}* See 1 Kings 6:9. Here, as the ark itself, so the mercy-seat that covered it, and the smaller cherubims which Moses made, and were fastened to that cover, Exod. 25:18, are meant. For all these were covered with the great cherubims mentioned in this verse.

**V. 19.** *{All this, said David, the LORD made me to understand in writing}* His meaning is, that God so inspired him, and gave him so full understanding of all the things before mentioned, as he could distinctly set them down by writing, and so leave them as a pattern to direct his son how to make all. For David was a man of God, 2 Chron. 8:14, and he did what he did herein by the Spirit of God, v. 12. Or the pattern set down in writing might be brought him by some prophet, 2 Chron. 29:25.

*{by his hand upon me}* By the hand of God may be meant the Spirit of God. Or this phrase may be used in allusion to Moses his receiving a pattern of the tabernacle from the hand of God himself, Exod. 25:40.

*{even all the works of this pattern}* All those mentioned before, and sundry others not here set down in particular.

**V. 20.** *{And David said to Solomon his son}* After he had given him direction what to do, he invites him to be diligent in doing it.

*{Be strong and of good courage, and do it}* See v. 10, and ch. 22:13.

*{fear not, nor be dismayed}* See ch. 22:13.

*{for the LORD God}* By this phrase he puts him in mind of the power of God, who would enable him to do his work.

*{even my God}* By this he puts him in mind of God’s special favor towards him, and promise made to him, ch. 17:8, 11, &c.

*{will be with thee}* See ch. 22:11.

*{he will not fail thee, nor forsake thee}* See Josh. 1:5. i.e. God would not cease to favor, assist and bless him. They who make a promise to a friend to do this and that for him, but do it not, are said to fail him; God will not do so. They who having begun to afford some help, but cease to help before the work be done, are said to forsake him; neither will God do so.

*{until thou hast finished all the work}* Though this principally imply that God would afford him his assistance and blessing continually to the very end of his work, yet it does not determine God’s presence with him only till that time: for the word until is oft used for perpetuity; God will never forsake thee. See 2 Sam. 6:23; Matt. 1:25.

*{for the service of the house of the LORD}* See v. 13.

**V. 21.** *{And behold}* That which follows, concerning all sorts of people to be ready to assist Solomon, was a matter remarkable.

*{the courses of the Priests and the Levites}* See ch. 23, 24, 25, and 26. The meaning is, that all the priests and Levites in their several courses should do as follows.

*{even they shall be with thee}* Assistant to you by affording their best counsel and aid.

*{for all the service of the house of the LORD}* See v. 13.

*{and there shall be with thee for all manner of workmanship}* About things of gold, silver, brass, wood, or any other materials.

*{every willing and skilful man}* These two things are requisite for affording of good help, and for well effecting a thing, skill or ability, and willingness and readiness of mind. Without the former a man can do no good, without the latter he will do none.

*{for any manner of service}* Sundry kind of services were requisite for building the temple, and making the things appertaining thereunto; but David here implies that there should be men fit for everything.

*{also the Princes}* Such are noted v. 1.

*{and all the people}* They of inferior rank and sort.

*{will be wholly at thy commandment}* They will be so far from rebelling against you, or hindering your work, as they will do what you shall appoint them.

## CHAPTER 29

**V. 1.** *{Furthermore, David the king said unto all the congregation}* The first word shows that David turns his speech to those that were present before him. This was such a congregation as is mentioned ch. 13:2.

*{Solomon my son whom alone}* Heb. *one*. That is, my one son. One is put for one alone, or for an only one.

*{God hath chosen}* See ch. 28:5. The particle one has reference to this word chosen; for David had many sons, but God chose only Solomon to be king, ch. 28:5.

*{is yet young and tender, and the work is great}* See ch. 22:5.

*{for the palace is not for man}* A palace uses to be the fairest of buildings. It is a title given to the royal house of a king. In that he says it is not for man, he implies that it was more magnificent and excellent than might be made for any man. In what respect it was so, see ch. 22:5.

*{but for the LORD God}* For his name, for his glory; to set forth the excellency of his Son Christ, and the many benefits that might be expected from him.

**V. 2.** *{Now I have prepared}* David reckons up what he himself had conferred towards the building of the temple, and for all things appertaining thereunto, not for ostentation, to boast of what he had done, but in a humble acknowledgment of God’s goodness in bestowing upon him what he had given towards that work, and also in giving him a heart to return it unto God for his service, v. 14, 16, and thereupon he blessed the Lord, v. 10. Besides, he makes mention before his princes of what he had done, to stir them up to do the like according to their places and means. For after this, they also offered to the house of the Lord, v. 6.

*{with all my might}* This has reference both to the willingness and cheerfulness of his spirit, and also to the uttermost of his ability, in regard of that portion of treasure which God had bestowed upon him.

*{for the house of my God}* This shows the reason of his bountifulness, that special relation which was betwixt God and him. See ch. 28:10.

*{the gold for things to be made of gold, and the silver for the things of silver}* See ch. 28:15, etc.

*{and the brass for things of brass}* See 1 Kings 7:41, etc.

*{the iron for things of iron}* See ch. 22:3.

*{and wood for things of wood}* Mention is made of cedar trees in abundance, ch. 22:4. But questionless David prepared other kind of wood also which was needful, as wood of olive trees, and fir trees; for of these were sundry things made, 1 Kings 6:23, 34; 2 Chron. 3:5.

*{Onyx stones}* See Ex. 28:9. This stone uses to be reckoned up among such stones as are accounted most precious, Gen. 2:12; Ex. 35:9, 27; Job 28:16; Ezek. 18:13.

*{and stones to be set}* For ornament and glory; such stones as use to be set in rings.

*{glistering stones}* Heb. *stones of ornament*. These are such as are called *stones of fair colours*, Isa. 54:11. The clear and bright glittering of a precious stone adds much to the esteem thereof.

*{and of divers colours}* The word is applied to garments, Judg. 5:30, and to birds also, Ezek. 17:3. Here it implies most choice stones which glittered with variety of bright colors.

*{and all manner of precious stones}* There are twelve distinct kinds set down, Ex. 28:17, etc.; Lev. 21:19, 20; Ezek. 28:13, and it may be David provided store of all of them, and others too.

*{and marble stones in abundance}* Marble is a hard lasting stone, which being made smooth glitters also. Many of them are very massy, so that tables, pillars, monuments for the dead, use to be made of them. Some are white, some black, some speckled and have variety of colors, Ezek. 1:6. It may be the costly stones laid for a foundation, 1 Kings 5:17, were marble.

**V. 3.** *{Moreover}* This word implies an addition to that which was before noted; which is further cleared by these words over and above in the end of the verse.

*{because I have set my affection}* Heb. *in my affection*. Or good pleasure. This shows the cause of his bounty, the love which he bore to, and the delight which he had in the house of God.

*{to the house of my God}* See v. 2.

*{I have of mine own proper good}* Heb. *there is to me a treasure*. Besides the public treasure of the kingdom, and the treasure of the house of God, he had a treasury to himself proper to his own use.

*{of gold and silver which I have given to the house of my God}* Or, *out of which I have freely given towards the building of the house of God, and towards the making of such things as are to be for the service of God*.

*{over and above all that I have prepared}* David hereby means that which he had gotten by war, or which was otherwise laid up in a public treasury.

*{for the holy house}* Or, *the house of the sanctuary*. It was called sanctuary, because it was dedicated as holy to God.

**V. 4.** *{Even three thousand talents of gold}* That is, eleven millions, two hundred fifty thousand pounds sterling. Here he declares what he had given out of his own proper treasure: for out of it, and other treasures, and by other means he had prepared a far greater sum, ch. 22:14.

*{of the gold of Ophir}* See 1 Kings 9:28.

*{and seven thousand talents of refined silver}* That is, two millions, six hundred twenty-five thousand pounds sterling. See ch. 22:14. Of refined metal see ch. 28:18.

*{to overlay the walls of the houses withal}* This has reference both to the gold and silver. For the walls in the most holy place and the holy place were overlaid with beaten plates of gold, and other walls with plates of silver.

**V. 5.** *{The gold for things of gold, and the silver for things of silver}* See v. 2. He gave his own silver and gold to the same use to which he gave the silver and gold out of the public treasury.

*{and for all manner of work to be made by the hands of artificers}* These artificers are called cunning men, ch. 22:15. Of every manner of work see ch. 22:15.

*{And who then is willing}* This interrogative shows that he propounded his own example to incite his princes and people to be bountiful also, according to their places and means, towards the house of the Lord; and what they did, to do willingly and cheerfully. Herein he followed the example of Moses, Ex. 35:5.

*{to consecrate his service}* Heb. *to fill his hand*. Namely, with gifts for the house of the Lord. See 1 Kings 13:33.

*{this day}* Presently, without further delay.

*{unto the LORD}* To the honor and glory of God. For the building of the temple, and making the things appertaining thereunto made much to the honor of God.

**V. 6.** *{Then the chief of the fathers}* See ch. 27:1.

*{and princes of the tribes of Israel}* Such as are mentioned, ch. 27:22.

*{and the captains of thousands and of hundreds}* See ch. 28:1.

*{with the rulers over the king’s work}* Such as were overseers and had the disposing of such things as were to be done for the king.

*{offered willingly}* V. 7 is noted the quantity of what they offered; which was an evidence of their bounty. This phrase sets out the manner of what they did, which made their bounty the more acceptable: for *God loveth a cheerful giver*, 2 Cor. 9:7.

**V. 7.** *{And gave for the service of the house of God}* See ch. 28:13, 21.

*{of gold, five thousand talents}* Eighteen millions, seven hundred and fifty thousand pounds sterling. See ch. 22:14.

*{and ten thousand drams}* The Hebrew word *אדרכנים* *Adarchonim* is once more used, Ezra 8:27, and another like it, Ezra 2:69; Neh. 7:71, 72, namely *דרכמונים* *Darchemonim*. The Septuagint translate both words *δραχμάς*. A dram is the fourth part of a shekel, whereof see ch. 22:14, in value seven pence halfpenny, of silver; of gold, six shillings three pence. For a shekel contained twenty gerahs, Ex. 26:13, and one gerah was three halfpence in silver. So then half a shekel (whereat every one was rated, Ex. 30:13) was fifteen pence. This tribute for the service of the house of God Caesar took to himself. Hereupon this question was propounded to Christ, Matt. 22:17, *Is it lawful to give tribute unto Caesar, or no?* This tribute was a double dram, in Greek *δίδραχμον*, Matt. 17:24. The drams here mentioned were of gold, and amounted to three thousand one hundred twenty-five pounds sterling.

*{and of silver, ten thousand talents}* That is, three millions, seven hundred and fifty thousand pounds. The whole sum of gold and silver here offered by the princes amounts unto two and twenty millions, five hundred and three thousand and one hundred twenty-five pounds. Add to this David’s offering, v. 4, which is thirteen millions, eight hundred seventy-five thousand pounds, and the total will be thirty-six millions, three hundred seventy-eight thousand, one hundred twenty-five pounds sterling.

*{and of brass eighteen thousand talents, and one hundred thousand talents of iron}* Talents of brass and iron are to be accounted according to their weight rather than their worth. In weight a shekel made half an ounce; and three thousand shekels made a talent: after which account one talent contained a hundred twenty-five pound Troy weight, twelve ounces going to one pound. Thus the eighteen thousand talents of brass amounted unto two millions, two hundred fifty thousand pound, and the hundred thousand talents of iron, to twelve millions five hundred thousand pound Troy weight. This was counted a bountiful gift; but if it be compared with David’s ch. 22:14, we shall find that David gave more talents of gold and silver than all the princes and people gave in gold, silver, brass and iron.

**V. 8.** *{And they with whom precious stones were found}* That is, they who had precious stones lying by them, or laid up in their treasuries. Under this word precious stones all sorts of precious stones may be comprised; every one brought such as they had. This phrase is used of the Israelites contributing to the tabernacle: *Every one brought of such things as were found by him*, Ex. 35:23, etc.

*{gave them to the treasure of the house of the LORD}* See ch. 26:20, 26, etc.

*{by the hand of Jehiel the Gershonite}* See ch. 26:21, 22.

**V. 9.** *{Then the people rejoiced}* This shows that under those that are reckoned up v. 6 many of the people are comprised.

*{for that they offered willingly}* See v. 6. A cheerful performing of a good thing revives the spirits of them that do it.

*{because with perfect heart}* See 1 Kings 8:6. What they did, they did not for ostentation’s sake, nor to please David or any other man, nor in show of piety, but sincerely, to help on the building of God’s house.

*{they offered willingly to the LORD}* That which was given to the Lord’s house, was to honor God thereby, and in that respect it was given to the Lord.

*{and David the king also}* Here we see the king distinguished from his subjects, called the people in the beginning of the verse.

*{rejoiced with great joy}* See 1 Kings 1:40. David’s heart was inflamed with such a zeal of God’s glory, that not only the things which himself did, but also those which his people did to that end, exceedingly cheered his spirit.

**V. 10.** *{Wherefore David blessed the LORD}* He could not suppress his inward joy within himself, but must needs express it outwardly by yielding solemn praise to God.

*{before all the congregation}* See 1 Kings 8:22. David thought it not enough in his own heart, or privately, to praise the Lord; but because the occasion was public, he does it publicly, in a greater assembly.

*{and David said, Blessed be thou, LORD God of Israel our father}* See 1 Kings 8:15. In the beginning of the chapter David directed his speech to his subjects, to stir them up to help on the building of the temple. Here he turns to God, after he had seen their forwardness to do what he desired, and he gives the praise thereof to God.

*{for ever and ever}* See ch. 16:36.

**V. 11.** *{Thine, O LORD, is the greatness}* This form of praising God shows what is true praise, namely, to acknowledge that to be God’s which is his, Matt. 6:13. Greatness attributed unto God in the abstract shows that he is great and wonderful in everything that belongs unto him.

*{and the power}* All power is in God. As his power is an almighty power, so what power soever is in any creature, comes from him.

*{and the glory}* This word sets forth the excellency of everything that is in God.

*{and the victory}* As God vanquishes all his enemies, so he gives victory to those that overcome their enemies.

*{and the majesty}* This sets out God’s royal dignity.

*{for all that is in the heaven and in the earth}* All things that be are comprised under these two, heaven and earth. They are in the one, or in the other.

*{is thine}* Belongs unto you.

*{thine is the kingdom, O LORD}* This sets forth the sovereignty of God. To him all things are subject as to their king.

*{and thou art exalted}* This is to be taken of God’s manifesting his dignity: for he cannot be exalted or lifted higher than he is. It is also taken of men’s acknowledging his high dignity; in which respect men are oft exhorted to exalt God, Ps. 34:3 and 99:5, 9.

*{as head above all}* God is resembled to a head, as he is higher than all, as he quickens and preserves all, and as he governs all.

**V. 12.** *{Both riches and honour come of thee}* Heb. *from thy face*. These two particulars show that whatsoever a man has he receives from God. It is the blessing of God that *maketh rich*, Prov. 10:22, and it is God that setteth up whom he will, Ps. 75:6, 7.

*{and thou reignest over all}* In regard of God’s absolute sovereignty and power he reigns over all; by his special grace he reigns over his church.

*{and in thine hand is power and might}* It is God that gives strength and power to whom he will.

*{and in thine hand it is to make great}* To advance some above others.

*{and to give strength unto all}* To enable all to do what he sees meet.

**V. 13.** *{Now therefore, our God}* Here David shows the reason why he acknowledged what he had done concerning God.

*{we thank thee}* Heb. *we confess to thee*. Namely, all that has been declared to be most due to you. And this is to thank God.

*{and praise thy glorious name}* Heb. *the name of thy glory*. By God’s name is meant God himself, even his divine essence; and also whatsoever it is wherein and whereby he makes himself known to us: in all which there is an excellency and glory; and that David would set forth, and therein praise God.

**V. 14.** *{But who am I, and what is my people}* David having in the former verses ascribed all to God, that he might further show that all glory does wholly and entirely belong unto him, he here puts away all the glory of what was done, from himself, and from his people. So Ps. 115:1. The interrogative adds much emphasis; and shows that he and his people compared unto God were nothing at all. So 1 Cor. 3:5.

*{that we should be able}* Heb. *retain or obtain strength*. Hereby he shows that it was God that enabled them every way to do what they did.

*{to offer so willingly after this sort}* Not only ability to do good, but also the very will and mind to do after a right manner, is of God, Phil. 1:13.

*{for all things come of thee}* Both the goods we have to bestow, v. 12, and also the mind to bestow them.

*{and of thine}* Heb. *of thine hand*. Of that which you have given us.

*{have we given thee}* That which is given towards God’s worship and service, is given to God.

**V. 15.** *{For we are strangers before thee and sojourners}* Ps. 39:12; 1 Pet. 2:11. This has relation to the condition of the saints here on earth. All men are strangers and sojourners; but saints best discern it, and most freely acknowledge it. They are called strangers in relation to a country of their own in another place, namely, heaven: and they are sojourners, or pilgrims, in regard of their travel towards that country. Here, at least in their mind and disposition, they have no abiding place, Heb. 13:14. David was a king, and very well settled in his kingdom, when he uttered this, and his people lived quietly in their own possessions; yet, in regard of their unsettled abode in this world, and of that inheritance they expected in heaven, he thus speaks of himself and of his people.

*{as were all our fathers}* He alludes to that which Jacob professes, Gen. 47:9, and to the condition wherein Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob were while they sojourned in Canaan, and to the condition of the Israelites while they were in Egypt, and when they passed through the wilderness into Canaan. See Heb. 11:13.

*{our days on the earth are as a shadow}* This metaphor sets out the shortness, frailness, changeableness of man’s life. A shadow is quickly gone; it has no solidity in it; it alters with every motion. See Job 14:2; Ps. 102:11 and 109:23 and 144:4; Eccles. 6:12 and 8:13.

*{and there is none abiding}* Heb. *expectation*. Nothing on earth can be expected firm and sure. David thus sets forth his own and his people’s mortal condition, to show that they had good cause to give unto the Lord what they could here, because they had no better way to dispose of what they had. Seeing they were not here to continue on earth, and had the use of the goods of this world but as strangers, they could no better bestow them than on him who could give them an eternal inheritance.

**V. 16.** *{O LORD our God}* See ch. 13:2; 1 Kings 8:28.

*{all this store that we have prepared}* This has relation to v. 2, 3, 4, 8; ch. 22:14.

*{to build thee an house for thine holy name}* Of building an house for God’s name see 1 Kings 8:44, 48. Of God’s holy name see ch. 16:10, 35.

*{cometh of thine hand, and is all thine own}* See v. 14.

**V. 17.** *{I know also, my God}* That relation unto God which he had before extended to himself and to his people, v. 13, he does here in particular apply to himself.

*{that thou triest the heart}* Ch. 28:9; 1 Sam. 16:7. This is a singular comfort, and matter of strong confidence to those that are of an upright heart, that God can and does discern the integrity thereof. This moved David, when his integrity was questioned by men, to appeal unto God, Ps. 7:8, 9 and 26:1, 2; 2 Kings 10:3.

*{and hast pleasure in uprightness}* Ps. 51:6. God prefers an upright heart before all manner of outward services.

*{As for me}* A man may more securely and safely judge of his own sincerity than of another’s.

*{in the uprightness of mine heart I have willingly offered all these things}* An honest upright heart makes men do good cheerfully. Uprightness of heart and cheerfulness of spirit accompany one another.

*{and now have I seen with joy}* A good man that is forward to do good himself, much rejoices at the good which others do: but evil men envy thereat.

*{thy people which are present here}* Heb. *found*. For all that could be found, and were fit to be there, were present.

*{to offer willingly unto thee}* Though the willing doing of a thing consists especially in the inward disposition of the heart, yet by the manner of doing it may easily be discerned.

**V. 18.** *{O LORD God of Abraham, Isaac, and of Israel our fathers}* These were the three great patriarchs from whom the children of Israel descended, and to whom the promises that tended to the good of that people were made. To strengthen their faith in those promises this title is oft used. See 1 Kings 18:36; Ex. 3:6, 15, 16 and 4:5. Seven times at least in this short form of praising God are these titles, Lord, God, used. See ch. 17:27.

*{keep this for ever}* David well knew holy zeal might wax cold, and that men might fall from good beginnings, and leave their first love; and therefore he calls upon God to preserve and increase that good grace and holy zeal which he had observed in his people.

*{in the imagination of the thoughts of the heart of thy people}* Those are as it were the spring from whence all good or evil flows from man. The purpose and desire of a man is set forth under this phrase. What proceeds from a true purpose of heart, used to be sound and constant. Barnabas therefore exhorts them that were converted that they would *with purpose of heart cleave unto the Lord*, Acts 11:23. To like effect tends this prayer. See ch. 28:9.

*{and prepare}* Or, *stablish*.

*{their heart unto thee}* Where the heart is well prepared and established or set to do a thing, there is great hope of a thorough doing it, and of constancy therein.

**V. 19.** *{And give unto Solomon my son a perfect heart}* David did not content himself with giving directions and exhortations to his son, as ch. 28:8, 9, 10, but earnestly prayed to God for him. For well he knew that all the preparations he had made, and all the counsel he had given would do him no good, except the Lord added his assistance and blessing, Ps. 127:1, 2.

*{to keep thy commandments, thy testimonies, and thy statutes}* See 1 Kings 2:3.

*{and to do all these things}* The former phrase of keeping may imply a holy inward disposition; this latter of doing, an outward answerable conversation. Or all these things may be referred to such injunctions as he gave him in charge; and then it implies that his heart first must be seasoned with piety to God before he could do such things as his father enjoined him.

*{and to build the palace}* See v. 1.

*{for the which I have made provision}* David made provision by purchasing a place where the temple was to be built, ch. 21:25, by providing materials, ch. 22:14 and 29:2, etc., and by prescribing a pattern, ch. 28:11.

**V. 20.** *{And David said to all the congregation}* See v. 10.

*{Now bless the LORD your God}* He contents not himself that he had blessed God, as v. 10, but stirs up also his people to do the same, Ps. 103:1, 2, 20, etc. This argues that his spirit was filled with a zeal of God’s glory. Of your God see ch. 22:18.

*{And all the congregation blessed the LORD}* They quickly took the exhortation. For their hearts also were filled with a zeal of God’s glory.

*{God of their fathers}* The fathers mentioned v. 18. See 2 Chron. 11:16.

*{and bowed down their heads}* This was a rite of religious worship when it was done to God, Gen. 24:26; Ex. 4:31. See on Gen. 48:12.

*{and worshipped the LORD}* With divine honor and respect.

*{and the king}* With civil reverence. Some apply this to Solomon, whom David appointed to be king: but it may be as well applied to David himself. The same word is oft attributed to God and man, but in a different respect, as Ex. 14:31; 2 Chron. 31:8.

**V. 21.** *{And they sacrificed sacrifices unto the LORD, and offered burnt offerings unto the LORD}* See 1 Kings 3:15.

*{on the morrow after that day}* Questionless upon the sight of each other’s liberality and alacrity they agreed together to make the next day a solemn day of rejoicing: and such was their zeal as they would put it off no longer than till the morrow.

*{even a thousand bullocks, a thousand rams, and a thousand lambs}* All these were clean creatures, and appointed for offerings under the law, Num. 7:15. Of the multitude of sacrifices see 1 Kings 8:63.

*{with their drink offerings}* See 1 Kings 3:15.

*{and sacrifices in abundance for all Israel}* Under this clause other sacrifices are comprised than those before mentioned. And these they brought in greater abundance, that there might be sufficient for all of all sorts there present to eat, and that in a liberal manner.

**V. 22.** *{And did eat and drink}* At a great feast, as 1 Kings 8:65.

*{before the LORD}* Before the ark. See ch. 17:16; 1 Kings 8:62 and 9:25.

*{on that day with great gladness}* See v. 9. Nothing works greater joy in a man’s heart than duties of piety zealously performed.

*{and they made Solomon the son of David king the second time}* This questionless has relation to 1 Kings 1:38, 39. For then was Solomon solemnly proclaimed king. In reference to that this is styled the second time. That first making of Solomon king was on a sudden, by reason of Adonijah’s conspiracy, and to suppress that faction. Neither can it be thought that the Israelites assembled from all parts unto that solemnization, which was so hastily done: only all that were then in Jerusalem and heard of what was done gathered themselves together, and gave their acclamations, 1 Kings 1:39, 40. But at this solemnity they were assembled from all the tribes of Israel, ch. 28:1. Whether this were in David’s days is questionable. They that think so gather their conjecture from the words immediately going before in this and the former verses, and also from the mention of David’s death after this act, v. 26. But because this solemnity of Solomon’s being made king was many ways like the other, especially in respect of sacrifices and feasting, it was not unfit to join them together. They which hold this inauguration was after David’s death, produce these arguments; that Zadok was made priest, that Solomon sat on the throne instead of David, and that all the sons of David submitted themselves to Solomon.

*{and anointed him}* This anointing was for further ratification of the former, 1 Kings 1:39.

*{unto the LORD}* He was by virtue of this anointing after a solemn and sacred manner devoted and bound to the service of the Lord.

*{to be the chief governor}* Under God, over his people.

*{and Zadok to be priest}* The inference of this after the word anointed implies that Zadok also was solemnly anointed to be priest, as Aaron, Ex. 29:7. And this was the rather here done because there was a change of the priesthood to another family, 1 Kings 2:35.

**V. 23.** *{Then Solomon sat on the throne of the LORD}* A royal throne is in general called the throne of the Lord, because all kingdoms are the Lord’s, Ps. 22:28. He is the King of kings, 1 Tim. 6:15. All kings bear his image, and govern under him. Besides, God in special manner chose Solomon to sit upon this throne, ch. 28:5. And the people who were governed by him were in special manner the Lord’s people, Ex. 19:5, 6. Yea, the law by which he was to govern was expressly given by the Lord, Deut. 17:18. And (which is the chief) this throne was an especial type of the throne of the Lord Christ, Luke 1:32.

*{as king, instead of David his father}* We do not read that he did this before the death of his father. See 1 Kings 1:20, 35 and 3:7.

*{and prospered}* This ratified the prayers of David, ch. 22:11.

*{and all Israel obeyed him}* See ch. 18:14 and 28:21. This is set down as an evidence of their acknowledging Solomon to be their sovereign. David had not this honor in the beginning of his reign, 2 Sam. 2:4, 10.

**V. 24.** *{And all the princes}* See ch. 28:1.

*{and the mighty men}* That is, all that had any command over soldiers, and all that were eminent for honor, wealth, etc. Or that were in any great office. Sundry heads of these are mentioned ch. 28:1.

*{and all the sons likewise of king David}* Of these see ch. 3:1, etc. David had so clearly made known the mind of the Lord concerning Solomon’s succeeding him on the throne, as that after the death of Adonijah, all his sons, even those who were born of more royal mothers, and were also elder in years, yielded to the will of God therein.

*{submitted themselves unto Solomon the king}* Heb. *gave the hand under Solomon the king*. This Hebraism implies that they engaged themselves by some external rite, as giving the hand, to yield fealty and subjection unto him. See Gen. 24:2; Job 17:3 and on Eccles. 8:2.

**V. 25.** *{And the LORD magnified Solomon exceedingly}* This has relation to that wisdom, wealth, honor, peace, and other excellencies that God bestowed upon Solomon according to his promise, 1 Kings 3:12, 13, whereby his fame spread abroad into all nations, 1 Kings 10:23, 24. Many particulars hereabout are recorded 1 Kings 4:2, etc.

*{in the sight of all Israel}* So as all the people well discerned that respect which the Lord had showed him, and that blessing which he had bestowed on them in establishing him king over them.

*{and bestowed upon him such royal majesty}* Here is meant the extent of his dominion, 1 Kings 4:21, his glorious palace and other buildings, 1 Kings 7:1, etc., his magnificent throne, 1 Kings 10:18, etc., and those other things which the Queen of Sheba so admired, 1 Kings 10:4, 5, together with his wisdom, and other extraordinary endowments, 1 Kings 4:29, etc.

*{as had not been on any king before him in Israel}* Neither on Saul, nor on David; no, nor on any after him, 1 Kings 3:13; 2 Chron. 1:12. Herein the desire of the people in David’s time was accomplished, 1 Kings 1:37, 47.

**V. 26.** *{Thus David the son of Jesse}* This phrase is sometimes used in contempt; sometimes for honor’s sake, ch. 12:18. See 1 Kings 12:16. Here it is used in the best sense.

*{reigned over all Israel}* Namely, the greatest part of his reign. See 2 Sam. 2:10, 11.

**V. 27.** *{And the time that he reigned, etc.}* See 1 Kings 2:11.

**V. 28.** *{And he died in a good old age}* These two words good and old are used in relation to the many years that he lived, even seventy, 2 Sam. 5:4, and to the integrity which he retained all his days, even in his old age.

*{full of days}* For he lived till his strength was wasted, 1 Kings 1:1, and withal he was satisfied in mind with that time God had afforded him, and was now willing to die. See ch. 23:1 and Gen. 25:8.

*{riches and honour}* That which he contributed toward God’s house, ch. 22:14, gives evidence of his riches; and the glorious victories he got over all the nations round about him, and the great respect his subjects bore him give evidence of his honor.

*{and Solomon his son reigned in his stead}* See v. 23.

**V. 29.** *{Now the acts of David the king, first and last}* His acts from his youth, before he was king, in the beginning of his reign, and all along to the end of his reign are comprised under these words first and last.

*{behold, they are written in the book}* Or, *history*. For he means a book of chronicles.

*{of Samuel the seer}* See ch. 9:22 and 26:28.

*{and in the book}* Heb. *words*. The books of the Chronicles are in the Hebrew titled *words of days*. See the title of this book.

*{of Nathan the prophet}* See ch. 17:1.

*{and in the book of Gad the seer}* See ch. 21:9. These three, Samuel, Nathan, Gad, as is supposed, one after another, wrote the two books which carry the title of Samuel. And who more fit to write that history than these holy men, who were *αὐτόπται* eyewitnesses of many things, and whose help and counsel was made use of in matters of greatest moment? So the same Nathan, Ahijah, Iddo and Shemaiah are thought to have written the beginning of the first book of Kings. See 2 Chron. 9:29 and 12:15 and 13:22.

**V. 30.** *{With all his reign}* That is, all the passages of his reign, whether prosperous or adverse. For David exceedingly prospered in many things, and many troubles also befell him.

*{and his might}* Both those valiant acts that he did himself, and also those great victories that were obtained under him.

*{and the times that went over him and over Israel}* The various occurrences that fell out either to himself or that people, whether good or evil, in his days.

*{and over all the kingdoms of the countries}* This has especial relation to those kingdoms which David subdued, and those also, which were in league with him, yea, and all the kingdoms with which he had anything to do.

## EDITORIAL NOTE
